# THE CONCEPT BENCH — rev 77.  ALL 75, IN FULL.

**THIS FILE EXISTS BECAUSE THE LAST ONE DID NOT (F335).** `DESIGN_PROGRAM_rev76.md` opened
*"CARRIER. Rule 16 applies: this file is the only home of the 78-concept bench"* and then said the
full concepts were in `subagents/workflows/wf_71e8a5d6-b52/journal.jsonl`. **That path is a
per-session agent scratch directory. It was never committed, the container it lived in has been
rebuilt, and it exists nowhere on this disk — `find /root/.claude -name journal.jsonl` returns only
THIS revision's run.** Rev 76's concept BODIES are gone; what survives of it is the four shortlisted
concepts and the next eight in `DESIGN_PROGRAM_rev76.md` §3, its 12 critic findings, and 66 bare
titles in its §4.

**SO THE STANDING RULE, APPLIED HERE: a concept round's output is committed IN FULL, IN THE
REPOSITORY, IN THE SAME COMMIT THAT ANNOUNCES IT. If it is not in `git ls-files`, it does not exist.**

---

## HOW THIS ROUND WAS RUN

**25 creative directors at `effort: xhigh`, 3 concepts each, 75 concepts, run under the rev-76
program's own §9 expansion brief.** Every director was given: the recovered spine, the machine's real
limits, the measured figure sheet, the owner's voice dossier, the cultural field, **the completeness
critic's 12 findings verbatim**, and the full list of the 78 concepts that already existed, with an
instruction that duplicating one would be screened out.

**THE KNOBS §9.2 ASKED FOR, AND WHETHER THEY WERE TURNED:**

| §9.2 asked for | what ran |
|---|---|
| `xhigh` on DREAM and AMPLIFY only | **DONE.** Grounding and screening ran at session effort. |
| at least a third of directors with no territory | **8 of 25 = 32 %.** Plus 4 bench promotions, which are seeded but not caged. |
| a 4th audit lens: *"has the improvement removed what made this dangerous?"* | **BUILT AND RUN** — see `DESIGN_PROGRAM_rev77.md` for how far it got. |
| two completeness critics, run BEFORE the synthesis as well as after | **BOTH EARLY CRITICS RAN AND ARE CARRIED BELOW IN FULL.** |
| feed §2 in as a prompt input | **DONE** — all 12 findings, verbatim, to every director. |

**THE HEADLINE NUMBER, AND IT IS THE CRITIC'S FINDING 4 ANSWERED:**

```
    CONCEPTS CONTAINING FOOD OR PEOPLE AS SUBJECT
      rev 76      0 of 11
      rev 77     55 of 75
```
*(the flag is each director's own declaration on the concept, not a later reading of it)*

**REGISTERS OCCUPIED: 25 directorial slots, 13 of them the registers the critic named as ABSENT.**
Every one of the 13 returned three concepts. None is still empty.

⚠ **CEILING ON EVERY SCORE BELOW.** `screen` is the mean of THREE independent screening lenses —
owner-fit, craft-and-beauty, freshness-and-range — each scoring all 75. It is a RANKING DEVICE, not a
measurement of anything, and the lenses were told to use the full range. **The audit, amplification
and synthesis stages hit a session limit part-way; what completed is recorded in
`DESIGN_PROGRAM_rev77.md` and NOT smoothed over here.**

---

## THE TWO COMPLETENESS CRITICS, RUN BEFORE THE SYNTHESIS, VERBATIM

### CRITIC 1 — verdict

> BLUNT VERSION. You have fifty concepts and about eight objects, and you have not checked whether you are allowed to sell any of them. Fix that first: every commercially significant item here is a Volkswagen T1 silhouette or roundel, and in 77 revisions of a repository that measures a tail board to 0.1 mm there is not one line about trade dress. Only [dream:beautiful-3] spots it. The pivot is already on your own bench — TRES HUECOS — because the three serving apertures are Tacombi's cut, not Volkswagen's pressing, and they are the strongest measured fact you own. Move the mark off the vehicle and half your legal problem and all of your originality problem resolve at once.
> 
> BEST IDEA, BY NAME: [dream:beautiful-1] DIEZ FLORES Y MEDIA. It is the only thing here that is beautiful before it is clever, it monetises the one asset nobody at the counter can see, it dresses every table and sells as metre goods off one file, hule is the right cultural register and it is cheap, and flowers are not somebody else's trademark. Pay the painter or commission a taller — that cost is not negotiable and it is small. Runners-up that I would actually buy: [dream:free-7-2] the salsa bottle (the only repeat-revenue object in the pool; check the refill legality with an inspector on day one), [dream:free-5-1] the picture book (the only children's object anyone keeps for twenty years, and it delivers F18 inside its own back board), [dream:beautiful-2] the PROVECHO tin tray, [dream:la-combi-cerrada-2] the kraft bag.
> 
> WORST IDEA, BY NAME: [dream:packaging-2] DOS MANOS — the napkin printed with a non-consenting customer's face for the next customer to wipe grease on. The concept says it cannot tell whether that is moving or tasteless. It is tasteless, it is a likeness violation, and it is food-contact. Kill it. Most likely to become a news story: [dream:frontier-inside-neck-1] EL CARNET, a stamped border-document pastiche issued by a US restaurant group in 2026 — it names its own problem and then proposes to proceed with a caveat that will be cropped out of the first screenshot. Also kill [dream:twelve-shadows-1] (a chalk outline in a dining room) and [dream:photographic-3] (a forged photograph of a real trading business's vehicle).
> 
> PAY FOR AND KEEP: the cloth, the salsa, the book, the tray, the bag, the lotería deck already on your shortlist. ART SCHOOL: [dream:la-combi-cerrada-1], [dream:sound-2], [dream:frontier-inside-neck-3], [dream:photographic-1], [dream:people-1], [dream:free-8-3], [dream:motion-2], [dream:environmental-2], [dream:sound-1], [dream:garment-1], [dream:garment-2], [dream:garment-3]. Their shared defect is that the idea lives in a label you can cut off — [dream:garment-1] diagnoses itself perfectly and ships anyway.
> 
> WHAT A TAQUERIA NEEDS THAT IS NOT IN THIS POOL: a gift card and its carrier; a t-shirt, a cap and a tote; THE MENU; a shelf-ready retail food pack with a barcode; and the delivery seal plus the bag someone opens at home having never seen the room — your largest unbranded surface. You skipped the entire top of the revenue funnel and went straight to struck bronze medals and vitreous enamel tooling, and not one concept states a cost, a price, an MOQ or a lead time.
> 
> DERIVATIVE, NAMED: Thomas Ruff's jpegs ([dream:photographic-1]), Fontcuberta ([dream:photographic-3]), Smejkal's Fatescapes ([dream:people-1], [dream:free-8-3]), Dorothy's blueprint prints (the whole austere register, including THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE), Panini ([dream:free-6-1], [dream:packaging-3]), Margiela ([dream:garment-2]), Altiverb ([dream:sound-1]), the gift-shop VW lamp ([dream:beautiful-3]), the branded burger bun ([dream:free-1-1]), Muji-plus-Everlane for the confession-on-the-object house move. The two concepts that name their own lineage — [dream:free-6-3] and [dream:beautiful-1] — are the two strongest in the pool. That is not a coincidence.
> 
> AND THE THING THAT WOULD WORRY ME MOST IF THIS WERE MY BRAND: forty of these objects apologise on their own face, in 6 pt, to a person holding a taco. One object confessing is a great idea. Forty is a company that does not believe in itself. Pick one, strip the rest, and put the measurement back where it belongs — under the object, making it right, and silent.

**[TOP] THE ENTIRE MERCHANDISE PROGRAMME IS BUILT ON A THIRD PARTY'S TRADE DRESS AND NOBODY HAS CHECKED. Almost every saleable object in the pool is a Volkswagen T1 silhouette, a VW roundel, or both — and this project has spent nine owner rejections and roughly eighteen revisions reverse-engineering the roundel's shape without once asking whether it may be printed on a product for sale. Exactly ONE concept of ~50 names it, and names it correctly: [dream:beautiful-3] GUIRNALDA, 'a recognisable T1 silhouette on a manufactured lamp is precisely what Volkswagen's licensing people look for... THE SHAPE IS THE MARK'. [dream:free-3-1], [dream:polychrome-1], [dream:child-2], [dream:free-4-1], [dream:f18-sticker-1], [dream:free-7-1], [dream:free-6-1], [dream:dimensional-3], [dream:twelve-shadows-2], [dream:free-1-1], [dream:sound-3] all sell or give away the mark and none of them mentions it. The medal ([dream:dimensional-3]), the sundial ([dream:twelve-shadows-2]) and the branding iron ([dream:free-1-1]) are the worst exposure because they are three-dimensional metal objects cut to the profile — that is trade dress, not fair-use depiction.**

* evidence: grep -rniE "trademark|trade dress|licen[cs]|infring" over every .md/.txt in the repo returns nothing about the right to sell VW-derived goods; the only trademark hit is F168, which is about `vw_canonical_2019.svg` being a DIFFERENT OBJECT from the 1955-67 pressing for fitting purposes. 77 revisions, zero IP analysis.
* remedy: One page of IP advice BEFORE any tooling spend, and it is cheaper than one enamel die. Then pick a lane: (a) licence it, (b) restrict VW-shaped goods to objects used in the owner's own rooms and never sold, or (c) MOVE THE MARK OFF THE VEHICLE. Option (c) is already sitting on the bench as TRES HUECOS: the three equal serving apertures are Tacombi's own cut, not Volkswagen's pressing, they are the strongest measured fact in the record, and they are legally clean. That is the pivot this pool should have made and did not.

**[TOP] THIS IS NOT FIFTY CONCEPTS. IT IS ABOUT EIGHT OBJECTS, REPEATED. Thirteen concepts are the 515.5 x 403 rounded rectangle in a different material ([dream:photographic-2], [dream:environmental-1], [dream:free-4-2], [dream:free-6-2], [dream:free-8-2], [dream:free-3-3], [dream:garment-3], [dream:packaging-2], [dream:food-handoff-1], [dream:people-2], [dream:polychrome-2], [dream:packaging-1], [dream:f18-sticker-2]). Twelve are a die-cut sticker of the bus. Three are a children's height chart ([dream:people-3], [dream:free-1-3], [dream:child-3]). Two are a pinata ([dream:dimensional-2], [dream:free-1-2]). Three are a tray. And the TITLES COLLIDE: MI COMBI appears three times ([dream:polychrome-1], [dream:child-2], [dream:free-4-1]), ARMA TU COMBI twice ([dream:packaging-3], [dream:free-6-1]), LADO E twice ([dream:garment-1], [dream:free-2-2]), AQUI TIENE twice, LA CHAROLA three times, YA ALCANZAS twice. A client presented with this cannot tell which one to buy, and neither can you.**

* evidence: Counted directly off the pool's own titles and specifications as supplied.
* remedy: De-duplicate before the next review. One owner per object: ONE sticker, ONE aperture artefact, ONE child's keepsake, ONE tray. Then the real question becomes answerable — which sticker — instead of being buried under eleven near-identical answers.

**[TOP] NOT ONE CONCEPT IN THE POOL HAS A PRICE, A COST, A MINIMUM ORDER OR A LEAD TIME. I am the person with the budget and I cannot buy anything here. Two retail prices appear in ~50 concepts ($14/$6 salsa in [dream:free-7-2]; $58-68/$32 napkin in [dream:free-7-3]) and no landed cost anywhere. Worse, the pool systematically reaches for the six most expensive processes in merchandise: vitreous enamel tooling ([dream:polychrome-2] wait — [dream:dimensional-1], [dream:free-5-3], [dream:people-3], [dream:free-8-2]), steel-rule dies, cut-and-sew pattern grading ([dream:garment-1], [dream:garment-2], [dream:frontier-inside-neck-2]), lithographic tinplate ([dream:beautiful-2]), screen-printed glass ([dream:free-7-2]), CNC brass and struck bronze ([dream:dimensional-3], [dream:twelve-shadows-2], [dream:free-1-1]). Almost nothing sits in the $4-$25 impulse band where counter merchandise actually sells, and several items ([dream:dimensional-3] medal, [dream:free-7-3] $58 napkin) are explicitly acknowledged as unable to pay rent.**

* evidence: grep -rniE "MOQ|cost of goods|unit cost|wholesale|price point|retail price|sell-through|barcode" across the repo: zero hits. The pool's own text: '$58 NAPKIN SELLS TO WELL UNDER ONE PER CENT OF TICKETS. It does not pay rent.'
* remedy: Every concept carries five lines before it is looked at again: landed COGS, RRP, MOQ, lead time, and an honest sell-through guess. Kill anything above $60 retail that is not the salsa or the book. Then note the shape of what survives — it will be paper, vinyl, cotton and glass, which is what a taqueria actually sells.

**[TOP] THE CONFESSION HAS BECOME A TIC AND IT IS NOW A BRAND LIABILITY. Roughly forty of these objects print their own uncertainty on their own face: PROFUNDIDAD NO MEDIDA, ACABADO: NO MEDIDO, CAMERA UNSOLVED — CUT HAND-TRACED, PROFUNDIDAD EXAGERADA, SHEET 3 OF 4 — NOT ISSUED, 812.0 IS PROVISIONAL, a permanently blank half-metre of woven tape ([dream:frontier-inside-neck-3]), a growth chart that tells a family the company does not know how tall its own bus is ([dream:people-3]). ONE object doing this is a superb, unrepeatable brand idea. Forty objects doing it is a company that apologises on every surface it owns, in 6 pt, to a customer holding lunch. Nobody buys a disclaimer. And it is not even original — 'we print the process on the product' is Muji c.2003 plus Everlane's transparent pricing c.2013.**

* evidence: The pool's own texts, e.g. [dream:free-5-3]: 'that line is LOAD-BEARING... If it cannot be read, kill the concept, because the mug alone is merch.' That sentence concedes the object is nothing without its apology.
* remedy: Choose exactly ONE confession object — I would make it THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE from the existing shortlist, because there the confession IS the subject — and strip the caption from everything else. On the other 49 the measurement should be invisible and load-bearing, not printed.

**[HIGH] THE SINGLE WORST IDEA IN THE POOL, BY NAME: [dream:packaging-2] DOS MANOS — specifically LA SERVILLETA, a napkin printed with the halftoned face of an identifiable, non-consenting customer, handed to the NEXT customer to wipe grease off their hands with. It is a likeness violation, it is food-contact, and it is an insult to the person depicted, delivered at scale, daily. The concept itself says 'I genuinely cannot tell which' — the most moving object or the most tasteless. It is not a close call. The rest of DOS MANOS is also weak: a 321 mm tray that will not stack loaded on a taco counter, and a third print unit specified for 19.9 mm of gold ink, which the concept correctly predicts an accountant will kill.**

* evidence: Concept text: 'printing a customer's face on a napkin the next customer wipes their hands on is either the most moving object in this whole programme or the most tasteless.'
* remedy: Kill it. If the counter/brass-line idea is worth keeping, it survives on the tray alone with no faces and no gold unit.

**[HIGH] THE SINGLE BEST IDEA IN THE POOL, BY NAME: [dream:beautiful-1] DIEZ FLORES Y MEDIA — the hule tablecloth of the roof mural. It is the only concept that is beautiful before it is clever; it monetises the one asset in this entire archive that nobody standing at the counter can see; it is fit-out AND metre-goods AND napkins off one file; hule is cheap, wipe-clean and exactly the right cultural register for a fonda; every plate of food in every room lands on the brand's own artwork, which is free photography forever; and — crucially given finding 1 — flowers are not Volkswagen's trade dress. Second best is [dream:free-7-2] the salsa bottle, which is the only genuine repeat-revenue object in the pool. Third is [dream:free-5-1] the picture book, which is the only children's object anyone keeps for twenty years.**

* evidence: Concept's own model dependency is honest and narrow (the homography that un-tilts a 76-degree board; +0.47 % aspect check) and its risk register names the only real blocker: authorship of the original painting.
* remedy: Fund it, and fund the remedy its own risk section already prescribes — find and credit the original painter, or commission a named taller to repaint the seven screens from the rectification. That cost is not optional and it is small next to enamel tooling. Ship cloth, napkin and a metre-good at retail.

**[HIGH] THERE IS NO FOOD IN THIS FOOD PROGRAMME. Two dozen concepts are flagged food-and-hand-off: true and then deliver a DRAWN taco of unknown form. The pool admits it repeatedly: 'nobody in this project has ever drawn food' ([dream:people-2]); 'no taco exists as a mesh and no photograph we hold shows the food at all' ([dream:motion-1]); 'the plancha is a bare 0.560 x 0.780 slab that has never had anything on it' ([dream:beautiful-2]). The one thing this business sells is the only subject the programme cannot render, photograph or draw. A taqueria's design programme that does not make anyone hungry is not a taqueria's design programme — it is a vehicle-restoration fanzine with excellent typography.**

* evidence: Pool text as quoted; and the archive holds nine exterior frames of a bus and zero food photography.
* remedy: Commission a food shoot before another artefact is drawn. Keep the shooting discipline [dream:photographic-2] already wrote — 'shot during service, not after; the hand in frame is the hand that made it; nothing placed for camera; nothing wiped' — and throw away the 900x700 plywood jig that concept wraps it in. The rule is the valuable part; the prop is a $1,200 excuse to delay.

**[HIGH] THE OBVIOUS COMMERCIAL OBJECTS A TAQUERIA NEEDS, ALL ABSENT FROM ~50 CONCEPTS, IN VALUE ORDER. (a) THE GIFT CARD and its carrier — the highest-margin object in hospitality, bought and kept by definition, breakage revenue, Q4-critical; not one concept. (b) T-SHIRT, CAP, TOTE — the three things that actually leave a counter in a customer's hand. The pool has two aprons, a chore coat and a two-tone overshirt, i.e. it went straight to cut-and-sew and skipped the printed blank that pays for it. (c) THE MENU ITSELF — the single most-read piece of design the business owns, and nobody designed it; LA CARTA is a bench title and nothing more. (d) RETAIL GROCERY PACK — if they sell packaged tortillas or masa at retail, a shelf-ready pack with a barcode is worth more than everything in this pool combined, and there is no shelf-facing packaging anywhere here. (e) THE DELIVERY SURFACE — the tamper seal and the bag opened at home by someone who has never seen the room is the largest unbranded surface the business owns in 2026, and it appears nowhere except accidentally in [dream:packaging-3]. (f) SHOPFRONT / window hours vinyl / blade sign. (g) enamel pin, magnet, keyring at $6-12, pure margin, kept forever.**

* evidence: grep across the whole repo for gift card / t-shirt / tote / enamel pin / barcode / delivery bag / storefront / wayfinding: zero hits outside a single generic 'signage/wayfinding' territory label in DESIGN_PROGRAM_rev76.md.
* remedy: Build (a), (b) and (e) first, because they pay for the rest. [dream:packaging-3] ARMA TU COMBI is the nearest live thing to (e) and should be re-briefed as the delivery system — seal, bag, sauce cup, carrier — with the collect-the-set mechanic as a bonus rather than the point.

**[HIGH] CONSENT IS A PROGRAMME-WIDE PROBLEM, NOT A PER-CONCEPT RISK NOTE. At least nine concepts print, sell or install identifiable non-consenting people photographed between roughly 2006 and 2013, including a minor: [dream:people-1] (die-cut cards, 'the child is a minor'), [dream:photographic-1] (a metre-tall print), [dream:packaging-2] (the napkin), [dream:free-2-1] (a signed edition of 100), [dream:free-8-3] RUIDO (sold broadsides), [dream:free-1-3] (a child's measured height ENGRAVED ON A PUBLIC WALL), [dream:free-4-2] (a real employee's hands on a commercial fixture), [dream:free-6-3] (a lithographed calendar), [dream:beautiful-2]. Each one writes an honest paragraph and then proceeds. Nine honest paragraphs is not a policy; it is nine separate letters from a lawyer.**

* evidence: Pool text, e.g. [dream:people-1]: 'Seven real people photographed between roughly 2006 and 2013 who never agreed to be printed.'
* remedy: One blanket rule, written once: NO human from the reference archive appears on any object that is sold, given away or permanently installed. If people are wanted — and they should be, see finding 7 — shoot new, with releases, and pay the staff. That is a two-day cost and it removes nine risks at once.

**[HIGH] THE THREE THAT WOULD ACTIVELY EMBARRASS THE BRAND, IN ORDER. (1) [dream:frontier-inside-neck-1] EL CARNET DE LA COMBI — a US-invested restaurant group issuing a stamped border-carnet pastiche about a Mexican vehicle, in 2026. Its own risk section describes the problem accurately and then offers to proceed with a caveat. There is no version of this that does not end up screenshotted without its caveat. (2) [dream:twelve-shadows-1] MEDIODIA VERDADERO — a 1:1 dark bus-shaped stain permanently inlaid into a working dining-room floor. Its own text calls it a chalk outline at a crime scene. It is a funeral installed three metres from the vehicle, which is still trading. (3) [dream:photographic-3] LA DECIMA — a deliberately forged photograph of a real, identifiable, currently-operating business's vehicle, made in the same format as the genuine record. Within a year it is cited as evidence. Honourable mention: [dream:free-1-1] EL FIERRO — branding food with a livestock iron, in a Mexican-American context, is a connotation the concept itself names and cannot defuse.**

* evidence: Each concept's own risk register, quoted above.
* remedy: Kill 1, 2 and 3 outright rather than sanding them. A concept whose stated mitigation is 'he should decide whether he wants it hanging in them' is a concept that has handed the client the reputational risk instead of solving it.

**[MEDIUM] WHERE THE POOL IS DERIVATIVE, WITH THE EXISTING THING NAMED. [dream:photographic-1] UN PIXEL, UN MILIMETRO is Thomas Ruff's 'jpegs' (2004-07), same move, bigger paper. [dream:photographic-3] LA DECIMA is Joan Fontcuberta (Sputnik, Fauna) — the fabricated archive presented in the archive's own format, done thirty years ago and better. [dream:people-1] and [dream:free-8-3] are Pavel Maria Smejkal's 'Fatescapes' inverted. The whole austere measured-poster register — including THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE on the existing shortlist — is Dorothy's blueprint prints, which are a commodity product in every museum shop. [dream:free-6-1] and [dream:packaging-3] are the Panini sticker album. [dream:garment-2] 1.02 DEG (deliberate error plus DO NOT CORRECT in the tech pack) is Margiela, twice over. [dream:motion-2] GRADO E is every model-viewer brand demo plus ScanLAB's confidence-scan look. [dream:sound-1] is an Altiverb impulse-response library with better copy. [dream:free-1-1] EL FIERRO is the branded burger bun. [dream:beautiful-3] GUIRNALDA is the VW-bus lamp sold in every gift shop. [dream:free-6-3] and [dream:beautiful-1] name their own precedents (Galas/Helguera, mercado hule) and are the better for it.**

* evidence: Direct comparison against known published work; the two concepts that DO name their lineage are the two least embarrassed by it.
* remedy: Every concept names its nearest existing object in one line, the way [dream:free-6-3] does. A precedent named is a position; a precedent unnamed is a discovery waiting to be made by a journalist.

**[MEDIUM] THE ART-SCHOOL SHELF, TO BE CULLED IN ONE PASS: [dream:la-combi-cerrada-1] NO CIERRA (a nine-second loop whose climax is a stack trace), [dream:sound-2] LADO B (a half-blank record at half price), [dream:frontier-inside-neck-3] LA CINTA 8130 (half of every metre deliberately empty), [dream:photographic-1], [dream:people-1], [dream:free-8-3], [dream:motion-2] GRADO E, [dream:environmental-2] 3.95:1 (a lighting scheme with nothing to sell and nothing to show), [dream:sound-1], [dream:garment-1] LADO E, [dream:garment-2] 1.02 DEG, [dream:garment-3] MANDIL 812. They share one failure mode, and the pool states it itself: the meaning lives in a woven label, a colophon or a 6 pt caption that can be cut out, and the moment it is, the object is a coat with odd pockets or a shirt with a crooked seam. An object whose idea is detachable is not a designed object.**

* evidence: [dream:garment-1]: 'the meaning lives entirely in a woven tape that can be cut out, after which this is a coat with an odd pocket layout.' The concept diagnoses itself correctly.
* remedy: Test every survivor with one question: if the caption is removed, is this still worth owning? [dream:beautiful-1], [dream:free-7-2], [dream:beautiful-2] and [dream:free-5-1] pass. Almost nothing else does.

**[MEDIUM] THERE IS NO IDENTITY SYSTEM UNDERNEATH ANY OF THIS. Fifty artefacts and no wordmark treatment, no type palette, no colour standards sheet, no photography direction, no packaging architecture, no rule for when the austere register applies and when the polychrome one does. Every concept invents its own ink count and re-derives the palette from scratch, and two concepts explicitly notice the collision — [dream:free-6-3]: 'Two house styles need a written rule about which is which, and nobody has written one.' The measured colour set is genuinely the strongest commercial asset in this archive and it is currently scattered across fifty blurbs as loose RGB triples.**

* evidence: Pool text as quoted; and the repo contains a 45-datablock material system with no corresponding brand colour document.
* remedy: Twenty pages of brand standards before object number two: two named registers (LA PLACA, austere, two inks; LA FONDA, polychrome, six) with a written rule for which surface gets which, the measured palette as Pantone/CMYK builds with proofing tolerances, and one type family. This is a week's work and it is what turns fifty artefacts into a programme.

**[MEDIUM] EIGHT-PLUS CONCEPTS COMMIT A WALL, A FLOOR, A WINDOW OR A CEILING IN ROOMS NOBODY ON THIS PROJECT HAS STOOD IN OR MEASURED, AND NONE HAS A LANDLORD'S CONSENT. [dream:environmental-1] UN HUECO cuts a service hatch into a kitchen wall in every site; [dream:twelve-shadows-1] inlays a floor; [dream:environmental-3] SIGUE LA TIRA paints twelve metres of wall; [dream:beautiful-3] hangs sixty pendants at a drop chosen against an UNMEASURED ceiling height; [dream:people-3], [dream:free-1-3] and [dream:child-3] each fix permanent graphics beside a bus. [dream:environmental-1] also flags, correctly, that a 1190-1245 mm service counter is well above the commonly cited US accessible-service-counter height and may not pass inspection at all. This programme has surveyed a 1963 vehicle to the millimetre and has not measured a single room it proposes to install in.**

* evidence: [dream:beautiful-3]: '340 mm of drop over a table is a real spatial decision in rooms nobody on this project has stood in, and the ceiling height in either site is UNMEASURED.'
* remedy: Survey Nolita and one other site — floor, ceiling, window line, counter heights, accessibility compliance — before another environmental concept is written. It is a half-day with a laser measure and it unblocks eight ideas or kills them honestly.

**[MEDIUM] THE CHILDREN'S BRIEF — 'spark joy... for children', the OLDEST live item in the register — IS ANSWERED WITH TWELVE STICKERS, THREE HEIGHT CHARTS, A PASSPORT, A WHISTLE, TWO PINATAS, A COLOURING MAT AND A TABLET GAME, AND THE ONE THING A CHILD ACTUALLY KEEPS IS BURIED. [dream:free-5-1] EL DIA QUE LE CORTARON EL TECHO — the 32-page picture book with the sticker bound into the back board — is bought, kept, gifted, read aloud and re-read for twenty years, carries the origin story no other object can carry, and delivers F18 inside itself. It is buried at position 40-something in a pool that opens with a lottery deck and a poster about illegibility. Meanwhile [dream:frontier-inside-neck-1] proposes handing children a border document and [dream:twelve-shadows-2] proposes selling them a sundial that is wrong in New York.**

* evidence: Pool text: the book's own risk register is the most self-aware in the set, including the roundel-at-8 mm test it demands before plates are made.
* remedy: Children's line = the book + ONE sticker (the die-cut in the book's back board IS that sticker) + the placemat. Kill the other ten. And run the book's own prescribed test — the roundel at 8 mm — before anything is printed, because a tenth rejection printed two thousand times is the worst outcome available to this project.

**[MEDIUM] IS ANY OF IT BEAUTIFUL? MOSTLY NO — AND THE PATTERN IS DIAGNOSTIC. Six objects in fifty are beautiful before they are clever: [dream:beautiful-1] the oilcloth, [dream:free-6-3] the Galas-register calendar, [dream:free-7-3] the embroidered servilleta, [dream:beautiful-2] the PROVECHO tin tray, [dream:beautiful-3] the lampshade, [dream:la-combi-cerrada-2] the kraft bag. Five of those six sit in the 'beautiful-not-rigorous' and 'free' pools — i.e. this programme only becomes beautiful at the exact moment it stops measuring, and it treats that as a confession rather than as the finding it is. Everything else is clever: it is a joke, a proof, a confession or a diagram. Clever sells once. Beautiful sells for ten years and gets photographed by strangers.**

* evidence: Six of fifty by direct reading; five of the six carry an explicit 'beautiful-not-rigorous' or 'free' tag in the pool's own territory labels.
* remedy: Re-weight the programme 60/40 toward warmth. The measurement is the SUBSTRATE — it is why the drawing is right, why the cloth is not stretched, why the tray is the true rectangle — and it should almost never be the SUBJECT. One object may be about the measuring. The rest should simply be correct, and beautiful, and quiet about it.

### CRITIC 2 — verdict

> The pool answers findings 1, 5, 6, 11 and 12, partly answers 2, 3, 4, 7 and 9(a)-(c), and leaves 8, 9(d) and 10 essentially untouched. Its two biggest failures are not aesthetic. FIRST, IT IS BUILT ON THE WRONG TREE: HEAD is rev 77, not rev 76 — `line_pass.py`, `la_rueda.py`, `sheet3_notissued.py` and F330–F336 are committed — so a third of the pool argues with a machine that no longer exists (hand-drawn-3's grep, food-handoff-3, free-1-2, free-6-1) while free-3-1 asserts an AO pass that still does not exist, and free-1-2 proposes to "override" an F18 trigger the owner fired at rev 77. SECOND, IT REPEATS THE PROJECT'S OLDEST DEFECT ON THE ONE SUBJECT WHERE IT MATTERS MOST: the people in seven concepts — the magenta puffer, the back to camera, the gaze into the lens, the headcounts of six and seven — are in no file in this repository, and those unpainted windows are carrying the consent arguments. Three more figures are wrong or frame-confused against live source: 1223.7 mm is a function of a pose angle bracketed 68.9–90° (1187–1273 mm), the counter at "1254 mm above ground" is the authored un-dropped constant the source itself warns about, and LA CINTA's ±2108/−1873 stations do not close against a 4065 mm body. On range: the nine absent registers are covered by label, but 515.5 × 403.0 r55 is now the new hairline rule across thirteen concepts (two of which contradict each other about whether the tray fits through its own hole), the pool duplicates itself eight ways because F335 left the bench unreadable, and "SUBTRACT eleven times out of eleven" has become CONFESS about thirty times out of seventy-five — a universal risk-absorber printed in 4 pt that changes no design decision, and which two concepts admit is itself the design. Food or people is the SUBJECT of 21 of 75, not the 55 that claim it; the strongest of those 21 are the ones that admit the model contributes almost nothing, which is the exact trade the redirect existed to avoid. Fix the tree, paint the human windows, build the galley camera and the AO pass, and re-run — the four concepts that are both model-load-bearing and about the hand-off are all blocked on the same afternoon of work.

**[TOP] (f) THE PEOPLE IN SEVEN CONCEPTS ARE NOT IN THE RECORD, AND THE FABRICATION IS LOAD-BEARING ON EVERY CONSENT ARGUMENT. 'Magenta puffer coat', 'back to camera', 'looking directly into the lens', 'the eater in the red shirt is face-on and centre-frame', 'a woman with her chin on her hand', and the headcounts 'seven real people' (people-1, packaging-2) / 'six people' (free-2-1) exist nowhere in this repository. There is no person census anywhere.**

* evidence: What the record actually holds: LEDGER_rev47.md:263 and PHOTOS_WANTED_rev47.md:76 / rev49.md:119 -- ref_nolita_front34.jpg, 'a child's head occludes the lower quarter'; OPEN_FINDINGS.md:433 (F220) -- three traced points sit on 'the child standing at the bumper', pixel values (205,165,157),(204,172,161),(193,166,149), i.e. HAIR, no garment, no orientation; AUDIT_rev12.md:868 -- in ref_side.jpg 'the man covers 33 % of the front-wheel window and his red shirt defeats a red-livery mask'; HANDOFF_rev12.md:172 -- '~37 % of that variance is the man working INSIDE'. `grep -rin 'puffer|magenta' *.md` returns only stars and a mask colour. free-2-1 builds its entire consent mitigation on 'the child is drawn from behind, which is how she actually appears in ref_nolita_front34.jpg (magenta puffer, back to camera)'. free-1-3 proposes to ENGRAVE 'a child, magenta puffer coat' at a measured height on a public wall. photographic-1 decides which of nine sheets can ship on the gaze of a man whose gaze is not recorded.
* remedy: This is rule 8 exactly: a number (and here an ethical decision) published from a window nobody painted. Crop and paint every human region in ref_side.jpg, ref_nolita_front34.jpg and ref_rear34.jpg, publish the crops, count the figures, and record orientation and framing as an OBSERVATION with n. Until then strike every clause in people-1, photographic-1, packaging-2, free-1-3, free-2-1, free-6-3 and food-handoff-1 that describes a person, and strike the mitigations built on them.

**[TOP] (f) 1223.7 mm IS A POSE, NOT A MEASUREMENT, AND FOUR CONCEPTS PRINT IT AS TRUE SIZE. The mural board's width is a function of the roof lid's OPENING ANGLE, which is an environment-overridable pose constant. The derivation is also misattributed: 1.7130 is the PROJECTED aspect, not the board's.**

* evidence: t1_shell.py: `LID_X0, LID_X1 = 0.9640, -1.0700` (difference 2.0340 m = the '2034'); `LID_ASPECT = float(os.environ.get("T1_LIDASPECT", 1.7130))`; `LID_W = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / math.sin(math.radians(LID_OPEN_DEG))`; `LID_OPEN_DEG = float(os.environ.get("T1_LIDDEG", 76.0))`, with the file's own comment at line 1303 bracketing it 'the roof's own width, 68.9-90 deg'. At 76 deg W = 1.2238 m; at 68.9 deg W = 1.2729; at 90 deg W = 1.1874. That is -36/+49 mm on a figure beautiful-1 prints as 'the true 2034 x 1224 mm rectangle', free-4-3 halves into a 1017 x 612 mm printed field, f18-sticker-3 prints as '1223.7 DERIVED from a measured board aspect of 1.7130 ... +/-30 mm' and free-7-3 charts. And the arithmetic does not close: 2034/1.7130 = 1187.4, not 1223.7. The source says why -- line 1321, `W * sin(a) = (LID_X0 - LID_X1) / 1.713` -- so the TRUE board aspect is 1.7130*sin(76) = 1.6621 and 1.7130 is what the camera saw. lid_gen.py's independent BOARD_ASPECT = 1.654, 68 % CI [1.591, 1.703], EXCLUDES 1.713.
* remedy: Quote the board as 2034 mm long by 1187-1273 mm wide, angle-conditioned, or state LID_OPEN_DEG in the same sentence. beautiful-1's '+0.47 % against lidmural.png' check is itself a function of that pose and must say so. Never again print the projected aspect and the true aspect as one number (rule 38).

**[TOP] (f) TWO CONCEPTS PUBLISH THE COUNTER HEIGHT IN THE WRONG FRAME, AND THE SOURCE ITSELF WARNS ABOUT THIS EXACT ERROR. beautiful-2: 'a counter whose top is 1254 mm above ground'. free-8-3: 'the slab top at 1.2540 m above ground' -- which is that concept's ONE claimed model contribution.**

* evidence: t1_detail.py: `CNT_ZT, CNT_ZB = 1.2540, 1.1470  # 107 mm thick` is AUTHORED UN-DROPPED; build.py subtracts the ride drop per vertex last. t1_detail.py:1524 records the confusion verbatim: 'a scalar put CNT_ZT 1.189 + 0.065 = 1.254, and the built slab then reads 1.240'. With drop(x) = 0.047925 + 0.017750x the slab top above ground runs 1190 mm at the counter's forward end to 1245 mm at the tail -- which child-3 ('1190 at the front, 1245 at the tail') and environmental-1 ('1189.8-1244.9 mm') both get right. beautiful-2's number is 9-64 mm high, on the single most important dimension in a hand-off, in the concept whose entire claim is that the model gives the illustrator the right eye height.
* remedy: Every above-ground height in this pool must be station-stated. Add one line to any concept quoting a z: the constant, the station, and the drop applied. food-handoff-1 already flags this as its risk 4 and is the only concept that does; it should be the template.

**[TOP] (e/f) THE POOL IS SPLIT ACROSS TWO EPOCHS OF THE RECORD AND ABOUT A THIRD OF IT IS ARGUING ABOUT A MACHINE THAT NO LONGER EXISTS. HEAD is rev 77, not rev 76. The line pass SHIPPED, F18's trigger FIRED, and the audience was RULED -- and concepts on both sides of that line are in the same pool contradicting each other.**

* evidence: `git log --oneline`: 6d112e5 'SHIP SESSION B -- THE LINE PASS EXISTS, AND CARD ZERO IS DRAWN THROUGH IT', b7cc1b7 'F332/F333 -- the line pass, and the four defects found in it by looking', 8cb0530 'F330/F331 -- TWO OWNER RULINGS, AND F18's TRIGGER IS FIRED'. `line_pass.py`, `la_rueda.py`, `sheet3_notissued.py`, `LEDGER_rev77.md` are all tracked. So: hand-drawn-3's 'I GREPPED EVERY .py IN THIS TREE: THERE IS NO FREESTYLE, NO GREASE PENCIL, NO AO PASS, NO CRYPTOMATTE' is FALSE; food-handoff-3's 'the line pass ... does not exist yet', free-1-2's 'building them is the keystone item', free-6-1's 'this is its first consumer' are FALSE. free-1-2's 'the sticker's deferral trigger is unmet and I am proposing to override it' is refuted by F330 (OPEN_FINDINGS.md:558): he ruled 'YES -- START IT NOW'. In the other direction free-3-1's '"cartoon with rendered depth" ... only the mesh has an AO pass' is FALSE -- F332's own closing clause: 'the SECOND half of the owner's sentence ... has no normal or AO pass yet.' And F331 (OPEN_FINDINGS.md:559) rules 'Every future shortlist must say which line each item is in' -- only 9 of 75 concepts do (child-1/2/3, free-8-1/2/3, f18-sticker-1/2/3).
* remedy: Re-base the whole pool on HEAD before screening. Every concept must state (a) its F331 line, (b) whether it consumes the line pass that exists or the AO pass that does not. Any concept whose model-dependency paragraph asserts an absence must have the grep re-run at HEAD, not quoted from the rev-76 program.

**[TOP] (d) THE APERTURE RECTANGLE IS THE NEW HAIRLINE RULE. 515.5 x 403.0 r55 is the entire content of at least thirteen concepts at thirteen scales. The critic asked for range and got one rectangle re-substrated -- and two of the thirteen make opposite decisions about it without knowing the other exists.**

* evidence: photographic-2 (1:1 jig), environmental-1 (1:1 hatch), free-4-2 (1:1 backlit case), f18-sticker-2 (1:1 decal plus 1:5 sticker), free-6-2 (1:1 minus 10 mm tray), free-8-2 (1:1 tray), garment-3 (1:1 chest panel), free-3-3 (1:1 chest panel), packaging-1 (1:2 box footprint 257.75 x 201.50 r 27.50), people-2 ('The frame IS the hole'), polychrome-2 (sheet cut to 515.5 x 403.0 r 55), sound-2 (sleeve die at 1:3), frontier-inside-neck-1 (the view through it). THE CONTRADICTION: free-6-2 shrinks the tray to 495.5 x 383.0 r45 'because a tray the size of the hole cannot go through the hole'; free-8-2 keeps it at nominal and concedes 'at nominal aperture size the tray cannot actually pass through the aperture ... I would keep it and print the dimension on the rim'. Same pool, same object, opposite rulings. Two aprons (garment-3 MANDIL 812, free-3-3 MANDIL 515) are the same move on the same rectangle. Two concepts share the title AQUI TIENE (free-4-2, f18-sticker-2) and are the same object.
* remedy: Cap the aperture family at two: one 1:1 fabricated object and one garment. Screen the rest out or fold them together. And settle the 10 mm question once -- it is a fabrication fact, not a matter of taste, and having it both ways in one document is exactly the defect the project's own rule 13 exists for.

**[TOP] (d) SUBTRACT WAS REPLACED BY CONFESS, AND IT IS THE SAME TIC. The last round's finding was 'SUBTRACT has been applied eleven times out of eleven, which is not a strategy, it is a tic.' This round resolves its central risk on roughly thirty concepts by printing an admission on the object in 4-6 pt type. It is a universal risk-absorber that costs nothing and changes no design decision.**

* evidence: 'PROFUNDIDAD NO MEDIDA' (polychrome-2), 'ACABADO: NO MEDIDO' (free-5-3), 'PROFUNDIDAD EXAGERADA / CONSTANTE DECLARADA' (dimensional-3), 'CAMERA UNSOLVED -- CUT HAND-TRACED' (people-1), 'PROF. AUTORIZADO / PROFUNDIDAD SIN MEDIR' (dimensional-1), 'SHEET 3 OF 4 -- NOT ISSUED' (frontier-inside-neck-1), 'MOTOR: NINGUNO' (free-3-2), 'UNMEASURED' end cards (motion-1, people-2, twelve-shadows-3), the blank tape half (frontier-inside-neck-3), the blank record side (sound-2), the 40 % tint (free-2-2). Two concepts admit the caption IS the design: free-6-2 -- 'the caption is load-bearing, not decoration ... if a production run ever drops that caption the object becomes dishonest'; free-5-3 -- 'that line is LOAD-BEARING ... If it cannot be read, kill the concept, because the mug alone is merch.' free-8-2 keeps a tray that does not fit through its own hole and prints the dimension instead of resizing it.
* remedy: Require of every concept: name one design decision the confession CHANGED. If the object is identical with and without the caption, the caption is not honesty, it is decoration about honesty. At most three concepts in the whole programme should carry a printed admission; on the rest, let the measurement change the object or leave it out.

**[HIGH] (f) LA CINTA 8130's ONLY CLAIMED MODEL DEPENDENCY IS A PAIR OF STATIONS THE RECORD DOES NOT HOLD, AND ONE OF THEM LOOKS LIKE A RETIRED ARTEFACT CONSTANT WITH ITS SIGN FLIPPED.**

* evidence: frontier-inside-neck-3 prints 'X_NOSE +2108, X_TAIL -1873' and stakes the whole concept on them: 'a length of tape laid against the actual bus registers with it'. t1_core.py carries `X_TAIL_OLD = -2.108  # the artefact value, kept only for _aft()` -- a number the source explicitly retired. STATE.md's bodywork x range is [-2.157, +2.160] and the body length is 4.065 m. 2108 + 1873 = 3981 mm, which is neither 4065 (the body) nor 4317 (with lids). So the tape's declared repeat (8130 = 2 x 4065, correct) and the stations printed along it are on different rulers and disagree by 84 mm over one repeat.
* remedy: Read the nose and tail stations off the mesh, print them, and re-derive the repeat from the same pair. If they cannot be made to close, the concept has no model dependency left and should be screened out on that basis rather than on taste.

**[MEDIUM] (f) THE PHOTOGRAPHIC SUITE MIXES TWO VEHICLES AND CALLS IT ONE RECORD -- rule 11, the rule that killed F99/F100/F140. It also assumes nine camera solves where the record holds one, and its own arithmetic does not close.**

* evidence: photographic-1 bills nine sheets as 'one per distinct photograph of this vehicle' and 'the entire photographic record of this bus'; photographic-3 opens 'Nine photographs of this bus exist'. Two of the nine -- ref_workshop.jpg and IMG_2073.jpeg -- are the GREEN vehicle, which HANDOFF_CARRIERS.md §0.1 lists under 'NOT the target, geometry only' and which rule 11 bars for paint and artwork. photographic-1 then proposes to print a camera-matched RED photoreal render inside a GREEN vehicle's silhouette at 300 dpi. Its size list gives EIGHT dimensions for NINE sheets. It does not state the duplicate rule: I verified by size that IMG_2053 = ref_nolita_front34b (550x412), IMG_2054 = ref_nolita_flank (450x450), IMG_2060 = ref_nolita_front34 (700x467), IMG_3842 = ref_playa_34 (500x400), IMG_3840 = ref_nolita_doorshut (480x320) -- the record's five byte-identical pairs, and a duplicate is not corroboration. On cameras, people-1 is right and photographic-1 is wrong: probe_rev39_flank.py:167 `sil = ok & (warp.max(2) < 250)` is a solved warp into ref_side.jpg's pixel frame and it is the ONLY one; people-1 says so and prints 'CAMERA UNSOLVED -- CUT HAND-TRACED' on the other three, photographic-1 assumes nine.

**[MEDIUM] (f) SMALL FIGURES NOBODY WATCHED PRINT -- THREE VALUES FOR THE FRINGE, TWO FOR THE SILL, AND THE ROUND'S OWN BRIEF IS WRONG ABOUT THE LAMPS.**

* evidence: FRINGE: environmental-1 says 64 balls, food-handoff-1 '~65', free-4-2 and polychrome-2 '65'. `bobble_fringe()` derives the count by `poly_offset(out, -FRINGE_INSET)` then `_resample_closed(out, FRINGE_PITCH)` with FRINGE_R, FRINGE_PITCH, FRINGE_INSET = 0.0090, 0.0260, 0.0130 -- so it is a derived quantity and nobody has printed it. LAMPS: this round's own brief says '115 festoon lamps'; the concepts say 118 and THE CONCEPTS ARE RIGHT. `BULB_X0, BULB_X1, BULB_PITCH, BULB_R = T._aft(-1.8000), 1.7000, 0.0286, 0.0110`; `_aft(-1.8) = X_AXLE_R - ((-1.8-X_AXLE_R)/-O_OLD)*O_NEW = -1.1 - 0.6944*0.773 = -1.6368`; n = round(3.3368/0.0286) = 117 and the loop is `for i in range(n + 1)` = 118. polychrome-1's '118 at 28.52 mm realised pitch' is exact. Do not 'correct' it to 115. But note the source carries two disagreeing provenance comments for one constant: t1_detail.py:3571 'a MEASURED 28.8 +- 2.0 mm', t1_shell.py:2455 'measured BULB_PITCH (28.6 +- 1.0'. SILL: free-4-2, f18-sticker-2, free-8-2, environmental-1 print 1307 mm; child-3 prints 1312 mm. Both are right at different stations -- 1307 is Z_SILL 1.3720 less a flat 65 mm (t1_shell.py:62), 1312 is station-corrected at bay 0 (x = +0.672) -- and no concept says which. child-3's 'the sills' 22.6 mm climb' checks out exactly (0.01775 x 1.270).
* remedy: Print the fringe count before any concept quotes it. Correct the round's brief from 115 to 118. Make every sill and counter figure carry its station.

## THE 75, BY DIRECTORIAL SLOT

## FOOD AND THE HAND-OFF

### CINCO ANTOJITOS  ·  screen 8.27/10

**REGISTER** polychrome — and hand-drawn (hand-painted), packaging-as-a-system, three-dimensional, food-and-hand-off  
**ARTEFACT** Five pieces of vitreous-enamelled steel service ware, each carrying one of the five food vignettes ALREADY PAINTED ON THIS VEHICLE, enlarged roughly 10× by a rotulista's hand and fired. A 400 × 260 mm tray (the two bolillos), a 300 mm charger (the torta), a 240 mm plate (the tostada), a 180 mm bowl (the prawn / ceviche), a 90 mm tumbler (the juice glass). Five colours plus the black rim line enamelware always carries. Every piece bears on its base, in 4 pt: the vignette's position on the board as a fraction of board height, the four measured colours, and the sentence THE STRIP INK (72,46,6) IS CONSTRUCTED; THE MEASURED MEDIAN IS (91,59,7) AND IS BLUR-LIMITED.

> The bus's own five hand-painted food vignettes, blown up ten times by another hand, fired onto the plates you eat off.

This vehicle already carries its own food illustration and this programme has never once drawn it. Five painted vignettes run down the menu strips: two bolillos with green sprigs, a torta with pink meat and green garnish, a tall pale glass with an orange, a tostada with magenta filling, a pink prawn. Their positions are read as fractions of the board's height — 0.047–0.096, 0.260–0.337, 0.453–0.511, 0.677–0.703, 0.814–0.868 — and their colours are measured off the rectified board, not invented: meat and shrimp (206,118,124), garnish (96,128,54), plate and glass (232,224,198), on a strip yellow of (172,144,17) at hue 48.0°.

Enamel them. Vitreous enamel on steel is the actual material of Mexican street service, and it is the only substrate in this programme that gets HANDED TO YOU WITH FOOD ON IT.

The enlargement is the whole risk and the whole idea. The source is forty pixels tall and blurred; scaling it in software would be a lie wearing the artwork bar's clothes. So a hand does it. Commission a rotulista, give him the rectified crop and the four colours, and let him repaint each vignette at 400 mm in industrial enamel on tin — Francis Alÿs's move in The Liar, the Copy of the Liar, where sign painters made the enlargements and authorship collapsed on purpose. This vehicle's artwork was put there by a hand. The only honest way to make it bigger is another hand.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — ALMOST NOTHING, AND IT SHOULD SAY SO. The vignette positions and the palette come from `lid_gen.py`'s rectified board, which is a photograph audit, not the mesh. The model contributes exactly ONE thing and it is the thing that stops the whole set stretching: the board's aspect is only known to ±0.09 (three methods give 1.520 / 1.654 / 1.82), which is why every shape in `lid_gen.py` is placed in board-normalised (u,v) with SEPARATE u and v radii. Transfer the vignettes in that same normalised frame or they will distort by up to 20 % and nobody will be able to say which way. That discipline is the model's gift here. The rest is the photograph and the painter, and pretending otherwise would be inventing a dependency.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) IT RE-DRAWS THE OWNER'S OWN ARTWORK, which SPEC §10.10 protects as a hard bar in his own words — 'ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ALL ARTWORK'. Three rev-76 concepts were flagged for doing this silently. This one makes the rule-34 defence out loud: the bar's OBJECT is the MODEL, and an enamel plate is a different object. He may still refuse, and refusing is a legitimate ruling, not a failure of the concept. (2) THE SOURCE IS FORTY PIXELS AND BLURRED. What comes back from the painter is HIS reading of a blur, not the original sign painter's drawing. There is no way round it and NO WAY TO MEASURE THE ERROR — 'it cannot be recovered from what we hold' applies to the vignettes and is stated on the base of every piece. (3) IT IS THE LOUDEST THING THIS PROGRAMME HAS MADE. Five saturated colours against a two-ink house style; next to the Uncertainty Plate it will look like it came from a different company. It did not. It came off the vehicle. (4) CULTURAL: rotulismo is a living, declining trade. Commissioning a rotulista is the answer; imitating one is appropriation, and 'Mexican-look' lettering drawn by us is the failure mode. Credit and pay him by name on the base. (5) ENAMEL TOOLING IS REAL MONEY AND THE FIRST FIRED PIECE CANNOT BE REVISED. (6) The strip lettering colour (72,46,6) that this programme has already quoted twice as a poster pairing is CONSTRUCTED, not measured. It is printed here with that admission, which slightly undercuts the object it decorates. Keep it anyway.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Vignette positions as fractions of board height, off `lid_gen.py`: bolillos 0.047–0.096, torta 0.260–0.337, juice glass 0.453–0.511, tostada 0.677–0.703, prawn 0.814–0.868. Left and right strips carry the IDENTICAL printed sequence. Strip widths as fractions of the board: left 0.0835, right 0.0794, top 0.0923. Colours, measured: STRIP (172,144,17) hue 48.0°, sat 0.88–0.93; PINK (206,118,124) meat/shrimp; GREEN (96,128,54) garnish; CREAMV (232,224,198) plate/glass. INK (72,46,6) is CONSTRUCTED — measured median (91,59,7), blur-limited. Mural board in-situ median (139,49,21), hue median 18.4°. Board aspect 1.654 with an honest bar of ±0.09 (three methods: 1.520 / 1.654 / 1.82) — hence normalised (u,v) placement. NOT USED AND FLAGGED: any per-item price. Prices exist only on the GREEN vehicle and the Nolita board; rule 11 bars artwork transfer between vehicles, so the green bus's board is evidence of the brand's FOOD, never artwork for the red bus.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — One rotulista, five 400 × 400 mm tin boards, ~5 days: £600–£1,200 depending on the painter. Photograph the boards flat, separate to five screens. Vitreous enamel on steel, short run: expect £4,000–£7,000 tooling and screen setup across the five shapes, then £8–£25 a piece at 500. IF ENAMEL IS TOO FAR: the identical five artworks screen-printed on 1.2 mm powder-coated steel trays — no kiln, roughly a tenth the setup, most of the look, and holdable in three weeks. Single-piece decal-fired prototypes from a small taller run ~£90 a plate, which is the sane way to see one before committing tooling.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — ONE BOARD. Give a rotulista the rectified crop of the PRAWN vignette (right strip, v 0.814–0.868), the four measured colours, and a 400 × 400 mm tin board, and ask for it back painted in enamel. One painter, one day, one object he can pick up. It also settles the programme's biggest open aesthetic question — whether an enlarged HAND beats a traced VECTOR — before a penny goes near tooling.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:food-handoff-2`

### SALE CALIENTE  ·  screen 6.67/10

**REGISTER** motion and sound — with food-and-hand-off, people; deliberately colliding hand-drawn line against photographic colour  
**ARTEFACT** A 12-second seamless loop. 24 fps, locked-off camera, no cuts. Delivered 1080 × 1350 and 1080 × 1920 H.264 for phone and in-store screen, plus a 3840 × 4800 ProRes 4444 master with the aperture matte on the alpha. 48 kHz stereo mix. RUNTIME 12 s. Colour count is the design, not a constraint: the drawn half is 2 flat colours plus line; the photographed half is uncontrolled full colour; nothing crosses the seam.

> Twelve seconds in which a drawn bus hands you a photographed taco, and the seam between the two is the brass line on the counter.

A still photograph cannot show a hand-off. It can only show the residue — the tray already landed, the hand already withdrawn. The transaction is a CROSSING, and a crossing takes time.

Twelve seconds, locked off, looping. The bus is a drawing: vector line, flat cream and flat red, occlusion sampled off the model — exactly the style he locked at rev 39. It never moves. Inside the aperture, and ONLY inside the 515.5 × 403.0 hole, everything is photographic: real hands, real steam, real grease, a real tray going out. The seam between the two worlds falls on the counter's front edge — 107 mm of slab with brass over the outer 18.6 % of it. The drawing stops at the brass. The food starts there.

Nothing else happens. A tray comes forward out of the drawn hole, crosses the brass, and lands in somebody's hands, which are also photographic. Sound: the plancha, the scrape of the spatula, paper, and one word called at seven seconds — ¡Sale!

The drawn half can also do what the render cannot. The drip rail carries 118 lamps at a realised 28.52 mm pitch, and today they render unlit pearl white; in both in-service photographs they are LIT and warm. A drawing simply draws them lit.

The collision is the point. Cold measured line on one side of a brass strip, hot uncontrolled colour on the other, and the boundary between them is the real boundary of the real business — the exact plane where the company stops and the customer starts.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — THIS ONE NEEDS THE MODEL MOST AND IT IS NOT READY, SO SAY SO. The drawn half is the Grease Pencil Line Art → SVG pass, which the rev-76 brief names as the FIRST JOB and which does not exist yet. What only the model can give: (a) a TRUE ORTHOGRAPHIC side elevation — the side ortho is the one camera that fits today — so the drawing does not swim against a locked-off live plate the way a perspective photograph of the bus would; (b) the exact 515.5 × 403.0 r 55 matte, in register with the drawn counter edge, generated rather than rotoscoped; (c) occlusion under the counter's underside and inside the aperture reveal, sampled from geometry, which is the 'rendered depth' half of the locked style. UNTIL THE LINE PASS LANDS the drawn half is a STAND-IN: the working side ortho, flat-shaded and posterised to two colours. That is a stand-in and is labelled as one on the file — it is not the deliverable. EMBLEM DECLARATION (F191): a flank framing composes the nose roundel out. Declared, not routed around. The emblem is still wrong at 0.8528 against a 0.9465 ceiling and this concept does not fix it.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) IT DEPENDS ON A PASS THIS PROJECT HAS NOT BUILT. If the line pass slips, this slips. Naming that is better than shipping a posterised render and calling it the style. (2) IT MIXES REGISTERS ON PURPOSE, AND MIXED REGISTERS ARE WHERE TASTE GOES TO DIE. A vector bus with a photographic mouth reads as a cheap collage the moment the matte is a pixel off. The only thing holding it honest is that the seam falls on a real edge of a real object. There is no safety margin here: it is either exact or it is a school project. (3) THE GREASE. Real steam and real grease WILL smear across the matte edge, and the correct answer is to LET THEM — food is allowed to break the drawing; the drawing is not allowed to break the food. To anyone who does not know that rule it will look like a mistake, and he may be one of them. (4) SOUND IS NEW TO THIS PROGRAMME ENTIRELY and a badly recorded sizzle is instantly cheap. Record it with a contact mic on the griddle or ship it silent — a phone mic in a busy room will make the whole thing feel like a competitor's. (5) THE GALLEY BEHIND THE HOLE IS NOT MEASURABLE IN Y. If the shot ever pulls back far enough to show galley depth, it is showing a construction. Keep the camera on the plane of the aperture. (6) It is a screen artefact for a client who has asked for LARGE-FORMAT PRINT (F192) and holds the delivery render (F193). It does not violate either hold — neither gates a line-and-flat-colour loop — but it is not the thing he last asked for, and that should be said to his face rather than hoped past.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55, three, EQUAL. Counter slab 107.0 mm thick, 321.0 mm plan depth, running x +0.918 to −2.188 (3106 mm), tail corner radius 150 mm (INFERRED). Brass nosing = 18.6 % of the slab's outer edge (measured 4.52 ± 0.23 px of a 24.84 px edge, corroborated at 0.191 on a second frame); brass linear albedo (0.6600, 0.4750, 0.1750) at roughness 0.255, tarnished. Galley strip light (1.000, 0.918, 0.790) at strength 3.04 — 'a serving hatch shows you its strip light'. Plancha 560 × 780 mm, top 1455 mm un-dropped. Body RED (196,49,36) hue 5.0° sat 0.816; CREAM (206,208,200), G>R. Festoon: 118 LAMPS at a realised 28.52 mm pitch — WATCHED PRINT this session from BULB_X0 −1.6368 → BULB_X1 +1.7000 at BULB_PITCH 0.0286. THE '115' IN CIRCULATION IS RETRACTED HERE AS THE BUILT COUNT: it is an INFERENCE about a 3.50 m rail from an FFT pitch of 28.6 ± 1.0 mm, and the competing peak-count estimator reads 19 ± 3 mm and is aliasing-limited. UNMEASURED and declared on the file: galley depth in Y; griddle temperature; the counter's height above ground (see CINCO SEGUNDOS, risk 4).

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The line pass is the keystone build item and is budgeted regardless. The drawn half is a SINGLE FRAME — it does not move — so render cost is one side ortho at delivery resolution (~5 min), not 288 frames. The live half is one afternoon at the pass: locked-off camera, a hard practical standing in for the strip light at (1.000, 0.918, 0.790), black card behind, so the roto is trivial — the aperture is a hard rectangle with a 55 mm radius and the camera never moves. Sound: contact mic on the griddle plus a shotgun at the pass, half a day. Realistically two weeks after the line pass lands. £0 in-house; ~£3,500 with an editor and a sound recordist.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — SHOOT THE LIVE HALF FIRST, THIS WEEK, WITH NOTHING RENDERED. Lock a phone on a tripod at the pass, hold the 1:1 cut aperture card from CINCO SEGUNDOS in front of the lens at the distance where the hole frames the hand-off, and record twelve seconds of one order going out, with sound. That single clip — a moving hand-off seen through a real 515.5 × 403.0 hole — IS the idea, and he can watch it on his phone before a single line is drawn. If it does not move him at that fidelity, it will not move him at 4K.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:food-handoff-3`

### CINCO SEGUNDOS  ·  screen 6.6/10

**REGISTER** photographic — and three-dimensional, people, environmental, food-and-hand-off  
**ARTEFACT** A framed photograph in which the MOUNT IS THE APERTURE. Window 515.5 × 403.0 mm, corner radius 55 mm, CNC-routed from the model's own aperture path in 2.4 mm cream conservation board; real white cotton ball-fringe (9 mm balls, 26 mm pitch, ~65 of them) glued 13 mm inside the cut edge exactly as `bobble_fringe()` builds it. Outer frame 700 × 560 mm, 40 mm cream-painted tulipwood, NO GLASS (glass would reflect the room and kill the illusion that you are looking through a hole). Behind it, one photograph on satin baryta, piezo pigment. Edition of three — one per serving bay. Ink count: photographic, plus one paint colour on the frame.

> The hand-off, photographed from the COOK'S side, framed in the actual hole.

Every food photograph ever made for a taqueria is taken from the customer's side: food centred, lit from behind, hero angle. This is the other one. The camera stands where the cook stands — 780 mm of griddle at his hip, the strip light over his shoulder at (1.000, 0.918, 0.790) — and looks OUT through a 515.5 × 403.0 mm hole at somebody waiting. Their hands come toward you. There is steam between you and them. That is the actual transaction of this business, and in seventy-six revisions nothing has been made from that side of it.

The mount is not a drawing of the aperture. It IS the aperture: 515.5 × 403.0, corner radius 55, routed from the model's own path, with real white cotton ball-fringe hung 13 mm inside the cut edge — 9 mm balls at 26 mm pitch, about 65 of them, exactly as the model builds it, and labelled INFERRED on the back because each ball is two pixels in the reference.

Three prints, one per bay, each hung at the sill height of the bay it was shot through. The three holes are identical to a tenth of a millimetre and no two of them are the same height off the road, because the bus leans nose-down at 17.75 mm per metre: 1312, 1323, 1335 mm at the three bay centres. Hang the row at those three heights and the frames lean the way the vehicle leans. Nobody will notice. It will still be true.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES A CUTTING LIST AND THREE HANGING HEIGHTS, AND NOTHING ELSE. Say that plainly rather than fake a dependency — the picture is a photograph. What only the model can give: the aperture cut path at 1:1 (515.5 × 403.0, r 55 — a photograph of the bus has perspective and cannot yield a true 1:1 path), the fringe's ball radius/pitch/inset as built, and the three station-corrected sill heights, which require the rake line and the bay centres together. It needs no line pass, no render, no new pipeline. EMBLEM DECLARATION (F191): the VW roundel is on the nose; an aperture crop composes it out. That is declared here, not silently routed around. F191's hold gates the PHOTOREAL DELIVERY RENDER; this is a photograph taken in a restaurant, so the hold does not gate it and this concept does not advance the emblem either.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — FIVE, none sanded. (1) It is a PHOTOGRAPH, in a programme that has spent seventy-six revisions building a 3D model. The model's total contribution is a cut path and three numbers. If that is not enough to justify it, the honest response is to kill it, not to bolt on a fake render dependency. (2) It puts real hands and a real plate in frame, so it can be shot BADLY in a way a vector drawing cannot. A bad taco photograph is worse than no taco photograph, and this brand's category is full of them. (3) IT ACTS ON THE EMOTIONAL BAR AND F92 SAYS 'do not act on it either'. The rule-34 object test, made explicitly rather than assumed: F92's object is the PLAYA HERO RENDER and `playa_env.py` as a delivery frame. A photograph taken at a working pass is a different object. F92's own sentence — 'the emotional bar that sits ABOVE clinical accuracy' — is what this concept is for, and it is quoted here rather than borrowed silently. He may still rule that the distinction does not hold. (4) THE COUNTER HEIGHT — the single most important dimension in a hand-off — IS CONTESTED INSIDE THIS RECORD. `CNT_ZT` is 1.2540 in the UN-DROPPED frame; the source's own above-ground figure (1.240 at the mean station) was computed with a rake coefficient since re-based from 33.0 to 17.75 mm/m; `REF_MEASUREMENTS` §6 reads 1.189 / 1.205 off the photograph. Print it as a disagreement or read it off the mesh first. Do not average them. (5) AUDIENCE: a sill at 1.31 m is above a small child's head. The Nolita frame shows exactly that — a child in a magenta puffer looking UP at the bus, and forty revisions have treated her hair as measurement contamination. Whether being below the sill is the point or the problem is UNRESOLVED, and it is a question for him, not a measurement.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm, corner radius 55 mm, THREE, EQUAL (t1_shell: BAY_W 0.5155, Z_SILL/Z_HEAD 1.3720/1.7750, BAY_R 0.0550). Bobble fringe ball radius 9.0 mm, pitch 26.0 mm, inset 13.0 mm, ~65 per aperture — INFERRED, each ball ~2 px. Sill heights above ground at the three bay centres (x +0.672 / +0.047 / −0.598): 1312 / 1323 / 1335 mm — DERIVED from t1_core.rake_drop() at t1_shell.BAYS centres and WATCHED PRINT this session; build.py's own scalar conversion prints 1.307 / 1.710 AG for the whole band, and the station-dependent figures are the finer answer. Rake 17.75 mm/m (1.02°), ride drop at x=0 47.9 mm. Plancha 560 × 780 mm, top 1455 mm un-dropped. Galley strip-light emission (1.000, 0.918, 0.790) at strength 3.04. Counter slab 107.0 mm thick, 321.0 mm plan depth, brass nosing 18.6 % of the slab's outer edge. Counter height ABOVE GROUND: CONTESTED, see risk (4). Galley DEPTH in Y: UNMEASURED — the source says so verbatim, so anything the lens sees past the aperture plane is a construction.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — CNC-routed mount ~£60. Ball fringe is haberdashery at ~£8/m; each aperture perimeter takes ~1.8 m. Frame ~£120, print ~£40. Three complete ≈ £700 plus a half-day shoot. No new pipeline, no render time, no new pass. The only long-lead item is a cook willing to have a camera at his shoulder during service — which is a permission problem, not a production one.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Print the aperture outline at 1:1 across four A3 sheets straight off the model's path, tape them, cut the hole with a knife, and take the card to the pass. Hold it up. Shoot twenty frames on a phone through it while an order goes out. One of them is the picture. In his hands this week, and it costs the paper. (The same cut card is also step one of SALE CALIENTE — the two concepts share their first physical object.)

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:food-handoff-1`
