## PEOPLE

### GRACIAS POR SU PREFERENCIA  ·  screen 7.33/10

**REGISTER** motion, hand-drawn line, sound, and the food-and-hand-off. Four registers the programme does not own, in one piece — and it is the owner's own locked style clause executed for the first time in seventy-six revisions.  
**ARTEFACT** A 16-second looping film, 2062 × 1612 px — the serving aperture at exactly 4 pixels per millimetre, 515.5 × 403.0 mm, corner radius 55 mm drawn as 220 px. The frame IS the hole. Delivered as a silent-safe MP4 and a ProRes master, plus a 24-page flipbook, 90 × 60 mm, one ink, as the physical object. Room sound only: the plancha, a bottle cap, coins on the slab. No music. No voice.

> A locked camera at the cook's eye, inside the middle bay, looking out — nothing in the world moves except hands, and six times in sixteen seconds a tray crosses the brass line and goes.

The brand's actual transaction is being handed hot food through a hole by a person standing there. The programme has drawn the hole eleven times and never once the hand.

One shot. You are inside the bus. The aperture's rounded rectangle is the film's frame and it never moves: 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55, three of them and equal, and this is the middle one. Across the bottom sixth runs the counter's brass nosing — 19.9 mm of gold in elevation, 18.6 % of a 107.0 mm slab edge, measured over 113 columns and corroborated on a second frame. That gold line is the only thing in the picture that is never touched, and every tray crosses it.

Everything the hands rest on is rendered from the model as vector line and flat colour with occlusion sampled off the mesh — the owner's own locked style, word for word, from rev 39. The hands, the paper, the food and the change are DRAWN BY A PERSON, on twos, in the vehicle's measured inks: RED #C43124, CREAM #CED0C8, GOLD #EFC245, strip yellow #AC9011, and the vignettes' PINK #CE767C and GREEN #608036 for what is on the paper. The seam between a machine-true aperture and a hand-drawn hand is visible in every single frame. That seam is the film.

Then the hands go, and the next pair comes up, and it loops.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Everything the hands touch. The aperture outline (515.5 × 403.0, r 55 — measured, and independently corroborated at ≈57 mm off the photograph), the counter's true section at the sill (107.0 mm slab, 321.0 mm plan depth, standing 291.0 mm proud of the 875 mm half-width flank), the 19.9 mm brass nosing at 18.6 % of the edge, the sill's height above ground, the bobble fringe round the cut edge, and — the part no illustrator can fake — the OCCLUSION PASS, so the shadow the cook's own body throws across the slab is sampled rather than invented. Without the model the aperture is a rounded rectangle somebody eyeballed. THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES NOTHING TO THE HANDS, and it must not: the camera height is the cook's eye, no human geometry exists anywhere in this tree, and no photograph gives a stature at a known depth. The camera height is a DECLARED POSE and the end card says UNMEASURED.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — SKIN. The moment you draw a hand you have chosen a colour for it, and this is a Mexican brand serving New York from a bus staffed by cooks in white toques. There is no measurement to hide behind, no ceiling to quote, and no honest way to abstain — a grey hand is a decision too, and a worse one. Get it wrong in public and the piece is the story. SECOND: nobody in this project has ever drawn food. Seven vignettes' worth of measured colour exists; the FORM of a taco does not, and a badly drawn taco kills sixteen seconds stone dead. THIRD: money. If a hand takes cash it reads as an advertisement; if no money crosses it reads as fantasy — pick one and live with it. FOURTH, DECLARED RATHER THAN ROUTED AROUND: the camera is inside the bus looking out, so the VW roundel is not in a single frame. This concept cannot advance the emblem, F191's hold is untouched by it, and it does not pretend otherwise. FIFTH: it will not match the rest of the programme. It will be warm, and the austerity will look colder next to it.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55, three of them, equal (t1_shell BAY_W 0.5155, Z_SILL/Z_HEAD 1.3720/1.7750, BAY_R 0.0550) — 2062 × 1612 px at the stated 4 px/mm, corner 220 px. Counter: CNT_ZT/CNT_ZB 1.2540/1.1470 = 107.0 mm thick; CNT_Y_IN/CNT_Y_OUT 0.8450/1.1660 = 321.0 mm deep, standing 291.0 mm proud of the 875 mm half-width; brass nosing CNT_NOSE_F 0.1860 × 107.0 = 19.9 mm in elevation (watched print). Sill at the middle bay 1323.2 mm above ground, DERIVED as z − rake_drop(x), rake_drop(x) = 0.047925 + 0.017750·x (watched print). Inks all measured off this vehicle except #482E06, which is CONSTRUCTED (the measured median is (91,59,7), blur-limited) and is labelled so on the end card. UNMEASURED and stated: the cook's eye height, the camera station, the form of the food, and the fact that no human geometry exists anywhere in this tree.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The rendered plate is one afternoon in Blender once the line pass exists — one locked camera, no animation, four passes. The hands are the cost: 16 s on twos is 192 drawings, realistically six hand-off cycles of about 32 drawings plus inbetweens and clean-up. One illustrator, three to four weeks, $8–15k. The flipbook is trivial once the drawings exist: 24 pages, 90 × 60 mm, one ink, saddle-glued at the spine, under $2 a copy at 500. This is the most expensive of my three and the only one that needs a person hired.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — ONE DRAWING, on his desk this week. A5 card: the aperture at 1:4 — 128.9 × 100.8 mm, corner radius 13.8 mm, brass line 5.0 mm — printed true from the model, and two hands crossing it drawn by hand in one sitting. He looks at it for two seconds and says yes or no to the entire film, before anyone is hired. If yes, the second step is a 12-drawing flipbook of one hand-off, which is a fortnight.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:people-2`

### ¿YA ALCANZAS?  ·  screen 6.2/10

**REGISTER** environmental and child — polychrome, three-dimensional, and the only object in the programme a stranger completes with their own body. Six measured inks and a pencil, against eleven concepts of two-ink austerity.  
**ARTEFACT** A growth chart. Vitreous enamel on 1.2 mm steel, two panels of 1000 × 140 mm, screen-printed in six colours taken straight off the vehicle's own mural board, mounted with its zero at the finished floor beside the bus in the Nolita room. Its graduations are not centimetres. They are the bus's own measured landmarks, each carrying its figure, its unit and its provenance grade in 6 pt. The seventh ink is a soft pencil on a chain, applied by the public.

> A height chart whose marks are the real bus, whose top graduation is a confession that we do not know how tall the bus is, and which is blank until people stand against it.

There is a photograph of a small blonde child in a magenta coat standing at the nose of this bus, looking up at it. She stands nearer the camera than the vehicle and no object of known size sits at her depth, so HER HEIGHT CANNOT BE RECOVERED FROM WHAT WE HOLD. The only children this company can measure are the ones who come.

So build the ruler. Not 10, 20, 30 — the bus: ROCKER TO GROUND 317.7 · FRONT BUMPER CROWN 450.1 · TOP OF THE TYRE 665.0 · COUNTER TOP, FRONT END 1189.8 · COUNTER TOP, TAIL END 1244.9 · SILL, FIRST WINDOW 1312.1 · SILL, THIRD WINDOW 1334.7 · ROOF AT THE REAR AXLE 1983.5. Two of those marks say the thing nobody has ever printed: THE COUNTER IS NOT LEVEL. It rises 55.1 mm over its own 3.106 m because the body is raked 17.75 mm per metre, and the three serving sills climb 22.6 mm from the first hole to the third. The middle sill is drawn as a soft band, not a line, because its position carries the pillar asymmetry this project retracted at 0.67σ — and a child can read a fuzzy mark.

Above every graduation, alone at the top of the panel: THE REAL BUS'S ABSOLUTE ROOF HEIGHT IS OPEN AND UNMEASURED. The chart runs out of certainty before it runs out of children. Staff pencil a mark, the family writes a first name and a date in their own hand, and photographs it. After a year the object is a crowd.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — EVERY GRADUATION. None of these heights exists anywhere in the record, because the whole project works in the authored un-dropped frame and converts nothing; above-ground heights need rake_drop(x) = 0.047925 + 0.017750·x applied station by station, which is a mesh operation. That is how the counter's 55.1 mm rise and the sills' 22.6 mm climb appear for the first time — a tape measure held against the real bus would never be trusted to find an 11 mm step, and no photograph resolves it. The model also supplies the six ink values (the mural board's own measured palette) and the confession at the top, which is STATE.md's verbatim sentence. AND THE MODEL SUPPLIES THE CHART'S LIMIT: every figure assumes a level ground plane and the model's own tyre state, so the panel prints its own ceiling — the rake is quoted 17.6 ± 3.4 mm/m, and nothing here has been checked against the floor the actual bus stands on.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — IT IS A FAMILY-RESTAURANT CLICHÉ AND IT KNOWS IT. Height charts are on the wall of every chain in America. This one lives or dies entirely on its 6 pt; if the measurements are decorative rather than load-bearing it is a Rainforest Cafe wall with better typography, and that failure mode is one design review away. SECOND, AND THIS IS THE ONE NOT TO SAND: it prints, permanently, on a wall in his dining room, that the company does not know how tall its own bus is. Any brand manager will read that as self-sabotage. It is also the most honest sentence the company could put in public and the reason the object is not decoration. Keep it, or do not build the piece. THIRD: children's names on a public wall. First names and a visit date only, in the family's own hand, and the company keeps no record anywhere — and a parent will still object, and will be entitled to. FOURTH: it is site-specific, permanent and unsellable. It commits a wall, it is not merch, and it does not travel to another site without surveying that site's floor. FIFTH: the fuzzy middle sill is a bet that a drawn uncertainty band means something to a stranger. On a height chart I think it does. It has not been tested on anybody.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — All watched print this session. rake_drop(x) = 0.047925 + 0.017750·x, above ground = authored z minus that. ROCKER TO GROUND 317.7 mm (STATE.md, mesh). FRONT BUMPER CROWN 450.1 mm, DERIVED from BLADE_TOP_Z 0.5360 at x +2.140 — and the real vehicle's bumper height has NEVER been independently measured; the one route proposed for it, a projection solve on ref_workshop.jpg, broke when the vehicle's own long edges were found not to share a vanishing point. TOP OF THE TYRE 665.0 mm (TIRE_R 0.3325, wheels skip the shear). COUNTER TOP 1189.8 mm at x +0.918 and 1244.9 mm at x −2.188 — a rise of 55.1 mm over 3.106 m. SILL 1312.1 mm at the first window and 1334.7 mm at the third, a climb of 22.6 mm over their 1.270 m centre span. ROOF AT THE REAR AXLE 1983.5 mm (mesh, n = 230). Apertures 515.5 × 403.0, r 55, three, equal. Six inks from lid_gen.py: #371A13, #9F2D16, #A34215, #AC7718, #B8964C, #AC9011. NOT PRINTED, DELIBERATELY: the two per-bay sill steps separately, because their difference is the 20.0 ± 30.0 mm pillar asymmetry retracted at rev 76 — the middle sill is drawn as a band of that width instead. UNMEASURED and printed on the face: the real bus's absolute roof height; the height of the child in ref_nolita_front34.jpg; the floor the actual bus stands on.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Vitreous enamel on 1.2 mm steel, two panels 1000 × 140 mm, six screens: roughly $900–1,600 for the first pair from a proper enamel sign maker, four to six weeks, and the tooling is reusable so a second site is a few hundred. It is real enamel because that is the substrate of the Mexican rótulo and the ex-voto, and because it survives a restaurant and takes graphite. A screen-printed vinyl on aluminium composite is a $120 proof of the same artwork if he wants to live with it for a month first. The pencil on a chain is $4.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Thursday, under $40. Print the chart 1:1 on a 2 m roll from any wide-format shop, tape it to the wall of the Nolita room with its zero at the finished floor, and measure the first three children who walk in. Photograph the strip with their marks on it. That photograph settles the whole question — whether the marks make people laugh or make them shrug — before a single enamel panel is fired.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:people-3`

### EL RUIDO ERA GENTE  ·  screen 4.8/10

**REGISTER** photographic, and people — the programme's first four-colour object, and the first time this archive is PRINTED as a photograph instead of mined as a measurement. Nothing here is drawn, ranged or set in a hairline rule.  
**ARTEFACT** A set of four kiss-cut cards. 4-colour process plus black type, 300 gsm uncoated warm white. Each card reproduces one reference frame at a stated modulus of ONE PHOTOGRAPH PIXEL = 0.20 mm, so each card is the size its own frame earns: ref_side.jpg 204.8 × 153.6 mm · ref_rear34.jpg 240.0 × 164.8 mm · ref_workshop.jpg 240.0 × 164.8 mm · ref_nolita_front34.jpg 140.0 × 93.4 mm. The vehicle is die-cut out of every one — one closed path per card, the bus a hole you look through.

> Cut the bus out of its own photographs, and what is left on the card is the people this project spent seventy-six revisions calling noise.

Four of the nine distinct photographs this company owns of its own bus contain people, and the record treats every one of them as damage. I ran the grep: FIFTEEN statements in TEN source files describe a human being as a problem. probe_rev39_flank.py carries a rectangle — MAN_ROWS, MAN_COLS = (420, 768), (90, 300) — whose only job is to delete the man in the red shirt: 210 × 348 px, 9.29 % of the frame, and the comment above it says the exclusion is PRICED, never quietly trimmed. lid_gen.py masked 6.09 % of the mural board because a palm frond and a man's cap were in front of it. t1_detail.py concedes that 37 % of a serving bay's tonal variance is the man and the surfaces he occludes and no dressing can produce it. F220 records three traced points at residuals −16.7, −16.1, −13.6 px sitting on a child's hair; removing her moved the published headline 43 % and its stated error 5.1×.

So print the photographs and cut the bus out. What survives the die is a man eating, looking straight down the lens; a woman with her chin on her hand waiting for lunch under a palapa; a child in a magenta coat facing a bus she is too short to be served from; a woman standing in the roof she has just cut open. On the reverse, in 7 pt, the line of source code that deleted each one, with its filename, so anyone can grep it. Hold the ref_nolita_front34 card up in the Nolita room and the real bus fills its own absence.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — THE CUT PATH, and only for one card. probe_rev39_flank.py already warps the render into ref_side.jpg's own pixel frame and computes the vehicle silhouette as `sil = ok & (warp.max(2) < 250)` — that mask IS the die line, at the photograph's exact camera, which no hand-tracing can match. It exists today. For the other three frames the camera is NOT SOLVED: those cuts are hand-traced and each card prints CAMERA UNSOLVED — CUT HAND-TRACED on its reverse. The model contributes one card's truth and honestly contributes nothing to the other three.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — CONSENT, and it is not solvable by design. Seven real people photographed between roughly 2006 and 2013 who never agreed to be printed. The man in ref_side.jpg is looking directly into the lens and is recognisable to anyone who knows him. The child is a minor. At 0.20 mm per pixel her head is about 14 mm across and she is seen from behind — which protects her and weakens the picture, and both of those are true at once. Do NOT fix this by blurring: blurring is the same subtraction tic in a softer coat. Fix it by asking, or by not printing that card, and accept that the set is worse with three. SECOND RISK: this card set is a critique of the work Donald has been paying for. It says, on an object, that seventy-six revisions cut the people out. Get the tone one degree wrong and it reads as insolence rather than as the company's own archive handed back. THIRD: 127 dpi. Every printer will phone and call it a fault.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Frame sizes read off disk: ref_side.jpg 1024 × 768, ref_rear34.jpg 1200 × 824, ref_workshop.jpg 1200 × 824, ref_nolita_front34.jpg 700 × 467. probe_rev39_flank.py MAN_ROWS (420, 768) / MAN_COLS (90, 300) = 210 × 348 px = 73,080 px = 9.29 % of a 786,432 px frame (watched print). lid_gen.py occluder mask 6.09 % of the board interior. t1_detail.py: 37 % of bay 1's variance. OPEN_FINDINGS F220: residuals −16.7, −16.1, −13.6 px, pixel values (205,165,157), (204,172,161), (193,166,149); −2.94 ± 0.46 px rms 2.78 → −2.05 ± 0.09 rms 0.55. Grep count 15 hits across 10 .py files (watched print). Modulus 0.20 mm/px = 127 dpi (arithmetic). UNMEASURED and printed as such: three of the four cameras are unsolved; whether ref_workshop.jpg's green bus is this same vehicle before its red paint is UNRESOLVED IN THE RECORD, and SPEC §7.2 forbids using livery colour to decide it.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Cheap and fast. 250 sets of four, digital 4-colour on 300 gsm uncoated, kiss-cut on a Zünd or Kongsberg from four supplied paths: roughly $400–700 all in, plus one afternoon of matte work for the ref_side card and a day of scalpel-accurate tracing for the other three. No new render, no new photography, no licensing. The only expensive line is legal time on releases, which is a decision, not a build.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — ONE card, tonight. Print ref_side.jpg at 204.8 × 153.6 mm on the office colour laser on 200 gsm, cut the bus out with a scalpel, write the probe_rev39_flank.py rectangle on the back in pen. Then take it into the Nolita room, hold it up in front of the actual bus so the real vehicle fills the hole, and photograph that. One photograph is the whole pitch and it costs nothing.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:people-1`

## PHOTOGRAPHIC

### 515.5 × 403  ·  screen 7.07/10

**REGISTER** photographic — still life, product and service; three-dimensional (a fabricated instrument); food-and-hand-off; people  
**ARTEFACT** Two parts. THE INSTRUMENT: one physical serving aperture cut 1:1 from the mesh — 12 mm birch ply, 900 × 700 mm outer, aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm with a true 55 mm corner radius, faced in the measured cream #CED0C8; a counter lip returning 321 mm at 107 mm thickness, brass across 18.6 % of its outer edge, set 118.0 mm below the sill, and TILTED 17.75 mm/m. THE OUTPUT: menu cards at 94.8 × 338.3 mm — the vehicle's own measured pillar-card size — face in 4-colour, reverse in one ink. 350 gsm uncoated.

> Build the serving hatch once, at full size, out of the model — then never photograph this brand through anything else, so the hole is the constant and the food and the hands are the variable.

Cut the serving hatch out of the model, build it once at 1:1, and make it the only frame this brand is ever photographed through.

12 mm birch, 900 × 700 outer, aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm with a true 55 mm corner radius, cut from the mesh's own outline. Faced in the measured cream, #CED0C8. A counter lip returning 321 mm at 107 mm thick, its outer edge carrying brass across 18.6 % of that edge — CNT_NOSE_F, measured over 113 columns and corroborated at 0.191 on a second frame — set 118.0 mm below the sill, which is the measured gap between Z_SILL and CNT_ZT.

And the lip is not level. The vehicle sits nose-down at 17.75 mm/m, so the real counter rises 55.1 mm across its 3.106 m — 1189.8 mm above ground at the front end, 1244.9 at the tail. Over a 900 mm prop that is 16.0 mm of tilt that no fabricator would guess and no photograph would ever have told them. That one detail is what separates this from a nice window.

Then shoot through it, during service: a plate, a forearm, a jug blender mid-pour, the tray crossing the brass. Camera fixed, frame never cropped. Output as cards at 94.8 × 338.3 mm, the vehicle's own pillar-card size, three of which hang on the real bus. Face in four colours; reverse in one — the dish, the date, the dimension, and one line of what was not measured.

The reveal depth is stencilled on the back edge: 70 mm, UNMEASURED, A CHOICE. Because it is.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The model is the cutting file, and without it this is decoration. It supplies the exact 1:1 outline (515.5 × 403.0, r 55 — and the radius is independently corroborated by the photograph at ≈12 px ≈ 57 mm, an agreement you can only state when you hold both), the counter's 107 mm thickness and 321 mm plan depth, the 18.6 % brass band, and the 118.0 mm sill-to-top offset. Above all it supplies the RAKE. No photograph in the set would have told a fabricator that the serving counter is not level; that comes from RAKE_DZDX and the drop model, and I validated that model against STATE.md's three published figures (71.0 / 28.4 / 47.9 mm) before using it. What the model explicitly cannot give is depth: the galley's Y is chosen everywhere and the source says 'DEPTH (Y) IS NOT MEASURABLE', so the reveal is a declared choice and is labelled on the object itself.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) THIS IS A FOOD SHOOT, AND FOOD SHOOTS ARE WHERE BRANDS GO GENERIC. The moment someone art-directs it — tweezers, a spritz bottle, a marble slab, a wiped rim — it becomes indistinguishable from every other taqueria's feed and the measurement becomes wallpaper. The only defence is a written, enforced rule: shot during service, not after; the hand in frame is the hand that made it; nothing placed for camera; nothing wiped. If the first set comes back styled, the concept has failed and should be killed rather than fixed. I would rather it die there than survive as competent food photography. (2) The prop asserts a dimension the photographs cannot check. The 70 mm reveal is a pose choice. Anyone who reads the stencil sees that; anyone who does not reads a fabrication as a measurement. That is the price and it is paid in public on the object. (3) IT SHOWS THE REAL KITCHEN — scarred hands, stained cloths, a black griddle — and the restaurant may not want that photographed. If it gets cleaned up, see risk 1. (4) It is the only one of my three with an operational dependency: it needs a working service and the cooperation of the people in it. (5) Adjacency, declared rather than hidden: the bench holds THE PASS, LA VENTANA, APERTURA, BODEGÓN, COUNTER HEIGHT and THE BRASS LINE, and the shortlist-adjacent TRES HUECOS makes the apertures a graphic mark with the vehicle deleted. This is not those. Those are drawings of the hole; this is the hole, built, used as a permanent camera jig. It is a companion to TRES HUECOS, and I would ship them together. No artwork is touched, so SPEC 10.10 is not engaged; no emblem appears, so F191 is not engaged.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm (BAY_W 0.5155; Z_HEAD 1.7750 − Z_SILL 1.3720 = 403.0 mm, read from t1_shell.py), corner radius BAY_R 55.0 mm, three of them, EQUAL. Counter CNT_ZT 1.2540 / CNT_ZB 1.1470 = 107 mm thick; CNT_Y_IN 0.8450 → CNT_Y_OUT 1.1660 = 321 mm plan depth; CNT_NOSE_F 0.1860 = 18.6 % brass band; CNT_X0 +0.9180 to CNT_X1 −2.1880 = 3.106 m length. Sill above counter top = 1372.0 − 1254.0 = 118.0 mm (same un-dropped frame). RAKE_DZDX 17.75 mm/m; counter top above ground 1189.8 mm at the front end, 1244.9 mm at the tail, RISE 55.1 mm — computed two independent ways and agreeing, on a drop model first validated against STATE.md's 71.0 / 28.4 / 47.9 mm. Cream (206, 208, 200) = #CED0C8. Brass linear (0.660, 0.475, 0.175), roughness 0.255, photographed at h ≈ 32°, s ≈ 0.86. Menu card 94.8 × 338.3 mm, centre z 1566.7 mm. UNMEASURED and stencilled as such: the reveal depth (70 mm), because the galley's Y is chosen everywhere and never measured.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The cheapest concept here by a wide margin and the fastest to a real object. One 2440 × 1220 sheet of 12 mm birch, a litre of matched paint, a brass strip, and half a day on a router table or CNC — call it under $200 in materials (ESTIMATE, not a quote). Cards are a short-run digital print, order of $80 per 100. Total under $400. It needs NO RENDER AT ALL: the model's entire contribution is a DXF and six dimensions, so there is no queue, no camera solve, no large-format chain, and no collision with F191 or F193. The instrument is also durable — it is a jig that keeps working for years, which is the only concept here with a compounding return.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — One hour, no budget, no model export needed beyond one printed outline. Cut the aperture alone — 515.5 × 403.0, r 55 — in cardboard or 6 mm ply. No counter lip, no brass, no paint. Hold it up in the doorway of a live service and shoot twelve frames on a phone: a plate coming through, a hand, a forearm, the griddle behind. Bring him the twelve frames beside twelve of the restaurant's existing photographs. If the twelve are not immediately better, the concept is wrong and we know inside seven days for the cost of a sheet of cardboard.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:photographic-2`

### LA DÉCIMA  ·  screen 5.4/10

**REGISTER** photographic — editorial forgery; the render placed against the photograph  
**ARTEFACT** A double-sided card, 128 × 100 mm, 600 gsm duplexed board, 4-colour both faces. RECTO: ref_side.jpg, the real near side, at 86.7 × 65.0 mm — the largest that frame can honestly be printed. VERSO: the off side, rendered and pushed back through the archive's own optical fingerprint (1024 × 768, 211.5 px/m, matched warm cast, matched JPEG artefact), at exactly the same size and in exact register, so that held to a window the two flanks coincide. The confession is set inside the image, not on a label.

> Nine photographs of this bus exist and not one shows the off side; this is the tenth, printed the same size as the real ones, and it says on its own face that it is not a photograph.

Nine photographs of this vehicle exist. I verified it this session: fifteen candidate files, ten distinct checksums, and the tenth resolves to a 246 × 197 downsample of ref_playa_34.png — 64 × 64 thumbnail difference 3.92, against 57 to 77 for every other frame. Nine scenes, twenty years, two countries.

Not one of them shows the off side. SPEC grades it E — never photographed. Twin outward-hinged cargo doors and three glazed windows in the same bay positions is an expert inference, not an observation. An entire flank of a vehicle that has been photographed for two decades.

So make the tenth. Render the off side, then push it back through the archive's own optical fingerprint until it is the same kind of object as the nine: 1024 × 768, the same 211.5 px/m from the 2.400 m wheelbase, the same warm cast the record notes in ref_side and ref_rear34, the same order of JPEG artefact — and print it at 86.7 × 65.0 mm, because that is the largest the real frame can honestly go.

A double-sided card. Recto the real near side. Verso the off side, same size, same register, so that held up to a window the wheels land on the wheels and the three serving holes sit against solid cargo doors.

Across the verso, inside the image and never on a label: SIDE, OFF SIDE (−Y) — GRADE E, NEVER PHOTOGRAPHED. THIS IS NOT A PHOTOGRAPH.

And it carries no folk art, no script, no sunburst. Geometry transfers between livery states; paint and artwork do not. Nothing licenses artwork on that flank, so there is none on the card. Inventing it is the one lie this piece could not survive.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Everything, and this is the only one of my three where that is literally true. The off side is not recoverable from any photograph that exists; it exists solely as geometry, and 76 revisions built it. There is also a precise mechanical gift: ref_side.jpg behaves almost orthographically across the flank — SPEC records a flat 211.5 px/m with NO VANISHING POINT, both hub centres at exactly one tyre radius — and the model's `side` orthographic camera already fits the vehicle today (5.90 → 4.0563 m against a 4.065 m body) while the front and rear cameras clip. So the forgery's projection is not a guess; it is the same projection the archive itself uses, and it is the one camera in the rig that works right now with no fix.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — The real one first. (1) THIS IS A FORGED PHOTOGRAPHIC RECORD OF A REAL VEHICLE OWNED BY A REAL BUSINESS, MADE IN THE SAME FORMAT AS THE GENUINE RECORD. Confessions printed inside images get cropped, screenshotted and reposted without them. Within a year somebody will cite this as evidence of what the off side of the Tacombi bus looks like, and it is an inference. That is not a hypothetical failure mode, it is the normal life of an image. Mitigations are real but partial: text inside the image; a physically double-sided card so the honest half travels with the forged half; the file never released as a bare JPEG. IF THE OWNER IS NOT COMFORTABLE WITH A FABRICATED PHOTOGRAPH OF HIS OWN VEHICLE EXISTING, THIS SHOULD DIE AT THE CONCEPT STAGE, AND I WOULD NOT ARGUE HIM OUT OF IT. (2) It bets the piece on a render this project's own instruments say is not good enough: gloss 0.412 against a 0.60 bar, flank worst region 0.686, five materials still a constant roughness. Photoreal is the one register where line art cannot hide those. So this is a genuine test of whether the photoreal model is any good, and it may return 'no'. That is a useful answer and it is half the reason to run it. (3) F191 AND F193, DECLARED NOT ROUTED AROUND. Unlike a line programme, this DOES touch what those holds gate. Two honest defences: the roundel is on the nose, so a pure off-side flank does not print the emblem at all — it clears F191 by geometry, not evasion; and this is not the delivery render, it is 1024 × 768, deliberately degraded, 86.7 mm wide. But the hold is his, and he should be asked once, as multiple choice, before it is made. (4) SPEC 10.10: this omits artwork rather than replicating it. The rule-34 defence is explicit — the bar's object is the MODEL, and here there is no evidence any artwork exists on that flank to replicate.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Nine distinct scenes, established this session by md5 over fifteen files (ten hashes) plus a thumbnail-difference test that identifies ref_source.jpeg as a 246 × 197 downsample of ref_playa_34.png at 3.92 mean absolute difference against 57–77 for all others. ref_side.jpg 1024 × 768 = 86.7 × 65.0 mm at 300 dpi. S = 211.5 ± 1.3 px/m from the invariant 2.400 m wheelbase (4.728 mm/px), with SPEC's note of a flat scale and NO VANISHING POINT across the flank. Body 4065 mm long, 1750 wide, spanning 859.75 px = 84.0 % of the 1024 px frame. `side` ortho camera fit 5.90 → 4.0563 m. Wheel geometry for the register: track 1369/1359, tyre Ø664.9, rim flange Ø439.6 (16-inch, not 15), rear arch gap 39.7 mm, front 40.7 mm — and the 1.0 mm difference is NOT printed as a fact, the lock is ±8. UNMEASURED and stated on the card: the off side's paint, artwork, hardware and glazing — SPEC grade E, never photographed; and the absolute roof height, which STATE.md calls OPEN and UNMEASURED.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — One render on a camera that already works, one degradation pass, one duplexed card. Machine time is hours, not days: a `side` ortho at 1024 × 768 is a single frame at roughly 5 minutes, and the degradation is a script. Duplexed 600 gsm cards, short run, order of $150 per 100 (ESTIMATE, not a quote). No fabrication, no photography, no access to the physical vehicle, no new geometry. The one genuine cost is a decision, and it costs nothing to take before anything is printed.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — No printing, no forgery, no commitment. Render the off side today at 1024 × 768 through the `side` camera that already fits, apply the degradation pass, and lay it beside ref_side.jpg at the same 86.7 × 65.0 mm on a single A4 sheet. One afternoon. That sheet puts the only two questions the concept turns on directly in front of him: does this read as a photograph, and do you want it to? If the first answer is no, it is abandoned for the cost of one render. If the second answer is no, it should be abandoned regardless — and that is a legitimate outcome, not a failure.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — False  ·  **id** `dream:photographic-3`

### UN PÍXEL, UN MILÍMETRO  ·  screen 4.07/10

**REGISTER** photographic — documentary / archival, polychrome (4-colour), people  
**ARTEFACT** A suite of nine sheets, one per distinct photograph of this vehicle, each printed at exactly one image pixel per millimetre with no interpolation — every pixel a hard-edged 1 mm square. Sizes are therefore not chosen: 1400 × 933 mm (IMG_2073), 1200 × 824 (ref_rear34, ref_workshop), 1024 × 768 (ref_side), 700 × 467, 550 × 412, 500 × 400, 480 × 320, 450 × 450. CMYK litho, uncoated 170 gsm warm white. Inside the bus's silhouette only, the sheet switches to a camera-matched photoreal render at 300 dpi — 40× the surrounding resolution.

> The entire photographic record of this bus, printed at one pixel per millimetre, with the measured vehicle rendered sharp inside its own silhouette and every human being left as a grid of 1 mm squares.

Nine photographs of this vehicle exist. The largest is 1400 × 933 px. The primary flank frame — the one 76 revisions of measurement were driven off — is 1024 × 768, which at 300 dpi prints 86.7 × 65.0 mm, smaller than a postcard. That is the whole archive, and nobody has said it out loud.

So print each frame at one pixel per millimetre. ref_side becomes 1024 × 768 mm. IMG_2073 becomes 1400 × 933 and fits a B0 sheet with 7 mm to spare on the long edge. At that scale the panel gap this project has argued about for years is 1.16 mm wide, the hubcap badge is 18 mm, and the seven people the record spent 76 revisions deleting — the child in the magenta puffer, the man in the red shirt whose shirt broke every front-arch measurement in rev 8, the cook whose sleeve killed a finding — become the largest legible things in the room.

Then, inside the bus's silhouette only, the sheet stops being the photograph and becomes the render: camera-matched, 300 dpi, forty times the resolution around it. The people stay in 1 mm squares. The vehicle goes razor sharp. That is an exact and slightly cruel portrait of what this project has actually been.

Each sheet carries its camera's provenance in the margin — SOLVED FROM EXIF, INFERRED, or UNSOLVED. Only ref_nolita_front34.jpg is solved (f = 544.4 px ± ~2 %, from its own EXIF). An unsolved sheet prints as pure photograph with an empty outline and the word UNSOLVED. That is not a hole in the series. That is the series.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Two things, both load-bearing. First, the composite: only the model can supply a camera-matched image of this vehicle at 300 dpi on a 1400 mm sheet — 16,535 × 11,024 px — which is 40× what the source frame holds and is unobtainable from any photograph that exists. Second, the silhouette matte itself, which requires the T1_ALPHA=1 transparent-background path that is built and guarded and, by the record's own account, has never once been run. This concept's first act is to finally run it. It is also the first honest test of the large-format delivery chain (hq_render.py → stitch.py → post.py), which F192 says has never been proven at size, on the owner's own ruling 'Bigger — large-format print.'

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — Four, none of them sanded. (1) IDENTIFIABLE PEOPLE, INCLUDING A CHILD, PRINTED A METRE TALL, NONE OF WHOM CONSENTED. The child is seen from behind, which helps; the eater in the red shirt is face-on and centre-frame and is not defensible without a release. This is not theoretical — the series may have to ship with two of nine sheets pulled, and if the eater cannot be cleared, sheet 1 does not print and the wrap states the absence. (2) It reads as a gimmick on a screen — 'someone blew up a bad JPEG' — and only works physically, at distance. It cannot be sold from a PDF, which is a real commercial problem. (3) The composite asserts sharpness inside a blur, and a casual viewer reads that as retouching rather than as an argument. (4) F191: on the front-3/4 frames the VW roundel falls inside the render silhouette, so the mark the owner has rejected nine times would print sharp and large. The rule is that the roundel stays PHOTOGRAPH, always, and the margin says so — 'NOSE ROUNDEL: PHOTOGRAPH. THE MODEL'S IS NOT RIGHT (F191).' That makes his hold a visible feature rather than something routed around silently. Separately, on F92: this reproduces existing photographs taken at Playa; it does not build, relight, or use playa_env.py as a delivery frame. F92's object is the rendered Playa hero. Different object (rule 34) — and this is nonetheless the first thing in the whole programme that serves 'the emotional bar that sits ABOVE clinical accuracy', because it is the only one that shows the bus in a place with people in it. SPEC 10.10 is satisfied, not defended around: the artwork is reproduced exactly and the composite may not clean it up.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Frame dimensions and print sizes, all watched print this session: 1400×933 / 1200×824 / 1024×768 / 700×467 / 550×412 / 500×400 / 480×320 / 450×450; ref_side at 300 dpi = 86.7 × 65.0 mm; IMG_2073 at 1 px = 1 mm = 1400 × 933 mm against a B0 sheet of 1000 × 1414 (7 mm margin). Nine distinct scenes, verified by md5 (fifteen files, ten hashes, the tenth a downsample). Scale S = 211.5 px/m from the 2.400 m wheelbase, so 1 px = 4.728 mm: panel gap GAPW 5.5 mm = 1.16 px, hubcap badge 86.9 mm = 18.38 px, festoon pitch 28.6 mm = 6.05 px, serving aperture 515.5 mm = 109.03 px, body 4065 mm = 859.75 px = 84.0 % of the frame width. EXIF: ref_nolita_front34.jpg, SONY DSC-RX100, 10.4 mm, 28 mm equiv, f/1.8, ISO 200, DigitalZoomRatio 1.0, DateTimeOriginal 2014-04-05 13:20:23, f = 544.4 px ± ~2 %; IMG_3840.jpeg, Canon EOS 5D Mark II, 24 mm, f/2.8, ISO 2000, 1/80 s, DateTimeOriginal 2010-10-04 16:00:11; the two are 1,278 days apart. Only two of the nine yield a date at all; the rest are UNMEASURED. Seven of the nine cameras are UNSOLVED and the sheets say so.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Genuinely cheap to prove, moderate to ship. A wide-format inkjet proof of one 700 × 467 mm sheet is tens of dollars; a full set of nine as CMYK litho on uncoated stock is a few hundred for a short run (ESTIMATE, not a measurement — I have no quote). The expensive half is machine time: one camera solve plus one very large render per sheet, and eight of the nine cameras are unsolved today. That is why the series is designed to ship incrementally and to print honestly when a camera cannot be recovered. Nothing here needs new geometry, new photography, or access to the physical vehicle — which directly answers the critic's finding 9(c). The render side is ~5 min a frame at preview size, so a 300 dpi band is hours, and the banded delivery chain already exists for the full sheet.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — One sheet, this week, 700 × 467 mm: ref_nolita_front34.jpg — the only EXIF-solved camera, and the frame with the child in it — printed at 1 px = 1 mm on a wide-format inkjet with nearest-neighbour scaling so every pixel is a hard 1 mm square, with a single 200 mm-wide vertical band through the bus where the render is composited at 300 dpi. One camera solve (already done), one 2362 × 5516 px render, one print. If the render slips, print the same sheet with the silhouette drawn as a hairline and the words THE BUS GOES HERE, AT 40× THIS RESOLUTION — that version is one afternoon with no render at all and still makes the entire argument in his hands.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:photographic-1`

## SATURATED POLYCHROME

### MI COMBI  ·  screen 8.0/10

**REGISTER** polychrome / child / packaging-as-object — six saturated screen inks on vinyl, two of them fluorescent  
**ARTEFACT** Die-cut vinyl sticker. 162.6 mm nose to tail (the bus at exactly 1:25, printed on the liner back as ESCALA 1:25). White gloss self-adhesive vinyl, SIX screen colours: measured red 196,49,36 · measured cream 206,208,200 · menu-strip yellow 172,144,17 · mural orange 163,66,21 · fluorescent orange (day-glo, the sun and all the lit lamps) · dark maroon. Two custom dies, one with a papel picado band across the top and one without.

> The original deliverable, finally made: the bus die-cut tight in six saturated screen inks, where the sixth ink is the line AND the occlusion pass, and the papel picado is the cut rather than the print.

Six inks, and five of them are the vehicle's own measured colours. The sixth is dark maroon and it does two jobs at once: it carries the contour line AND the ambient-occlusion pass, posterised to one threshold and printed as a solid. That is the owner's locked style executed as separations instead of as an effect — "vector line and flat colour, shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset" becomes a six-screen job the moment occlusion stops being a grey and becomes an ink. Nothing else in the 78 has done this; every other line concept treats shading as tone.

Drawn from hero34r, the rear three-quarter — the side that opens. You get the counter, the propped tail board, the drip-rail string of 118 lamps at their realised 28.52 mm pitch, and all three holes. You do not get the nose, so the VW roundel the owner has rejected nine times is off the artefact entirely. That is declared on the sheet, not smuggled: F191 stays shut and this object does not pretend otherwise. The hubcaps are drawn as ⌀274.0 red domes with a plain light disc at ⌀86.9 where the badge goes — a disc, not a glyph.

The die is cut tight to the bus, plus the sun, plus a band of papel picado — CUT, with its own perforations, not printed. A papel picado and a die-cut sticker are the same operation: one closed path. The wheels get the earlier cartoon treatment he said he liked.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Everything, and the dependency is his, not mine: the locked style says "shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset", so without an AO pass the sixth ink is a guess. The model also supplies the rear-three-quarter camera (hero34r already exists in studio.py), the 118 lamps at 28.52 mm realised pitch, the hubcap at ⌀274.0 with its 86.9 mm badge circle, and the tail board propped at the built 38.0 deg. No photograph in the reference set is a clean rear three-quarter with the board up. ARTWORK DEFENCE (rule 34 / SPEC 10.10): the mural, the script and the Calidad sunburst are STYLISED to six flats on this object. The hard bar's object is the MODEL; a 162 mm sticker is a different object and cannot carry a 2569748-pixel mural. Said out loud rather than done quietly.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — THREE, and I am keeping all three. (1) His locked scene — "nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado" — excludes the taqueria. A child gets a vehicle and not the hand-off. This is the one of my three that answers the critic's finding 4 with a NO, and it does so because the owner locked it, not because I subtracted. (2) DAY-GLO IS FUGITIVE. Fluorescent screen ink loses a large fraction of its brightness in months of direct sun. On an object briefed to be "something families keep", the sun is literally the first thing that will fade. A lightfast orange survives and is visibly duller. I am not making that swap for him — it is a choice between an object that shouts now and an object that lasts, and he should make it looking at both. (3) SPEC and playa_env.py both REFUTE papel picado in the reference — the band is 55.1 % foliage, 13.4 % crimson heads, no flag silhouettes. Printing it is an invention. He licensed it himself and deferred the decision to "when the sticker is actually being built". This is that moment; the two dies are the question.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Proof: under $100 for 20 of each die, digitally printed and contour-cut, 2-3 working days at any local vinyl shop. Production: six-colour screen on vinyl, 1,000 units, roughly $1,200-1,800 including two custom steel-rule dies at $150-250 each; under $0.60 a unit at 5,000. Fluorescent screen ink on vinyl is a standard trade product. The blocker is not printing — it is that NO line pass and NO AO pass exist in this tree yet. Both come out of the same Grease Pencil Line Art build the programme has already named as its keystone, so this concept pays for that item rather than waiting behind it.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Build the Grease Pencil Line Art pass plus an AO pass on the hero34r camera that already exists, posterise the occlusion to ONE threshold, lay the six flats by hand, and get 20 contour-cut proofs of each die printed digitally — one with the papel picado band, one without. Two stickers on his desk this week and one sentence: WHICH DIE. That is the trigger he set himself in rev 39 and nobody has ever pulled it.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — False  ·  **id** `dream:polychrome-1`

### CON TODO  ·  screen 7.83/10

**REGISTER** polychrome / food-and-hand-off / people — six-colour screen print, two fluorescents, on a sheet cut to the aperture  
**ARTEFACT** Six-colour screen print, edition of 100. Sheet cut to 515.5 x 403.0 mm with 55 mm radius corners — the serving aperture at 1:1, so the paper IS the hole. 300 gsm uncoated. Inks: fluorescent orange (the blown exterior), fluorescent yellow (the strip light), PINK 206,118,124, GREEN 96,128,54, CREAMV 232,224,198, and a dark. Hung as a set of three it puts the flank on the wall at true size.

> The view nobody has made in 76 revisions — from inside the galley, out through bay 2, five seconds before service, with two hands meeting over the brass line.

The sheet is the hole: 515.5 x 403.0 mm, corners at r 55, three of them equal because the mesh says equal.

Across the top, the bobble fringe — 65 balls, 9 mm radius, 26 mm pitch, 13 mm inset — with INFERIDO set under it, because each ball is about two pixels in the only frame that holds it. Across the bottom, the counter: the cream slab, 107 mm thick and 321 mm deep, and its brass nosing capping 18.6 % of the outer edge. That fraction is one of the most carefully taken measurements in this repository — 4.52 ± 0.23 px of a 24.84 px edge, corroborated at 0.191 on a second frame — and it has never once been drawn. Behind, the warm fluorescent tube in its measured emission colour, printed as a bar of day-glo yellow: "a serving hatch shows you its strip light."

Outside the hole: nothing. A flat field of fluorescent orange, because the roof opening does all the light and the exterior blows out. No palms, no market hall. Refusing to draw the place is how this obeys F92 — "deprioritised, not cancelled… do not act on it either" — instead of quietly rebuilding the Playa terrace, which the last round did without naming the ruling.

Two hands meet over the brass line with a plate between them, and they are DRAWN, commissioned from a named illustrator or rotulista, in the vehicle's own food palette: PINK for the meat, GREEN for the garnish, CREAMV for the plate. Those are the colours the bus already uses to draw food on its own menu strips. Credit printed: hands and food drawn — everything else measured.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The interior view exists only in the model. No photograph looks OUT of this vehicle and none can be taken — the bus is parked indoors at Nolita with a wall behind it. The model gives the aperture at 515.5 x 403.0 r55, the fringe's 65 balls at 26 mm pitch, the counter slab and its 18.6 % brass fraction, the plancha edge at top z 1.455, the worktop at the measured z 1.5010, and the strip light's position and emission (1.000, 0.918, 0.790). It does NOT give the hands, the food, or the galley's depth — Y is authored everywhere in t1_detail, never measured, and the docstring says so. All three absences are printed on the sheet.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) THE GALLEY'S DEPTH IS NOT MEASURED. The entire interior perspective is a construction. The sheet carries PROFUNDIDAD NO MEDIDA, and anyone who knows the record will see exactly where we made something up — in the middle of the picture, not at the edge. (2) Commissioning a human hand inside a project whose whole authority is machine measurement will read to someone as a betrayal of the method. My defence is that a drawn hand is honest and a modelled hand would be a fabrication — there is no human geometry anywhere in this tree and building one for a print would be inventing a person. It will still annoy the reader who wants everything measured, and I would rather annoy them than fake a person. (3) A flat fluorescent void where Playa should be may simply read as an UNFINISHED PRINT, especially to a restaurateur who has stood in that place. That is the bet. There is no version that keeps the void and removes the risk — putting palms back in is both worse design and an F92 violation.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Proof this week for the price of an A3 print. Edition of 100 at six colours: roughly $2,000-3,000 at short-run screen rates ($45-70 per colour pull), plus a cutting die at ~$180 or hand-trimming to an r55 template, plus the illustrator's commission — budget $800-2,500 for a named hand and treat it as a CREDIT, not a cost (that is Alÿs's answer to appropriation and it is also just better work). Fluorescent screen inks on uncoated stock are standard. Machine cost: one new camera dict entry in studio.py's table beside hero34f/hero34r/side, and one ~5 minute render. No new pipeline.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Add ONE camera to studio.py's camera table, inside the galley at bay 2 looking out (it is a single dict entry next to the ones that already ship), render one frame at 1600x1100, print it A3, and draw the hands, the plate and the fluorescent void straight onto it in marker. One sheet in his hands this week showing him the only view of his own bus that nobody — no photographer, no customer, no revision — has ever made.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:polychrome-2`

### EL OTRO LADO  ·  screen 6.5/10

**REGISTER** polychrome / environmental / people — a puesto lona, six flat inks one face, one day-glo the other  
**ARTEFACT** Double-sided PVC blockout banner (lona). 4200 x 2200 mm finished, hemmed, brass grommets at 500 mm centres. Face A: the show side at exactly 1:1 in six flat saturated inks. Face B: the off side as a bare silhouette knocked out of FLUORESCENT PINK PVC STOCK — the substrate is the ink, because wide-format inkjet cannot make fluorescent. Artwork is vector, so 1:1 costs nothing in resolution.

> The bus at true size on the cheapest object in Mexican street trade — and on the back, the side nobody has ever photographed, in one screaming pink, with everything we don't know left blank.

A lona is what every puesto in Mexico hangs for shade and for signage. It is loud, cheap, grommeted, and it is the exact register this programme has never once entered.

Face A is the show side at true size, from the ONE camera that fits today — the side orthographic, ortho 5.90 against a 4065 mm body. Three apertures at 515.5 x 403.0, r 55, on centres 0.6720 / 0.0470 / -0.5980, equal because the mesh says equal and not 0.507/0.516/0.526, which is retired. Belt line at 1207 mm. Counter slab 107 mm thick, 321 mm deep, brass at 18.6 % of its edge. Wheels 2400 mm apart, tyres ⌀664.9, hubcaps ⌀274.0. At 1:1 the mural lid and the Señor Tacombi script can be replicated ABSOLUTELY, at their measured colours — this is the one artefact in the programme where SPEC 10.10's hard bar is not a compromise but a capability.

And standing at bay 2, life-size, the cook: white cap, white apron with the script, drawn by hand off ref_side.jpg. The record has spent forty revisions treating him as contamination — "a pale band turned out to be the vendor's shirt". Here he is the point. You stand next to him.

Face B is the off side. One ink. Silhouette only — no cargo doors, no windows, no panel lines, because SPEC grades that elevation E, never photographed, and every line on it would be invention. Walk round the banner and the vehicle loses everything we know.

Small along the bottom of Face A, where a spec plate goes: ALTURA TOTAL — NO MEDIDA. A 1:1 object that admits it does not know its own height.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The side orthographic camera at 1:1, and nothing else can give it. Every reference frame of this bus has perspective in it — the project spent revisions fighting exactly that, and a photograph physically cannot produce a true elevation. The mesh also gives the OUTLINE of a side nobody has photographed, which is the only honest way to draw Face B: silhouette from geometry, everything below grade S omitted. And the artwork is vector flats posterised off the pass, so 1:1 is free — this does not need the large-format pixel chain F192 says is unproven, and it touches nothing F191/F193 gate, because there is no photoreal render anywhere in it.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) THE TITLE. "El otro lado" in Mexican Spanish is the border. This bus went from Playa del Carmen to Nueva York. A blank day-glo pink side captioned NUNCA FOTOGRAFIADO is available to be read as being about people who cross and are not recorded. I am not removing that reading and I am not pretending it is not there; he owns restaurants and he should decide whether he wants it hanging in them. The defused alternative title is LADO E and it costs the piece most of its charge. (2) A lona is the cheapest thing in the field. Tacombi's identity sits at the design-led end of hospitality; a grommeted PVC banner is a deliberate downshift into the puesto register and it may simply read as CHEAP rather than as vernacular. That is the whole bet of this territory and there is no half-version of it. (3) At Nolita, where the actual 1963 bus is parked in the room, a 1:1 banner of it is redundant and could be read as diminishing the real thing. This object is for the locations that do NOT have the bus — say that when you place it, or it undercuts the only one that does.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — 1:10 test: 420 x 220 mm double-sided on the same PVC, under $30, 3 days. Full banner: 4.2 x 2.2 m double-sided blockout PVC, hemmed and eyeletted, roughly $200-400 from any trade wide-format printer, 3-5 working days. The one real constraint is fluorescent — inkjet cannot make it — which is why Face B is knocked out of fluorescent PVC stock the trade already holds, rather than printed. Vector artwork, so no resolution cost and no dependence on hq_render/stitch. A $300 banner is also the cheapest possible place to prove the large-format chain F192 says has never been proven, at a hundredth of the price of finding out on a museum print.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Render the side ortho that already works, posterise it to six flats, knock the off-side silhouette out of a fluorescent pink field, and order a 1:10 double-sided PVC test — 420 x 220 mm, hems and grommets and all, under $30. He holds a small banner, turns it over, and the bus disappears. If he turns it over twice, build it at 4.2 metres.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:polychrome-3`

## THE HAND-DRAWN LINE

### DE MANO EN MANO  ·  screen 8.33/10

**REGISTER** hand-drawn (hand-cut relief, three blocks) · polychrome, three measured inks · food-and-hand-off · people · given away  
**ARTEFACT** Three hand-cut linoleum blocks, image 258 × 202 mm on 320 × 240 sheets, printed BY HAND (baren or small press). Give-away edition on 60 gsm warm newsprint, unlimited, handed over with the check; a signed edition of 50 on 250 gsm rag. Three inks, every one off this vehicle's own measured surfaces: menu-strip yellow (172, 144, 17), body red (196, 49, 36), and a warm dark key (91, 59, 7).

> A tray of tacos crossing the brass line, cut backwards into lino by a human hand — the one image in the whole programme that contains the transaction the restaurant actually performs.

258 × 202 mm is not a poster proportion either: it is SERVING APERTURE No. 2 AT 1:2 — 515.5 × 403.0, corner r 55. The print's trim IS the hole you are served through.

The view is from INSIDE, looking out. Across the bottom, the counter slab, 107 mm thick, its brass nosing occupying 18.6 % of the outer edge — 19.9 mm of gold in elevation, the figure the source itself prints. Scalloped inside the cut edge, the white bobble fringe: 9 mm balls at 26 mm pitch, about 65 per aperture. Top left, the warm fluorescent strip tucked 20 mm under the head rail — 'a serving hatch shows you its strip light.' Behind, the plancha, 560 × 780, top at 1455 mm.

And crossing the brass line, a tray, and two hands: the cook's out of the dark, the customer's out of the light.

Cut into the block, backwards, by the same hand, in the lower margin: MANOS Y COMIDA — SIN MEDIR. NO EXISTE GEOMETRÍA HUMANA EN EL MODELO. Because there isn't: 229 objects, 366,490 faces, and not one hand, not one taco, not one plate. Forty revisions built the counter, the plancha, the hooks and the tools, and never once put anybody behind them.

In relief you cut away the light. So the carver is not tracing a contour — he is removing everything the model says is lit, which makes the occlusion pass a CUTTING MAP, not a drawing. Any wobble, any slip, any reversed letter stays in.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Two things, one of which does not exist yet and I will not pretend it does. FIRST, the frame the hands cross is true: aperture, slab thickness, brass fraction, fringe pitch and the strip light are all measured, so the print is a hand-drawn image inside a surveyed box. SECOND, the carving map. Relief printing removes the light, so the natural source is an AO/occlusion pass — and I GREPPED EVERY .py IN THIS TREE: THERE IS NO FREESTYLE, NO GREASE PENCIL, NO AO PASS, NO CRYPTOMATTE. It has to be built, and building it is shared infrastructure with the keystone line item, so this concept pays for something else too. Until then the carver works from a value-inverted 96-spp render, which costs one render. There is also no interior camera in the set (front, side, hero34f, hero34r, rear), so one perspective camera must be added — cheap, and it dodges the front/rear ortho clipping problem entirely.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) THE LARGEST DELIBERATE INVENTION IN 76 REVISIONS. A project that refused to invent a 2 mm bay taper because it sits below the measurement floor is about to invent two human hands and a tray of food. That is the risk and it is the point; the sheet confesses it in cut letters rather than in a footnote, and the confession is what makes it honest rather than sloppy. (2) IRREVERSIBLE. A wrong gouge kills a block, and three hand-registered blocks on newsprint will misalign by a millimetre or two. THE MISREGISTRATION IS KEPT. (Related to the bench's MISREGISTRO, which I am citing, not rebuilding — misalignment is a by-product here, not the subject.) (3) THE DEPTH IN THIS IMAGE IS CHOSEN, NOT MEASURED: galley Y is authored everywhere in the source, by the source's own admission. Only the aperture, slab, brass, fringe and strip light are measured, and the margin key says exactly that, item by item. (4) CULTURAL. This sits in the Posada / Taller de Gráfica Popular lineage, which is a real one with living heirs. No skull. If a Mexican printmaker cuts it, commission and credit; if not, say plainly it is an homage to the economics — one block, cheap stock, given away — and not to the iconography. (5) The food must be drawn from THIS restaurant's actual plate, in front of the cook, or it is a stock taco. THE EMBLEM, DECLARED: this is an interior view; the nose and its roundel are behind the carver's shoulder and out of frame by construction. F191 untouched.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Serving aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55 (image = that at 1:2); counter slab top 1254.0 / bottom 1147.0 = 107.0 mm thick, plan depth 321.0 mm; brass nosing CNT_NOSE_F = 0.1860 of the outer edge = 19.9 mm of gold in elevation (the source's own printed figure, corroborated 0.191 on a second frame); fringe ball r 9.0 mm at 26.0 mm pitch, inset 13.0 mm, ~65 per aperture (INFERRED — each ball is ~2 px); galley strip-light emission (1.000, 0.918, 0.790) tucked 20 mm under the head rail; plancha 560 × 780 mm, top at 1455 mm. INKS: menu-strip yellow (172, 144, 17) MEASURED, hue 48.0°, sat 0.88–0.93; body red (196, 49, 36) MEASURED; key (91, 59, 7) MEASURED MEDIAN — the (72, 46, 6) commonly quoted is CONSTRUCTED, and is not used here. UNMEASURED AND SAID SO ON THE PRINT: both hands, the food, the tray, and the galley's depth.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Cheapest of the three to prove and the most craft-expensive to finish. Lino, gouges, brayer and three inks ≈ US$120. Hand-printing 300 give-aways on newsprint is an afternoon and ≈ US$0.03 a sheet. A commissioned printmaker for three cut blocks is realistically two to three days' work — quote it. The custom inks are worth mixing properly: use the MEASURED strip-lettering median (91, 59, 7). The (72, 46, 6) in circulation is a CONSTRUCTED value, set darker because the reading is blur-limited, and it should not be printed as measured — the rev-76 programme already made that mistake once.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — THIS WEEK, ONE BLOCK, ONE INK. Cut the key block only — aperture, slab, brass band, fringe, two hands, one tray — at 1:2 on a US$9 lino sheet, working over an inverted print of an existing render. Print thirty by hand with a wooden spoon on butcher paper. Put them on the tables Friday with the check and watch whether anybody takes one home. He is holding a print in three days, and thirty customers vote on it before a second block is cut.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:hand-drawn-3`

### PÍNTALA TÚ  ·  screen 6.8/10

**REGISTER** child · hand-drawn (crayon and marker) · polychrome — but the colour arrives from the crayon box, not the press  
**ARTEFACT** A3 placemat, 420 × 297 mm, 90 gsm uncoated warm cream, printed ONE SIDE, ONE INK — a pale non-photo cyan chosen to drop out of a red-channel scan (the exact ink is UNMEASURED and must be proofed). Pads of 100. Second phase: a monthly die-cut vinyl sticker, kiss-cut, from one child's finished sheet.

> The machine draws the whole bus in an ink that disappears, and a five-year-old draws the only line anybody ever sees.

The underlay is the true orthographic side elevation at 1:12, so the WHEELBASE IS EXACTLY 200 mm ACROSS THE SHEET and the printed scale bar says so. Body 338.8 mm long, roof 165.3 mm, tyre ⌀55.4 mm, the three serving holes 43.0 × 33.6 mm with a 4.6 mm corner — three of them, equal, because the mesh says equal. The roof is not level: it falls 3.6 mm across this sheet, which is 42.6 mm of real rake over the 2400 mm wheelbase, and the mat prints that in the margin.

Everything the machine knows is in the vanishing blue. Everything it does not know is left EMPTY: the mural lid is an outlined field 169.5 × 102 mm captioned LA DE VERDAD TIENE 10 FLORES Y MEDIA · OCHO PÉTALOS CADA UNA — both measured. The three apertures are empty. The off side is not drawn at all, because nobody has ever photographed it.

Along the bottom, at 1:1, fourteen festoon bulbs at the real 28.52 mm pitch, 22 mm balls. The bus carries 118. PARA PINTAR LOS 118 NECESITAS NUEVE MANTELES. A child must come back nine times.

Then scan the finished sheet and keep the red channel. The blue underlay, the dimensions, the machine — all gone to paper white. Only the crayon survives. 366,490 faces of measurement do 100 % of the input work and appear in 0 % of the output. That is the territory, made physical, on a thing that gets salsa on it.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The underlay is not a style, it is a RULER: a rectified true-orthographic elevation at a stated scale, which no photograph of this vehicle can produce (every frame we hold is oblique or foreshortened). The side ortho camera already fits — 5.90 ortho against a 4.065 m body — so this needs no new pipeline at all. The model also supplies exactly what to LEAVE BLANK: it knows the mural lid's footprint (2034 × 1223.7 mm) without knowing what to draw in it, and it knows the off side is grade E, never photographed, so that side is simply absent from the sheet.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — FOUR, none of them sanded. (1) You hand the brand's only line to a five-year-old and you cannot take it back — sooner or later a mat comes back with something obscene on it, and somebody at the restaurant has to be the censor. Printing a child's drawing as saleable merchandise also needs a parental release; that is an operational cost, not an aesthetic one. (2) IT DOES NOT SATISFY F18 AND MUST NOT BE SOLD AS IF IT DOES. The owner LOCKED that style himself: 'cartoon with rendered depth — vector line and flat colour, shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset.' A wax crayon has no sampled occlusion. If he adopts this as the sticker he is overruling his own rev-43 lock, and he should be told that in the same breath he is shown it, not discover it later. (3) SPEC §10.10, ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ARTWORK: this sheet reproduces none of the mural, paisley, script or menu strips. Rule-34 defence, made explicitly: the bar's object is the MODEL; this is a derivative print, and it OMITS the artwork rather than re-drawing it, leaving its measured footprint empty. (4) The drop-out is UNMEASURED — a phone camera under restaurant light may not lose the cyan cleanly. Proof it before printing 2,000. THE EMBLEM, DECLARED: this is a side elevation and the VW roundel sits on the nose, so it is out of frame by geometry, not by cowardice. F191's hold is untouched.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Body length 4065 mm; wheelbase 2400 mm (= exactly 200 mm at 1:12); roof at rear axle 1983.5 mm; tyre ⌀664.9 mm; serving apertures 515.5 × 403.0 mm, corner r 55 mm, THREE, EQUAL; rake 17.75 mm/m = 42.6 mm over the wheelbase; festoon pitch 28.52 mm and BULB_R 0.0110 (22 mm balls); 118 bulbs BUILT (not the 115 in circulation, which is a photograph-side inference about a 3.50 m rail); mural lid opening 2034 mm × 1223.7 mm DERIVED; 10 full flower heads + 1 half; 8 petals per rosette (k=8 power 42.6 against k=12 at 6.3); off side grade E, never photographed. Scale reductions (÷12) are DERIVED by division and labelled as such. NOT USED: the retracted pillar asymmetry; the retired bay taper.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Trivial. 100 A3 digital one-ink sheets ≈ US$40; at 2,000 offset, one ink on cheap uncoated, ≈ US$0.06 a sheet. A cup of crayons is US$8. The whole first run is under US$60 and needs no code, no render queue and no new camera. The sticker phase is a standard kiss-cut vinyl run, ~US$0.35 each at 500.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — THIS WEEK, ZERO CODE. Take one existing side render, throw a levels curve at it until it is pale cyan, laser-print 20 copies on the cheapest cream A3 the copy shop has, and put them under trays on Saturday with a cup of crayons. Photograph the returns. He is holding twenty children's drawings of his own bus by Monday, and the ones that come back tell you whether to build the proper 1:12 underlay at all.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:hand-drawn-1`

### EL PICADO  ·  screen 6.33/10

**REGISTER** hand-drawn (one-shot enamel, brush and mahl stick) · three-dimensional object · people — a named rotulista is the author, credited in his own hand  
**ARTEFACT** TWO OBJECTS, HUNG AS A PAIR. (a) THE PATTERN: 1400 × 640 mm sheet of 80 gsm brown kraft, the word 'Señor Tacombi' at 1:1, perforated by hand with a pounce wheel (hole pitch UNMEASURED — it is whatever wheel the painter owns), charcoal-blackened from use, hung with its tape and thumb-marks intact. (b) THE PANEL: 1278 × 544.3 mm, 1.2 mm steel folded 15 mm on all four edges, sprayed body red (196, 49, 36), the script painted by hand in one-shot enamel, BRIGHT SILVER, tarnish off.

> The measured word, pierced into brown paper so a human hand could carry it onto steel — the machine's line and the hand's line hung side by side, and the difference between them printed underneath.

1278 × 544.3 mm is not a poster size. It is the exact panel the real script occupies on the flank — x +784 to −494, z 445.3 to 989.6 — so the object is a 1:1 slice of the bus, cut out and hung on a wall. THE WORD, ACTUAL SIZE.

The transfer is the point. A signwriter does not trace; he pounces. The full-size drawing is pierced, charcoal is beaten through the holes, and a field of dots appears on the ground for the brush to chase. That perforated drawing is called a CARTOON — the same word the owner locked into F18 — and in Spanish the operation is *picado*, pierced. So the pattern is the machine's exact contour, and the panel is what a hand did with it, and neither is the piece: THE PAIR IS THE PIECE.

The silver is ruled, not chosen: 'Bright silver, same as Tacombi', rev 62, tarnish shipped OFF. The measured clean-silver chromaticity is (127.4, 124.9, 130.0), luminance 0.66 linear. The painter matches that, not a swatch book.

Under the pair, an A4 sheet: the painted panel photographed square-on and registered against the model's own outline, with the departure printed in millimetres. THAT FIGURE DOES NOT EXIST YET AND THE SHEET SAYS SO — it cannot exist until the panel is painted. The instrument exists (`compare_script.py`) and carries its own confessed blind spot in the source: it scores the TEXTURE against the reference mask and never looks at the panel the texture lands on.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The pounce pattern has to be 1:1 AND TRUE, and the flank is not flat — the V-swage and the belt line run under those letters, so a photograph of the word is both foreshortened and swaged. `conform_panel_true()` already lays that panel onto the real skin; unrolling it gives the letterforms as they would be if the steel were flattened, which is the only correct thing to pierce. No photograph in the reference set can do this. The model also carries the cautionary fact printed on the pair's caption card: the panel was SQUASHED 15.8 % VERTICALLY for two revisions and a whole-lockup IoU of 0.942 could not see it. The machine was wrong, and green.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — IT DEPICTS NEITHER FOOD NOR A PERSON, AND I AM NOT PRETENDING OTHERWISE — its human content is a human's hand-work and a credited human author, which is not the same thing as the hand-off the critic asked for. That job is DE MANO EN MANO's. Beyond that: (1) THE OBJECT'S ENTIRE CONTENT IS A MEASUREMENT BEING OFF ON PURPOSE. This project's standing bar is 'any single measurement off is unacceptable.' Here the departure is the subject. He may read it as the project surrendering, and that reading has to be put to him in the ask, not discovered on the wall. (2) SPEC §10.10 / F94 lock ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ARTWORK, and this hand-repaints the flank script. Rule-34 defence, stated plainly: the bar's object is the MODEL, and this changes nothing in the model — but a viewer can reasonably read a hand-repainted brand word as defacement, and the caption card must own that rather than hide it. (3) APPROPRIATION. Rotulismo is a living, declining trade with living practitioners. The only honest answer is Alÿs's: COMMISSION, DO NOT IMITATE, credit by name and pay a real fee. If the budget will not carry a commission, do not make this object in-house as pastiche. (4) It is 1.28 m of painted steel. If he hates it, it still exists and somebody has to store it. THE EMBLEM, DECLARED: the roundel is on the nose, 1345 mm forward of this panel's leading edge (2129 − 784), so it is out of frame by measured geometry. F191 is untouched.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Script panel 1278.0 × 544.3 mm (x +784 to −494, z 445.3 to 989.6), with TWO independent derivations of the height agreeing — 0.5424 m from the texture aspect, 0.5440 ± 0.008 m photographed — and width photographed 1.2784 ± 0.012 against 1.278 built, −0.4 mm; body RED linear (0.5520, 0.0294, 0.0176) = sRGB (196, 49, 36), hue 5.0°, sat 0.816; measured clean-silver chromaticity (127.4, 124.9, 130.0) carried at SILVER_Y 0.66 linear; SENOR_TARNISH = 0.0 by owner ruling rev 62; roundel centre x +2129 mm, so 1345 mm forward of the panel edge. UNMEASURED and printed as such: pounce hole pitch; the hand-vs-machine departure, which cannot exist until the panel is painted. NOTE ON THE WORD: 'picado' here is the signwriter's pierced cartoon. It is NOT a claim that papel picado is on the vehicle — SPEC refuses that (55.1 % foliage, 13.4 % crimson heads, no flag silhouettes) and the owner has deferred the question to himself. This does not re-put it.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The pattern is US$20 of kraft, tape and a haberdashery tracing wheel. The panel is real money: 1.28 m² of 1.2 mm steel, folded and sprayed, ≈ US$150–250, plus a rotulista's day and his one-shot enamel — UNMEASURED here; budget a day rate plus materials and a written credit. Total realistically US$600–1,200 for one object. Feasible in two weeks, most of it lead time on the steel. The registration sheet is a 20-minute job with an existing script once the panel is photographed square-on.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — THIS WEEK, UNDER US$20, AND IT DECIDES THE COMMISSION. Tile-print `tex/senor.png` (4096 × 1738, already on disk) at 1:1 across A3 sheets, tape them into one 1278 mm sheet, and pierce the letterforms by hand with a US$6 sewing tracing wheel. Beat charcoal through it onto a sheet of card. Hand HIM the pounced card: his own word, made of dots, by a hand, actual size. If the dots do not move him, do not spend US$1,000 on the steel.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — False  ·  **id** `dream:hand-drawn-2`
