## THREE-DIMENSIONAL OBJECTS

### LA TAPA  ·  screen 8.7/10

**REGISTER** food-and-hand-off / service ware / three-dimensional / polychrome (two enamels)  
**ARTEFACT** A two-piece enamelled pressed-steel service set, made as Mexican peltre. EL PLATÓN: bowl, outside Ø274.0 mm, drawn depth 70.5 mm, lip radius 137.0 mm — the hubcap's own revolve profile, inverted. LA CHAPA: dish, outside Ø86.9 mm, blank floor. Two enamel colours (body red #C43124, rim cream #CED0C8), one rolled steel rim, no print, no decal, no logo. Base of each piece stamped incuse in the steel.

> The hubcap is already a bowl. Turn it over, enamel it, and hand the food across the counter in the wheel.

Take `_cap_prof()` — twenty-one (y, r) pairs, the hubcap's own revolve, the same list the mesh is built from — and invert it. You have a vessel 274.0 mm across and 70.5 mm deep. That is not a bus-shaped novelty; that is a serving bowl in exactly the proportion a serving bowl wants to be, and it happens to be a wheel.

The colours are the vehicle's, not a palette: the dome is the body red (196, 49, 36), the flange is the cream of the painted rim ring (206, 208, 200). The retired-readings list already says it — THE WHITE RING IS THE RIM, NOT A WHITEWALL — and this object is the sentence made in steel.

The centre dish is Ø86.9 mm and it is BLANK. That is deliberate and it is the honest declaration critique 8 asks for. STATE.md prints it in its own voice: the hubcap badge is SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY, CAP_EMBLEM_WFRAC has never been compared to a frame. We do not know what is on it. So nothing is on it, and the base says Ø86.9 · SIN MEDIR.

On the underside, five dimples at radius 111.5 mm, cutter radius 23.5 mm — the wheel disc's five vents, which sit under the cap where no photograph we hold can see them (5-fold harmonic A5 0.0024–0.0571, SNR 0.5–3.3, against the render's 0.214 at SNR 8–11). Present, invisible, on the face you don't eat off. Stamped: CINCO RESPIRADEROS · NINGUNA FOTOGRAFÍA LOS VE.

This is the item that gets a person and a taco into the programme, because the taco lands in it.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The model gives the DIE SECTION. Tooling for a drawn bowl needs a profile, and a photograph cannot give one — depth is the thing a frame does not hold. `_cap_prof()` goes straight to a CNC'd draw punch, and CAP_LIP_R 137.0 mm is derived from the list rather than typed, so the tool and the vehicle cannot drift apart. The DIAMETER is measured and strong (hubcap D / tyre D 0.4134 photographed vs 0.4211 built, 302-ray circle fit, sd 0.79 px). The 70.5 mm DEPTH is AUTHORED and UNMEASURED — the source says so verbatim: 'validated against a ratio of two DIAMETERS, and it never bore on depth.' That goes on the base too: PROF. AUTORIZADO · PROFUNDIDAD SIN MEDIR.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — Four, none of them sanded. (1) Enamel chips, and a chipped rim is bare steel that rusts — which is romantic in a photograph and a conversation with a health inspector in a kitchen. It also contradicts his own rev-61 ruling, 'I want this 3d model to look like new'; an object designed to patina is on the wrong side of that and he has to be the one who decides. (2) The blank centre reads as an unfinished sample to ninety-nine guests out of a hundred; the caption that redeems it is on the base, where nobody looks. If he hates it, the whole honesty argument dies in one sentence and the dish becomes a plain salsa dish. (3) Wheel-shaped tableware is one step from diner kitsch and two steps from a Cars merchandise aisle. The only defence is the exactness — if a supplier rounds 274.0 to 'about 27 cm' and eyeballs the red, the object is worthless and should be killed rather than shipped. (4) It goes into service, so it will be dropped, stacked and washed 400 times; peltre survives that, which is why it is peltre and not ceramic.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Genuinely cheap for what it is. Draw die + trim die for the 274 mm bowl and a small die for the 86.9 dish: trade estimate US$3–6k tooling, MOQ 500–1,000, roughly US$6–11 a piece landed. THESE ARE TRADE ESTIMATES, NOT MEASUREMENTS — get one real quote from a Mexican enamelware works before any of them is repeated. Supplier UNVERIFIED. Enamel colour matching to a measured sRGB triple is the one technical risk: frit colour is specified by the enameller's own standards, not by hex, so budget a strike-off round and approve it against a printed swatch of #C43124 in daylight. No trademark exposure anywhere on the object — no roundel, no wordmark, nothing but two colours and a diameter.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — This week, no tooling at all: 3D-print the 274.0 mm bowl and the 86.9 mm dish overnight from the revolve profile, spray them in the two measured colours, put a taco in the bowl and a spoon of salsa in the dish, and photograph it on a counter. He is a restaurateur; that is the only presentation format he needs. Second-cheapest alternative if the print reads too plasticky: a manual metal spinner can spin one 274.0 mm aluminium bowl off a wooden chuck cut on a desktop router in an afternoon — one raw, unpainted, heavy bowl in his hand, with the twenty-one-point profile taped to the back.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:dimensional-1`

### DALE  ·  screen 8.4/10

**REGISTER** child / joy / hand-made (not a machine trace) / polychrome, seven measured papers / destruction  
**ARTEFACT** A piñata of the bus at 1:4 — 1016 × 437.5 × 496 mm (body length 4065, width 1750, roof at rear axle 1983.5, all divided by four). Armature: eleven transverse ribs plus two longitudinal spines, CNC-cut in 3 mm greyboard as TRUE CROSS-SECTIONS of the mesh at 100 mm stations. Core: a real barro olla, ~180 mm, set where the engine was. Skin: newspaper and engrudo, then papel de China fringe cut with fierritos in seven measured colours. Roof-edge fringe cut at 7.13 mm pitch, 118 tabs. Fill: ~900 g dulces. Commissioned from a Puebla or Mexico City taller, not imitated in-house.

> Seventy-six revisions of measurement, built at 1:4 in cut paper and clay, and destroyed by a seven-year-old on a Saturday.

THE CANDY GOES WHERE THE ENGINE WAS. Engine: none. Transmission: sold. Dulces: 900 g. That is the concept and it is also literally true of the vehicle.

The skin is seven papers and every one of them is measured off the bus: the mural's ground #371A13, its red #9F2D16, its orange #A34215, its gold #AC7718, its pale petal-core #B8964C, the menu-strip yellow #AC9011, the body red #C43124, on a cream #CED0C8. That is the answer to 'eleven concepts, one aesthetic, two inks' — not a decision to be more colourful, but seven pigments that were already read off the object.

The fringe along the roof edge is cut at 118 tabs, 7.13 mm apart, because the drip rail carries 118 lamps at a realised 28.52 mm and 1:4 is 1:4. (I watched that print this session: BULB_X0 −1.6368, BULB_X1 1.7000, pitch 0.0286, 117 segments, 118 balls. The '115 festoon lamps' in circulation is an FFT inference about a 3.50 m rail, not this model. Correct it.)

The far flank is the problem and therefore the best part. SPEC grades the off side E — NEVER PHOTOGRAPHED. A piñata spins on its rope, so the side nobody has ever seen will face the room half the time. Build it as the expert inference — twin cargo doors, three windows — and hang a tag on the rope: LADO IZQUIERDO · NUNCA FOTOGRAFIADO. The programme's honesty, rotating slowly above a party.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The rib set, and nothing else. Eleven true cross-sections at 100 mm stations in the model's own X is a slicing operation, and slicing needs a CLOSED body: STATE.md prints non-manifold edges (body) = 0, over 229 meshes and 366,490 faces. No photograph gives a cross-section; a photogrammetry scan gives a noisy shell that slices into ragged rubbish. This is the single cheapest, most direct use of the fact that the source is dimensional, and it costs one script and one laser-cutter sheet. Everything downstream — paper, paste, clay — is a craft the model has no business touching.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — Real and I am not softening any of it. (1) It cannot be kept. F18's bar in his own words is 'spark joy and be something families keep' — this object sparks joy and is then beaten to pieces. It half-answers his oldest brief and openly fails the other half. If he wants the keepsake, that is EL TROQUEL, not this. (2) One photograph of a child clubbing a Tacombi bus, cropped and reposted without the context, is a bad afternoon for whoever runs the account. In Mexico this reads as a birthday; out of context it reads as something else, and there is no mitigation, only a decision. (3) The clay olla shatters. That is what makes the crack sound right and it is also what an insurer will ask about. Named, not solved. (4) Papel picado: SPEC says the vehicle carries none — the band is a continuous flowering mass, 55.1 % foliage, 13.4 % crimson heads, 5.5 % cream florets — and HE has reserved that question for himself ('Leave it open, I'll decide when the sticker is actually being built'). This concept USES the cut-tissue craft and ASSERTS NOTHING about the vehicle. That distinction goes on the tag. Do not re-put the question. (5) Rule-34 defence, made rather than assumed: SPEC 10.10's absolute-artwork-replication bar governs THE MODEL. Cut tissue at 1:4 cannot replicate the mural and does not claim to — it is a derivative object, stated as one.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The cheapest concept in the set by an order of magnitude. One laser sheet of 3 mm greyboard, an olla, newsprint, flour paste, seven colours of papel de China, and a commission fee. Trade estimate US$250–600 for the first one from a taller, less if a piñatería is already making three a week — UNVERIFIED, get a quote. No tooling, no MOQ, no supply chain, no trademark exposure. Lead time is days, not months. Commission the practitioner rather than imitate the practice (Alÿs's answer, and it is also simply better work): the credit line reads as a collaboration instead of an appropriation, and it costs the same.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Cut all eleven ribs and the two spines on one laser sheet — three hours of file prep, twenty minutes of cutting — and slot them together dry on the bar. No paper, no paste, no candy. It is a 1016 mm skeleton of his bus standing up in space, and it is the FIRST TIME IN SEVENTY-SIX REVISIONS THAT THE OBJECT HAS EXISTED PHYSICALLY AT ALL. If he only ever sees one thing from this whole territory, make it that. Cost: a sheet of board.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:dimensional-2`

### EL TROQUEL  ·  screen 8.0/10

**REGISTER** beautiful-not-rigorous / precious and perishable off one tool / three-dimensional / food  
**ARTEFACT** ONE die, two objects. The die: a Ø55 mm circular bas-relief, cut on a 4-axis CNC in tool steel (for striking) and in food-grade polycarbonate (for moulding), from a 16-bit Z pass rendered through the existing side orthographic camera. Object A: a struck bronze medal, Ø55 mm, ~4 mm thick, ~65 g, obverse the bus in relief, reverse an incuse inscription, edge lettered. Object B: a tempered dark chocolate tablet, Ø55 mm, ~28 g, off the same relief, given with the check.

> A depth pass IS a bas-relief — the same mathematical object — so cut one die and let it make the thing that lasts forever and the thing that is eaten in a minute.

Nothing in the 78 has used the fact that the model is a solid. This does, at the most literal level available: a Z buffer is a single-valued height field, a bas-relief is a single-valued height field, and there is no translation step between them. Render the side ortho, invert, cut.

The obverse is the show side at 1:81.3 (4065 mm body across a 50.0 mm relief field). It is the only view the machine can honestly give: the side orthographic camera FITS (ortho_scale 5.90 from y = +26.0 m, target z 1.52 — it clears the raised mural lid at bbox top 3.132), while front and rear ortho are set to 3.55 and CLIP. One face, and the reason is stated.

The reverse is where this stops being decoration. A medal's native content is a date and a fact, and this project has ninety of them:

COMBI · 1963 / COMPRADA VERDE EN MÉXICO, D.F. · $3,000 / LE FALTABAN TRES VENTANAS / ABIERTA 6·II·2006 / MOTOR: NINGUNO · TRANSMISIÓN: VENDIDA

and around the edge, incuse:

LARGO 4065 · ANCHO 1750 · ALTURA: SIN MEDIR

STATE.md: 'the real vehicle's absolute roof height is OPEN and UNMEASURED.' A tolerance has never been printed on a toy box. Print it on the edge of a medal instead.

The chocolate carries the same relief and the same three lines on its wrapper. A measurement that cannot be recovered from what we hold is well served by an object that melts.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — This is the strongest model dependency of my three and it is not decorative. A relief needs DEPTH, single-valued, clean, at a resolution you choose. A photograph has none. Photogrammetry gives noise that a CNC faithfully reproduces as noise. The mesh is closed (0 non-manifold edges) and overwhelmingly quads (229,489 quad, 1,040 tri), so the height field comes out smooth enough to cut without hand-sculpting. ⚠ IT DOES NOT EXIST YET AND I CHECKED: `studio.py` sets no `use_pass_z`, there is no normal or AO pass, and there is no mesh export path at all. Building it is one line plus a 32-bit EXR output — an afternoon — and it is the SAME infrastructure the line-art keystone needs, so it is not a detour.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) THE PRIMARY RISK IS TONE AND IT IS NOT FIXABLE BY CRAFT. A struck bronze medal of your own delivery van is either the most charming object in the programme or it is insufferable self-regard, and there is no middle. A medal is the language of mints, institutions and armies. A taqueria issuing one is a joke that only works told absolutely straight — if the inscription winks even once, it dies. He is entitled to find it pompous and he must see it before anything is tooled. (2) THE DEPTH EXAGGERATION IS INVENTED. A relief compresses ~2 m into ~1.2 mm, and how deep the three serving holes read is the medal's most sculptural feature — but `BAY_DEPTH = 0.42` is flagged in source as NOT MEASURED. So the single most expressive number on the object is a pose choice, exactly the class of figure this project refuses to publish. It cannot be avoided in a relief. It gets stamped, not hidden: PROFUNDIDAD EXAGERADA · CONSTANTE DECLARADA. (3) One face only, because two ortho cameras clip and the off side is grade E. That is a machine limitation being dressed as a design decision, and saying so is the only honest move. (4) The artwork survives only as FORM, not paint — at 1:81.3 the 'Señor Tacombi' script is 15.7 mm long and will not be legible. Under SPEC 10.10 that is a derivative object, not the model; the rule-34 defence is made here rather than assumed. (5) F92 note, made rather than skipped: this concept does NOT touch the Playa hero, does not re-propose `playa_env.py`, and does not act on a deprioritised deliverable. It does borrow the one sentence in the record worth borrowing — 'the emotional bar that sits ABOVE clinical accuracy' — because a medal is an emotional object or it is nothing.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Split the two hard. THE CHOCOLATE IS CHEAP AND SHIPS FIRST: a CNC'd polycarbonate mould of 21 or 24 cavities is a trade estimate of US$400–900, a chocolatier tempering to a supplied mould is routine, and the unit is pennies plus wrapper. THE MEDAL IS A YEAR-TWO OBJECT: reduction-cut or direct-CNC hub, hardened die, a small striking house, trade estimate US$2.5–5k tooling with an MOQ around 200–500, roughly US$18–35 landed in bronze. ALL FIGURES ARE TRADE ESTIMATES, NOT MEASUREMENTS — one real quote before any of them is repeated. Two supply chains, two MOQs, and that is a genuine weakness of pairing them; the answer is that they share the one expensive artefact, the relief master, and nothing else. No trademark exposure: the VW roundel is 3.4 mm at this scale and illegible, and it is composed out on purpose — DECLARED, per critique 8, not silently routed around.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Do not touch metal in week one. Enable the Z pass on the existing side ortho camera, render it 16-bit, invert it, and 3D-print the Ø55 mm relief at 0.05 mm layers overnight — a grey plastic medal in his hand inside twenty-four hours. Then, for the showpiece, cut the SAME height field at Ø300 mm in MDF or plaster on a desktop router: a big pale roundel of the bus in relief that reads across a room, costs a US$40 blank plus an afternoon, and is also the literal pattern every later object comes from. Show him the plaster first and the 55 mm print in his palm second; the size difference is the pitch.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:dimensional-3`

## ENVIRONMENTAL / SIGNAGE / WAYFINDING

### SIGUE LA TIRA  ·  screen 7.67/10

**REGISTER** environmental and polychrome and hand-drawn — five hand-mixed enamels, brush-cut by a commissioned rotulista. Wayfinding that is also the menu.  
**ARTEFACT** A painted band, industrial enamel on plaster, running the full length of the room at the height of the bus's own menu strip. It leaves the vehicle at the lid edge, crosses the wall as a plain unlettered field of menu-strip yellow for most of its run, and fills with words only where it reaches the ordering point. Five inks, all from the vehicle: STRIP yellow (172,144,17), lettering ink, and the three vignette colours — PINK (206,118,124) for meat and shrimp, GREEN (96,128,54) for garnish, CREAMV (232,224,198) for plate and glass. Words set on the shallow upward arch the bus itself uses, in the read sequence and at the v-fractions the record holds: GOURMET · TACOS · & · TORTAS · [torta vignette] · FRESH · [juice vignette] · JUICES · CEVICHE / TOSTADAS · [tostada] · SHRIMP · & FISH · [prawn] · TACOS y. Painted by a named rotulista, credited on the wall in 25 mm caps.

> The menu is already painted on the bus. Let a sign painter carry it off the vehicle and along the wall, and let it stop where the reading stopped.

This is the strongest un-used asset in the repository: the bus's own menu, read at 22× and stored as fractions, in three registers across three cities, and never once drawn.

Two things make it a piece of work rather than a decal.

First, the gap. The top strip has 695 px hidden behind palm fronds, and the record's own finding is that the gap accommodates 13.0 ± 0.6 characters — and GOURMET TACOS is 13. The record refuses to call that a reading; it calls it a CONSISTENCY. So the band paints a thirteen-character gap and leaves it EMPTY, hairline bracket beneath, caption in 12 mm caps: 13.0 ± 0.6 CARACTERES · CONSISTENTE, NO LEÍDO. A hole in the restaurant's own menu that nobody may fill. That is the uncertainty made into a room-scale object instead of a tint on a drawing.

Second, the three lines at the foot. At v 0.935–0.966 the bus carries three small lines that resolve to nothing — '?ANO M?', '??O?A?', 'Torta?'. They are painted as exactly what they are: illegible small type, brush-made, unreadable. Brass plaque beneath, 40 × 90 mm: NO SE PUDO LEER · 3 LÍNEAS.

And the ink is captioned honestly, because that is the house style: TINTA (72,46,6) — CONSTRUIDA. MEDIDA (91,59,7). The core was set darker because the measurement is blur-limited. Printed on the wall, 12 mm, at the end of the band.

This is not ROTULISTA. That is an animation of the bare red flank painting itself. This is a wall, a person, and a day's work in enamel.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Less than the other two, and I will not fake it. What the model contributes is scale-honesty: the strip's content is stored board-normalised in (u, v) with separate u and v radii precisely because the board's own aspect is only known to ±0.09, so it scales onto a twelve-metre wall without the ellipse and arch errors a photographic trace would carry. It also gives the true band height and the arch construction, and the vehicle-frame heights so the band leaves the bus at the right place rather than a place that looks right. And it gives one refusal, which matters more: the three pillar menu cards are 94.8 × 338.3 mm with words 1.5 px tall, NOT MEASURABLE. So where the band reaches the cards it paints only the band structure the material resolves — a thin red rule, a dark script logo in the top 12 %, a red band, warm rows, a red band across the bottom 8 % — and no invented words. A photograph of the bus would have tempted someone to make them up.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) Appropriation. Rotulismo is a living declining trade with living practitioners. The answer is a commission at a real day rate with a name on the wall, not a designer imitating brush-cut letters — and if the budget only stretches to the imitation, do not make this. (2) The artwork bar. SPEC 10.10 and F94: 'ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ALL ARTWORK', his words, 'a hard bar'. Rule 34's object test carries this — the bar's object is the MODEL, and this wall is a derivative made by a third party in a room. But the defence is not free, because a rotulista extending the bus's own strip puts NEW WORDS IN THE BUS'S VOICE, which is the exact thing the bar exists to stop. Ask him before a brush is loaded. Do not proceed on my argument alone. (3) It can beat the bus. Twelve metres of yellow against 4.065 m of red is not a fair fight; the plain unlettered run is a mitigation, not a guarantee. (4) It assumes a room with the bus in it. Without the vehicle to leave, the band has nothing to continue from and degrades into a stripe. Say that on the drawing. (5) The VW roundel is absent here too — a menu strip has no nose. F191 stays shut. DECLARED. (6) The empty thirteen-character gap will be filled in by a manager within a year unless it is protected in writing.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Menu-strip yellow (172,144,17), hue 48.0°, saturation 0.88–0.93, ink excluded — MEASURED. Lettering ink (72,46,6) — CONSTRUCTED, measured median (91,59,7), blur-limited; captioned as such on the wall. PINK (206,118,124), GREEN (96,128,54), CREAMV (232,224,198) — all MEASURED. Cap height 64 px = 0.46 of the strip height. The side-strip v-fractions in full, left and right identical: GOURMET 0.001–0.041, bread-roll vignette 0.047–0.096, TACOS 0.105–0.145, & 0.155–0.191, TORTAS 0.204–0.244, torta vignette 0.260–0.337, FRESH 0.379–0.416, juice vignette 0.453–0.511, JUICES 0.523–0.555, CEVICHE / TOSTADAS 0.590–0.637, tostada vignette 0.677–0.703, SHRIMP 0.731–0.755, & FISH 0.759–0.783, shrimp vignette 0.814–0.868, TACOS 0.894–0.918, y 0.925–0.933, three lines 0.935–0.966 NOT LEGIBLE. Top strip: 695 px occluded, gap = 13.0 ± 0.6 characters, a CONSISTENCY not a reading. Menu cards 94.8 × 338.3 mm; their words 1.5 px, NOT MEASURABLE. Board aspect honest bar ±0.09. The wall's own length is UNMEASURED — nobody in this project has seen the room.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — One metre of test: about $150 including a sign painter's hour and materials. A full wall: a rotulista's multi-day commission plus paint — real money, real scheduling, and a wall that must be prepared. UNMEASURED and location-dependent; get a quote against a measured wall length, which nobody in this project has. The whole thing is reversible with a roller, which is the argument for trying it in one location before four.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — One metre, this week. A 1000 × 260 mm offcut of MDF rolled in the measured yellow (172,144,17), with the single word TORTAS brushed on the bus's own shallow arch by an actual sign painter — one hour, about $150. Carry it into Nolita, prop it against the bus so the painted yellow and the vehicle's yellow are in one frame, and take one photograph. If the two yellows sit together the whole wall is approved by that photograph; if they fight, the concept dies for $150 and an hour, which is the cheapest kill available anywhere in this programme.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:environmental-3`

### UN HUECO  ·  screen 7.5/10

**REGISTER** environmental — and three-dimensional, food-and-hand-off, and child. Not a mark, not line-work: a piece of joinery you are served through.  
**ARTEFACT** A working service hatch, built at 1:1 from the model, installed in the kitchen wall of every Tacombi that does not have the bus. Face: 3 mm folded steel, powder-coated to CREAM (206,208,200), one colour. Aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm, corner radius 55, fibre-laser cut from the model's own outline. Counter: a 107.0 mm solid slab, 321.0 mm deep, with a brass nosing plate wrapping 18.6 % of its outer edge. A white bobble fringe on a tape scalloped inside the cut — 64 balls, r 9 mm, 26 mm pitch. A warm-white LED strip tucked 20 mm under the head rail, gelled to (1.000, 0.918, 0.790). The two-tone break painted 100.0 mm below the sill. Then the same rounded rectangle becomes every opening in the room: the till window, the bathroom vision panel, the mirror, the menu frame. One module, no drawing.

> Don't put a picture of the bus on the wall. Cut its hole in the wall and hand food through it.

Nolita already has the bus. DC and Miami have a logo. The transferable object is not the vehicle's image — it is its one repeated hole, and the thing that happens through it.

So: build the hole. 515.5 × 403.0, r 55, at the sill height the model holds. Three of them if the wall allows, and here is the fact that makes it architecture rather than decoration — the three holes are exactly equal, but the vehicle is raked 17.75 mm/m nose-down, so their sills sit at 1312.1 / 1323.2 / 1334.7 mm above ground. A 22.5 mm fall across the row. Build them level and you have built a diagram. Build them falling and the room inherits the bus's stance, and nobody will know why the wall feels alive.

The counter does the same: the slab is flat in the vehicle's own frame, so above ground it climbs 55.1 mm from 1189.8 mm at the nose end to 1244.9 mm at the tail. A counter that is not level, on purpose, for a reason you can print.

Then the second hole. Same 515.5 × 403.0, sill at 700 mm — UNMEASURED, no bus ever had it. It exists because there is a child in ref_nolita_front34.jpg in a magenta puffer coat, bumper-height, looking up at a counter she cannot see over, and forty revisions have treated her as noise on a nose measurement. F18's audience, answered in steel instead of vinyl.

This contradicts TRES HUECOS deliberately. That deletes the vehicle to make a mark. This keeps the one part of the vehicle that does the work, and puts a cook behind it.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Everything. This is a 1:1 fabrication drawing and it cannot come from a photograph. The model gives the true outline (r 55 blended, not a guessed radius), the sill and head above ground per station including the rake, the 107 mm slab section, the 321 mm plan depth, the 18.6 % brass fraction, the fringe pitch, the strip light's position and emission colour, and the 100.0 mm belt offset. The side orthographic camera already fits the vehicle (4.0563 m ortho against a 4.065 m body), so the cut path exports as a true elevation, not a traced perspective. A tape measure in the Nolita room would give you none of the above-ground heights, because the vehicle is raked and the tape does not know it.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — Four, none of them sanded. (1) A service counter at 1189.8–1244.9 mm above the floor is far above the 34 in / 864 mm figure commonly cited for an accessible service counter in the US — that figure is EXTERNAL and secondary, VERIFY WITH THEIR ARCHITECT, it is not a machine number. Either you drop a section and break the 1:1 truth, or the piece fails inspection. Choose on the drawing, in writing. (2) Multiplying a unique object cheapens it. The Nolita bus is one bus; a wall with its hole in it, in four cities, could turn the bus into a template and the brand into a chain that quotes itself. (3) The child's hole is an invention, in a programme whose entire authority is that it invents nothing. It is marked DECLARED on the drawing and it will still be the thing a critic points at. (4) The VW roundel is not in this artefact — a hatch has no nose. F191 stays shut when this ships. DECLARED here, not routed around silently.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm, corner radius 55, three, equal (t1_shell BAY_W / Z_SILL / Z_HEAD / BAY_R). Sill above ground per bay, computed live this session from drop(x) = 0.047925 + 0.017750x at BAY_CX (+0.6720, +0.0470, −0.5980): 1312.1 / 1323.2 / 1334.7 mm, spread 22.5 mm; heads 1715.1 / 1726.2 / 1737.7. Counter slab 107.0 mm thick, 321.0 mm deep, 3.106 m long, top above ground 1189.8 mm front to 1244.9 mm tail, rise 55.1 mm — all watched printing. Brass nosing 18.6 % of the slab's outer edge (CNT_NOSE_F 0.1860, measured 4.52 ± 0.23 px, corroborated 0.191 on a second frame). Belt break exactly 100.0 mm below the sill at every station. Rake 17.75 mm/m. Cream (206,208,200). Strip-light emission (1.000, 0.918, 0.790). Fringe r 9 mm at 26 mm pitch — the source says 65 balls per aperture, the inset outline computes 63.9; call it 64 and mark the ball geometry INFERRED, each ball is ~2 px in the reference. Child's hole sill 700 mm: UNMEASURED, DECLARED.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Prototype under $150 and one afternoon. A production hatch — folded steel, brass nosing, solid slab, integrated strip — I would budget $3–6k per opening installed, but that is a guess and must be replaced by a real fabricator quote before it is printed anywhere. The laser file is free: it is an export of a closed path the model already holds. The hard part is not fabrication, it is item (1) of the risk.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — One hole, on the pass, this week. A 900 × 750 mm sheet of 12 mm MDF, sprayed cream, jigsaw- or laser-cut to the model's exported 515.5 × 403.0 r55 outline; 64 white pom-poms on a tape stapled 13 mm inside the cut; a 300 mm warm LED strip taped behind the head rail. Stand it on the pass in Nolita. Have a cook hand a taco through it to the person standing there. Photograph that, once. Then, holding the photograph, ask him the one question nobody has asked: his deferral trigger on the die-cut sticker is 'build it after the model is done' — is a hatch a person can be served through enough of 'done' to start the sticker? That is finding 12, asked with an object in his hand instead of a form.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:environmental-1`

### 3.95 : 1  ·  screen 5.57/10

**REGISTER** environmental — light, at architectural scale. The only concept in the programme with no ink at all.  
**ARTEFACT** A lighting scheme and one fixture, not a graphic. A single large low LATERAL source — a 4.2 m linear softbox aperture running one wall, its centre at roughly the vehicle's belt height and its face turned across the room, not down — beneath a matte, deliberately absorbing ceiling over the seating. No downlights. No track. No sun, no dapple, no sky, no haze band: the record refutes all four of those on this specific place, by measurement. Delivered as a lighting plan on one A1 sheet, a fixture specification, and an acceptance test written as three ratios rather than three lux figures. Colour temperature warm; the measured shadows on that terrace run WARMER than the key (db* +2.4), which is the opposite sign to every sun-and-sky rig and the opposite of every 'moody restaurant' scheme in New York.

> Playa del Carmen was measured as a light in 2026. Install the light. The bus can stay where it is.

This project already owns something no restaurant designer has ever owned: a photometric measurement of one specific beach terrace on one specific afternoon, solved to three ratios and reproduced by a rig that runs.

The signature: the same red reads 3.95 : 1 between the face turned toward the opening and a face 72° away. A vertical column runs 12.5 : 1 across its own diameter. And the counter-intuitive one that makes the whole room — an up-facing cream surface is DARKER than a vertical one facing the opening (0.93 : 1) and brighter than one turned away (1.87 : 1). Tables darker than the faces above them. That is why the reference photographs feel like a place you sat down and a New York dining room does not.

The ceiling on all of it, stated on the sheet: playa_env.py reproduces 4.00 / 1.91 / 0.787 against measured 3.95 / 1.87 / 0.772, and the last of those was never fitted to.

And F92, named rather than acted on quietly. The Playa hero is RULED-rev57b, 'DEPRIORITISED, NOT CANCELLED', and the carriers say do not act on it either. Rule 34's object test: that ruling's object is a RENDER — playa_env.py as the delivery frame. Its object is not a room's luminaires. I am not proposing the render. I am proposing the light. He can overrule that distinction in one sentence, and he should be given the chance before anything is bought.

F92 also carries the sentence this programme has been missing, and it goes on the plan in full: 'the emotional bar that sits ABOVE clinical accuracy.'

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The model is the mock-up rig, and this is the only proposed use of playa_env.py that F92 does not gate. It is self-contained, imports nothing from the project, has one entry point, and already builds the terrace's tables, chairs, gingham skirt, napkin boxes and the ORDENAR sign at their measured depths. Point it at the bus, render side and hero at three lamp positions and three ceiling reflectances, and you have a photometric visualisation of the room before a fixture is specified — solved in scene-linear, so the ratios are readable and not an artefact of the view transform. Nobody has ever used the rig for anything but a hero still that has been on hold since rev 58. Note honestly: the model contributes the SIMULATION and the target numbers. It contributes nothing to the fixture itself, which is an off-the-shelf linear softbox and a bucket of matte paint.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) It is a dark room and the pass will break it. One low lateral source under an absorbing ceiling means the horizontal surfaces read at 1.87 : 1 below the verticals — food photographs beautifully and a health inspector may not accept the illuminance at the griddle. There will have to be a second, hidden, unphotogenic task light over the plancha, and it will violate every ratio above it. That fixture is drawn on the plan and labelled TASK LIGHT — OUTSIDE THE MODEL. (2) He cannot look at a lighting plan. This concept is entirely hostage to its first step, and if that photograph is unconvincing the idea is worth nothing. (3) The F92 boundary is real. A reasonable reader can say I acted on a deprioritised deliverable through a side door. I have made the object argument in the open; it may not survive him. (4) Playa in a New York February may read as gloomy rather than warm, and the only defence I have is a measured chromaticity sign, not a feeling. (5) No ink, no line, no mark — there is nothing here to put in a portfolio and nothing to sell. It only exists once it is switched on.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — SPEC 10.23, all measured off the reference terrace: red 3.95 : 1 flank-to-72°-face; palm trunk 12.5 : 1 across its own diameter; up-facing cream 0.93 : 1 against a vertical facing the opening and 1.87 : 1 against one turned away. Rig reproduction 4.00 / 1.91 / 0.787 — the third never fitted to, which is the ceiling and is printed beside the number. Shadow/key chromaticity split db* +2.4 (shadows warmer). Airlight bound dY < 0.004, canopy shadow floor Y p5 = 0.014–0.019 across the full depth range — i.e. aerial perspective measures zero, so no haze. Foliage median linear luminance 0.104–0.113× the cream bodywork, L* gap 52.1 — the number to hit if anything green enters the room. The galley's own practical, for the bays: emission (1.000, 0.918, 0.790). Everything about the ROOM's dimensions is UNMEASURED here; this is a light, not a plan of their building.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The test is $80 and one evening. A real installation is a lighting designer's fee plus perhaps six to ten fixtures and a ceiling repaint — UNMEASURED, get a quote, and do not let anyone print a number for it off this page. The simulation costs four side renders at about five minutes each on the existing machine, sequentially, no new code. Feasibility risk is not technical, it is regulatory: see risk (1).

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Two photographs, this week, same table, same taco, same camera, same exposure. Frame A: the room as it is lit tonight. Frame B: every ceiling light off, one 1.2 m warm LED batten on a stand at 900 mm, three metres to one side, face turned across the table not down, and a piece of black moleskin or matte board held above as the absorbing ceiling. About $80 of hardware. Print both at A4, hand them over with no commentary and one line on the back — 'the same red, 3.95 to 1' — and let him do what he does, which is decide in three seconds.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:environmental-2`

## APPAREL AS A DESIGNED GARMENT

### 1.02°  ·  screen 5.07/10

**REGISTER** garment  
**ARTEFACT** Two-cloth overshirt, one horizontal seam, cream over red. ~250 gsm cotton twill, both cloths piece-dyed. Brass-gold 2 mm piping insert. ZERO INK. Sizes are chest x STATURE — stature sets one thing only, the height of the seam off the floor.

> A two-tone shirt whose colour break is not level and does not close: the belt line, wrapped round a body, fails to meet itself by 19.9 mm, and the failure is left open on the front placket.

RAKE_DZDX = 0.017750 — 17.75 mm per metre, 1.0169°, nose down, re-based at 4.5 sigma from a retired 33.0. The vehicle's two-tone break sits at Z_BELT 1.2070 m above ground at the ride-drop station, exactly 100.0 mm below the window sill at 1.3070 — and SPEC forbids deriving either from the other. Two separate measurements that happen to land 100 mm apart.

The seam is set out on the flat pattern at 1.0169° to the hem and runs continuously round the body. It therefore cannot close. Over a 1120 mm circumference it descends 19.9 mm, so where it returns to the placket it is 19.9 mm below where it started. THE STEP IS LEFT IN. Every size has its own: 17.8 / 18.8 / 19.9 / 20.9 / 22.0 mm, woven on the tape.

A pin-tuck runs below the seam at 28.169 % of hem-to-seam — V_APEX / Z_BELT, the pressed V-swage, whose identity V_APEX + V_RISE == Z_BELT the build asserts and holds. Brass-gold piping along the last 18.60 % of the seam from centre back: CNT_NOSE_F, the fraction of the counter's outer edge that is brass. It stops where the nosing cap stops.

Cloths: cream (206, 208, 200) — G ABOVE R, a faintly green cream; rev 3 shipped R > G and it was wrong — over red (196, 49, 36), hue 5.0°, sat 0.816. The orange-red (196,106,36) is retired; do not match to it.

Graded by stature: the seam sits at 1207 mm above the floor for every size. The garment fits the vehicle, not the body.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The belt line is a FUNCTION — z_belt(x) = Z_BELT0 - RAKE_DZDX*x — and the source says a bare Z_BELT is correct at one station only. Anyone tracing a photograph either draws the break level or draws an angle confounded with camera roll and ground slope; only the model holds the vehicle's rake as one locked constant separable from those two. The V_APEX + V_RISE == Z_BELT identity, which gives the tuck its 28.169 %, is asserted by the build every run and cannot be read off any frame.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — Every QC inspector in every factory will flag the seam as crooked. "1.02° INTENTIONAL — DO NOT CORRECT" has to sit in the tech pack three times and the first production run will still come back level. Named because it WILL happen. Larger risk: a red-and-cream horizontally split shirt on a taqueria counter is one step from a fast-food uniform, and 19.9 mm of misregistration will not rescue it if the cloths are cheap. Third: for a wearer whose shoulder sits below 1207 mm the seam is above the shoulder and the garment cannot be cut — the bus's belt line is over a small child's head. Where that lands on a growth chart is UNMEASURED HERE and is a commonly available figure. Look it up; do not guess (F229).

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Cheapest of the three: two cloths, one seam, otherwise ordinary construction. Sample ~$180. Production ~$38-48 landed at 300. The two real costs are lab-dipping two colours to measured sRGB values rather than to a swatch book, and the stature grade, which doubles the SKU count. Cut the SKU count by offering three stature bands, not five.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — A SEAM BOARD. 400 x 300 mm, the two cloths joined on the 1.0169° line, the 19.9 mm step left open at the placket end, brass piping running the last 18.6 % of the run. Cut and sewn in an afternoon, mounted flat. He holds it, sees a seam that does not meet itself, and asks why — which is the entire concept delivered in one object.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — False  ·  **id** `dream:garment-2`

### MANDIL 812  ·  screen 4.67/10

**REGISTER** garment · food-and-hand-off · people  
**ARTEFACT** Service coat plus bib apron, cut for one hole. Cream heavy twill (~320 gsm) inside the visible band, plain calico outside it, felled seams in the band and overlocked outside. Bound cuff, no placket flare. ZERO INK.

> A customer sees exactly 403 mm of the cook — from 812 to 1215 mm above the floor she is standing on — so the coat is designed only there and plain everywhere else.

Watched print this session, and nobody in 76 revisions had computed it. The van floor's top sits at 0.5600 m un-dropped; Z_SILL is 1.3720 and Z_HEAD 1.7750. All three are body-fixed, so the differences are frame-independent: THE APERTURE SILL IS 812.0 mm ABOVE THE FLOOR THE COOK STANDS ON, AND THE HEAD IS 1215.0. The plancha's top is 895.0, the galley worktop 941.0.

Through a 515.5 x 403.0 hole a customer sees a 403 mm band of a person — waistband to armpit. No face. No feet. Hands only when they come to the sill.

So the coat is CONSIDERED only between 812 and 1215. In the band: the good cloth, felled seams, a towel loop at the sill line, a bound cuff at 5.5 mm with no placket flare because a cuff catches on a 55 mm radius corner. NO FASTENING INSIDE THE BAND AT ALL — buttons sit above 1215 and below 812, so what the customer sees is unbroken cloth. Outside the band: calico, overlocked, cheap.

The bib apron is 321.0 mm wide, the counter slab's own plan depth. The apron is the counter, worn.

The sleeve stops 100 mm short of the elbow BY CHOICE. The plancha's top is a model constant; the radiant field above it is UNMEASURED and nobody should pretend otherwise.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — 812.0 and 1215.0 exist nowhere else on earth. They are differences between a floor and an aperture that no photograph frames together, and the aperture is ray-tested rather than counted. CEILING, STATED: the floor top is derived on a centred-prism convention corroborated against exactly one other object (plancha, 1.1900 + 0.530/2 = 1.4550, which matches the recorded top), and van_floor's z carries NO PROVENANCE GRADE in the source. 812.0 IS PROVISIONAL UNTIL SOMEONE GRADES IT. That is a finding handed back, not a number to print unqualified.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — A coat that is only good where it can be seen is a satire of restaurant economics, and the person wearing it is the one being satirised. A working cook may find it insulting, and the person most likely to see it first is a Tacombi employee, not a customer. The counter-reading — that the band is also where the wear happens, because that is what leans on the sill — lives in the same garment and does not cancel the first. Both are true at once and that is the point. Second risk, structural: this is the only concept here that collapses if one number is wrong, and that number is provisional. If van_floor's z is authored rather than measured, the band is in the wrong place and the garment is merely eccentric.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Sample ~$350 (two cloths in one body is the fiddly part; the split must fall on a horizontal line, not a panel seam). Production is an ordinary chef-coat cut with an unusual cloth split — ~$45 landed at 200. Apron ~$14. Cheapest route to a first run: split-cloth the FRONT only and keep the back single-cloth, at the cost of the joke's completeness.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — A TRUE-SIZE CARDBOARD APERTURE. One piece, 515.5 x 403.0 mm, corner radius 55.0, plotter-cut from 3 mm grey board, no ink on it at all. He holds it up in Nolita and looks through it at whoever is on the pass. Two pencil ticks on the back edge at 812 and 1215 from the floor. It costs about $15, it is in his hands this week, and it makes the entire argument before a stitch is sewn.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:garment-3`

### LADO E  ·  screen 4.0/10

**REGISTER** garment  
**ARTEFACT** Chore coat. 12 oz undyed cotton-hemp canvas, 2/1 twill. Cut-and-sew, five pattern pieces per front. ZERO INK, zero print, zero applied trademark; the only lettering is jacquard-woven in three colours of thread on the inside-neck tape. Sizes 1-5, graded by body circumference.

> A chore coat with three pockets on one side and nothing whatever on the other, because one side of this bus has never been photographed.

The show side carries three serving apertures. build.py ray-tests them and prints "open serving apertures on +Y: 3"; they measure 515.5 x 403.0 mm, corner radius 55.0, and the mesh reads them back as 516/515/516 — EQUAL. SPEC grades the off side E, never photographed.

So the wearer's right body carries a band of three openings and the left body is one uncut panel. No pocket, no welt, no flap, no seam beyond what construction demands. From the wearer's left this is a plain coat. You have to walk round a person to find the vehicle, and when you get there one side of it is missing.

The band is scaled so the aperture run occupies the same fraction of the garment's circumference (43.924 %) as it occupies of the vehicle's 4065 mm length. Size 3, 1120 mm circumference: pocket 142.0 x 111.0 mm, r 15.15, gaps 32.9. Equal pitch — the record's pillar asymmetry is 0.67 sigma and three equal pillars sit inside the measurement.

Three identical openings, three different insides — which is literally what the bus is. Front = bay 1, storage, a plain hand pocket. Middle = bay 2, the rack, divided for a pen and a thermometer. Rear, on the back panel = bay 3, the tool wall: six loops, because that bay carries gal_hook x 6 and two hanging tools and faces away from every customer.

All topstitching twin-needle at 5.5 mm gauge — GAPW, the panel shut line.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The count is RAY-TESTED through the shell, not counted from panes; the equality is a mesh read-back (516/515/516); the 0.67-sigma retraction that forces equal pitch is a model measurement with a stated sigma; and grade E — the licence to leave a whole side blank — is the record's own admitted hole. A photograph of this bus cannot tell you the other side is unknown. Only the model can. The bay-by-bay interiors come from the built inventory (gal_hook x 6, gal_tool x 2 in bay 3).

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — An asymmetric garment reads as a manufacturing fault. QC will reject it, a proportion of customers will return it as defective, and a left-handed wearer gets no usable pocket at all. Second: the pockets grade WITH the body, so a size 5 pocket is physically larger than a size 1 — no cut-and-sew house does this and every one will "correct" it. Third and worst: the meaning lives entirely in a woven tape that can be cut out, after which this is a coat with an odd pocket layout. It is a joke about not knowing, sold to people who do not know there is a joke.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Pattern 2 days + sew 1 day; first coat in canvas ~$400. Production at any small cut-and-sew house, MOQ ~50/size, ~$70-85 landed. Woven inside-neck tape ~$1.20 at 500. The only unusual demand is a 5.5 mm twin-needle gauge — needle bars commonly hold 4.0/5.0/6.0, so 5.5 may need a custom bar. If it cannot be had, the tape prints the gauge that was actually achieved and the error with it.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — ONE POCKET ON A BOARD. A single 142.0 x 111.0 mm r 15.15 canvas patch, twin-needle 5.5, sewn to a 200 x 250 mm canvas ground and mounted on card, with the woven tape tacked beside it. A sample machinist does it in an hour. He can put his thumb in it this week.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — False  ·  **id** `dream:garment-1`

## DIGITAL / MOTION IDENTITY

### LA COMANDA  ·  screen 6.33/10

**REGISTER** child / polychrome / food-and-hand-off / interface — the five-ink answer to a two-ink programme  
**ARTEFACT** A browser page on a cheap tablet clamped by the host stand, plus its OWN 58 mm thermal receipt printer beside it (not the kitchen's). One game = 90 seconds. Output: one ticket, 58 mm wide × ~120 mm long, thermal paper, one colour, fractions of a cent each. Screen palette: five inks measured off the vehicle plus three off its own painted food vignettes. No accounts, no back end, nothing stored.

> A child works the counter for ninety seconds and the printer hands them a ticket that is redeemable for the sticker this project has owed since rev 44.

A tablet by the host stand and an eighty-dollar thermal printer. A child stands at it and works the counter for ninety seconds.

The screen is the bus's show side, taken from the model's own side orthographic camera — the one ortho in this tree that actually fits (5.90 gives 4.0563 m of vertical at 1600 × 1100; front and rear give 2.4406 against a 3.132 m bbox and clip). A face appears in one of the three holes and orders off the real board: PUERCO ASADO. BARBACOA. PESCADO. POLLO COSTEÑO. MAIZ Y POBLANO. The child taps it onto the plancha — which in the model is a 0.560 × 0.780 m slab with nothing ever on it — then waits, because waiting is what a counter actually is, then passes the plate out through the hole. Three orders. The roof lid lifts to 76°. The 118 lamps come on down the drip rail one at a time, 28.52 mm apart. The printer chatters.

The ticket carries their name, what they cooked, the date, and at the foot the bus in die-cut line with CANJEABLE POR UNA CALCOMANÍA — hand this in for a sticker.

That is the point. The die-cut sticker is the oldest live row in the register, the original deliverable, open since rev 44, waiting on 'build it after the model is done' — a condition with no date on it. This gives it a date, an occasion, and a reason a child would want it.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — REAL BUT PARTIAL, AND I WILL NOT INFLATE IT. The model gives three things and no more. (1) The true side elevation and the three genuinely equal holes at 515.5 × 403.0 with a 55 mm corner radius — drawn by hand they would come out unequal, because every drawing of this bus ever made has. (2) The articulation, so the bus on screen opens the way the object opens: roof lid 76.0° (DECLARED, inside a 68.9–78° bracket), tail board 38.0°, hatch 64.0° (NOT MEASURED). (3) The 118 lamp positions on the drip rail, so the light runs at the real pitch. It does NOT give the food, the faces, the hands, or the interior — all of that is drawn by a person. THIS CONCEPT COULD BE BUILT WITHOUT THE MODEL AND WOULD BE WORSE, NOT IMPOSSIBLE. Saying so is cheaper than faking a dependency.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) THE PRINTER IS AN OPERATIONAL HAZARD. This puts a games machine on the end of a thermal printer inside a working restaurant. Share the kitchen printer and the kitchen unplugs it inside a week, correctly. It needs its own device at the host stand and a hard rate limit. That is a real cost and a real failure mode, not a footnote.
(2) IT PICKS A SIDE IN AN UNRESOLVED FIGHT. The programme's centre of gravity is design-literate adults; his ONLY stated audience brief is CHILDREN AT THE RESTAURANT, and 'spark joy... something families keep'. This concept chooses the children on purpose, and it will look slight beside a lotería deck and an uncertainty plate. Somebody has to actually build for the stated audience instead of noting the conflict in a findings list.
(3) FREE STICKERS COST MONEY AND MAKE A QUEUE AT THE COUNTER during service. Small, real, his to price — not mine to wave away.
(4) IT STYLISES THE ARTWORK. The mural, the script and the sunburst become flat shapes at 40 px. SPEC 10.10's 'ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ALL ARTWORK' — his words, a hard bar — has the MODEL as its object; this is a derivative drawing and a different object under rule 34. DECLARED, not assumed. The ticket's bus obeys F18's locked STYLE (vector line and flat colour, occlusion sampled off the 3D asset) but NOT its locked SCENE, because the sun and the papel picado are a conflict he deferred to himself — 'Leave it open, I'll decide when the sticker is actually being built.' DO NOT RE-PUT IT.
(5) THE EMBLEM IS SIMPLY NOT IN THIS DRAWING — it is a show-side elevation and the roundel is on the nose. Declared here rather than quietly routed around. F191 is untouched and stays shut.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Three apertures 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55, EQUAL — and NOT the retracted pillar asymmetry: 20.0 mm against σ 30.0 is 0.67σ and three equal pillars sit inside the measurement. Side ortho 5.90 → 4.0563 m vertical at 1600 × 1100 (fits the 3.132 m bbox); front/rear 3.55 → 2.4406 m and CLIP — arithmetic I ran this session. 118 lamps, rail 3.3368 m, realised pitch 28.52 mm — watched printing from BULB_X0 −1.6368 / BULB_X1 1.7000 / BULB_PITCH 0.0286. THE '115' IN CIRCULATION IS THE PHOTOGRAPH-SIDE FFT INFERENCE ABOUT A 3.50 m RAIL (28.6 ± 1.0 mm), NOT THIS MODEL'S COUNT. Plancha 0.560 × 0.780 m, top z 1.455. Roof lid 76.0° DECLARED inside a 68.9–78° bracket; hatch 64.0° NOT MEASURED. Menu read off the real boards. Palette: RED (196,49,36), CREAM (206,208,200), STRIP yellow (172,144,17), mural ORANGE (163,66,21), GOLD (172,119,24), PINK meat (206,118,124), GREEN garnish (96,128,54), CREAMV plate (232,224,198). ⚠ The strip INK (72,46,6) is CONSTRUCTED, not measured — the measured median is (91,59,7) and is blur-limited. Caption it as constructed or don't print it.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Tablet ~$150. Thermal printer ~$80. Paper: pennies a ticket. Build: about a week of front-end, no back end, no accounts, no data collection — which for a children's product is not a saving, it is the point. Nothing is stored and nothing is tracked. The five screen inks are the vehicle's own measured colours and the three food inks already exist in lid_gen.py, measured off the painted vignettes on its own roof board, and have never been used for anything.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — PRINT THE TICKET WITH NO GAME BEHIND IT. Ten of them, hand-filled, off an eighty-dollar thermal printer: a name, three tacos, the date, and at the foot the bus in die-cut line with CANJEABLE POR UNA CALCOMANÍA. Put them on the counter on a Saturday, hand them to ten children, watch what happens. That is $80 and an afternoon, it needs no software at all, and it answers the only question that matters before anyone writes a line of code. IT IS ALSO THE ASK THE LAST ROUND MISSED (their own finding 12): the question for him is not 'shall we build the sticker' — that was ruled in rev 43 — it is 'IS THIS HOW THE STICKER GETS HANDED OVER?'

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:motion-3`

### LA PASADA (The Pass)  ·  screen 5.8/10

**REGISTER** motion / photographic / food-and-hand-off / people — three registers the programme had none of  
**ARTEFACT** An 8-second seamless silent loop. 96 frames at 12 fps (on twos). Two cuts: 1080 × 1350 for a phone, and 1600 × 1100 for the screen above the pass and the site hero. MP4 (h.264) + WebM, ~4 MB. Photoreal, rendered in the shipped studio rig — no ink, no substrate, no print. Runtime 8 s, loops forever.

> The camera goes inside the bus for the first time in seventy-six revisions and watches a plate cross the brass line.

Eight seconds, on a loop, and the camera never moves. You are inside the bus at the cook's eye, three hundred millimetres behind the middle serving hole. The hole is the frame for the whole eight seconds: 515.5 × 403.0 mm, corner radius 55, one of three, all equal. Across the bottom of the picture runs the counter's brass nosing — 18.6 % of the slab's outer edge, measured on one frame and corroborated at 19.1 % on a second. Overhead the galley's fluorescent strip, emission (1.000, 0.918, 0.790) at strength 3.04, does the lighting; the roof opening does the rest. A hand comes in from your side carrying a paper-lined plate with three tacos. It crosses the brass line. Another hand takes it. Both leave. Two beats of empty aperture. Loop.

That is the whole thing, and it is the only image in this programme in which anybody is given anything. Nine distinct photographs of this bus exist and all nine are from the customer's side, because the man who works there is standing exactly where this camera goes. This is the reverse shot and it has never been taken.

It also ships geometry: two forearms, one plate, three tacos. The first human geometry in seventy-six revisions and the first food ever. Beyond the aperture is white — 'keep studio' has been ruled three times and this does not reopen it.

F92's emotional bar that sits ABOVE clinical accuracy, reached without touching the Playa hero. No terrace, no palms, no playa_env. The hold is untouched.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Total, and specifically: the model can put a camera where no photographer can stand. All nine reference frames are exterior; the cook's position is occupied by the cook. The aperture that frames the shot is measured (515.5 × 403.0, r 55, sill Z 1.3720 un-dropped = 1.307 above ground); the brass line the plate crosses is measured (CNT_NOSE_F = 0.186 of the slab edge, on a 107 mm slab 321 mm deep); the light source is the built practical. AND THE MODEL SUPPLIES THE PIECE'S OWN CAPTION: t1_detail states outright that galley DEPTH (Y) IS NOT MEASURABLE. So the frame around you is measured to the millimetre and everything behind you is a construction — that sentence goes on the end card.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — THREE, none of them sanded.
(1) IT COLLIDES WITH F193 HEAD-ON. He has held the photoreal delivery render three times, volunteered — rev 58, 64, 65: 'I don't think the bus is ready yet.' The rev-76 brief's line that 'a line program does not touch what F191/F193 gate' DOES NOT COVER THIS: this is a photoreal render, moving. F191's object (the emblem) is genuinely absent — the nose is not in frame — but F193's object is the render itself. This must be put to him as multiple choice before a single frame is queued. It is a better question than any of the twelve the last round asked.
(2) HANDS. A bad CG hand is worse than no hand, and he rejected a nose for looking like an Amtrak train. He will report a bad hand and his repeat is a measurement. Wrist forward only, under two seconds on screen, mostly in the strip light's shadow — and if it still fails, the honest fallback is a photographed real hand composited against the rendered aperture, which is a different and legitimate object, not a rescue.
(3) THE FOOD IS INVENTED. No taco exists as a mesh and no photograph we hold shows the food at all. UNMEASURED, printed in the credit line, not hidden.
AND ONE MEASURED OBSTACLE NOBODY HAS EVER HIT, BECAUSE NOBODY HAS RENDERED TWO CONSECUTIVE FRAMES: no Cycles seed is set anywhere in the render path. I grepped it — `cycles.seed` appears once in this tree, in probe_rev55_truenorm.py, and never in studio.py. So consecutive frames draw independent noise and the measured floor is 2.04 % of pixels differing by more than 8 levels on hero34f, 4.37 % inside the front-wheel box, 7.90 % on the body. Over 96 frames that is boil and it will look cheap. The fix is one line and has never been tried here — pin scene.cycles.seed to a constant so the grain is static rather than boiling — and per rule 3 it is not fixed until the two-frame difference has been WATCHED collapsing. If it does not collapse, this ships denoised or it ships flat.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Serving aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm, corner radius 55, THREE, EQUAL (built read-back 516/515/516). Sill Z 1.3720 un-dropped = 1.307 m above ground; head 1.7750 / 1.710 AG. Counter slab 107.0 mm thick, 321.0 mm deep (y 0.845→1.166), running x +0.918 → −2.188; brass nosing CNT_NOSE_F = 0.186 of the outer edge, corroborated 0.191 on ref_rear34.jpg; brass linear (0.6600, 0.4750, 0.1750), roughness 0.255. Galley strip light emission (1.000, 0.918, 0.790) at GAL_LUM 3.04 (watched print). RED (196, 49, 36); CREAM (206, 208, 200). Render noise floor 2.044 % of pixels > 8 levels on hero34f, 4.37 % in the front-wheel box, 7.90 % on the body. Build time watched this session: 30.5 s at T1_SUB=1, VERIFY: 0 fail, 0 warn. UNMEASURED and stated on the face: the food, the hands, and the galley's entire depth axis.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Geometry: two forearms, one plate, three tacos — roughly six new meshes, one modelling session. Render: ~5 min a frame at 1600 × 1100 / 96 spp, so 96 frames is about 8 hours — ONE overnight run, sequentially from one script (do not fan out Blender; it is CPU-bound on 4 cores and two instances make both slower). Freeze the tree for the duration. Cut and encode: an hour. Zero licence exposure, zero trademark exposure (the roundel is not in frame), no third parties, no unit cost. The most expensive thing about it is the permission.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — ONE STILL, THIS WEEK, AND NO GEOMETRY AT ALL. Render a single frame from the cook's side of the middle aperture — empty hole, brass line across the bottom, white beyond. Five minutes on the machine. It is an image nobody in this project has ever seen: the inside of the bus, looking out. Put that one frame in front of him with one question — 'is this worth eight seconds?' — and the whole concept is either alive or dead by Friday, before a hand is modelled or a queue is launched.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:motion-1`

### GRADO E  ·  screen 5.0/10

**REGISTER** three-dimensional / spatial — the register the programme has never entered at all  
**ARTEFACT** A single web page carrying one <model-viewer> element; a glTF 2.0 binary (.glb) of the vehicle, Draco-compressed, target under 25 MB; a USDZ twin for iOS AR Quick Look. Opens by tapping a link on any phone. Orbit with a finger; tap AR and stand the bus on the floor at 1:1 — 4065 mm long, 1750 wide, roof 1983.5 at the rear axle. No app, no store, no account. Hosting: free.

> The bus, at full size, in his actual restaurant, rendered at the confidence the record actually has — walk to the side nobody photographed and it stops existing.

A link. He taps it at the host stand and the bus is standing in the room at full size. He walks round it.

The show side is solid — red, cream, three equal holes, the counter, the brass line. Then it starts telling the truth. The tail board goes hollow, a cage of lines, because it is BUILT AT 38.0° AND PHOTOGRAPHED AT 28.0° and that has been unresolved for seven revisions, and because its 550 mm width is a pose choice with an upper bound of 590 mm and NO LOWER BOUND. The rear hatch goes hollow: the build log's own words, watched printing this session, are 'OPEN 64.0 deg [angle NOT MEASURED -- no frame shows it]'. Then he reaches the off side and the vehicle stops. The whole cargo-door flank is SPEC grade E, never photographed, an expert inference — and it hangs there as four metres of empty wireframe in his restaurant.

No poster does this. A tolerance has never been printed on a toy box and a confidence level has never been walked around. Every drawing lies by being finished. This one shows you exactly where the record runs out, at the scale of a body, in a room.

The roundel goes hollow too. It scores 0.8528 against a control ceiling of 0.9465 and he has rejected it nine times. Making that the subject instead of the shame is the whole bet.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — It IS the model — there is no version of this without it, and two mesh facts make it possible rather than merely desirable. (1) NON-MANIFOLD EDGES ON THE BODY = 0, which is why it survives export, triangulation and being walked around at eight inches. A photogrammetry scan of the real bus would not. (2) 229 NAMED MESH OBJECTS, which is what lets confidence be assigned PER PART rather than per pixel — the grades already exist in SPEC and OPEN_FINDINGS, so the confidence pass is a lookup table against an object inventory the machine already writes. The piece is not an illustration of the record; it is the record, served as a body.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) IT WALKS STRAIGHT INTO F191 AND CANNOT COMPOSE ITS WAY OUT. In AR the viewer puts the phone eight inches from the nose; you cannot crop a room. This concept's answer is deliberate and arguable: the emblem is not measured to standard, so IT RENDERS AS A GHOST LIKE EVERYTHING ELSE THAT ISN'T, and the page says so in the same flat voice. He may read that as a failure dressed up as a concept. I am not going to pretend that reading is unreasonable.
(2) IT HANDS HIM A BUS WITH A MISSING SIDE, ON PURPOSE. For seventy-six revisions his instinct on being shown something wrong has been to report it, and his repeat IS a measurement. If he reads the wireframe flank as 'you didn't finish it' rather than as the subject, this dies in ten seconds and takes some credibility with it. There is no mitigation except framing: the first sentence on the page has to be the confession, before he finds it himself.
(3) AR IS A GRAVEYARD. Most branded AR objects are opened once. The honest defence is that the ORBIT page — no AR, just a bus you can turn with a thumb — is the deliverable, and AR placement costs one extra file.
(4) SPEC 10.10, 'ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ARTWORK', his words, a hard bar. This piece DEFACES NOTHING — it renders every piece of artwork exactly as the model carries it — but it renders some of the artwork's CARRIERS (the tail board, the off flank) as ghosts. That is a rule-34 distinction and it is declared here rather than assumed: the bar's object is the MODEL, and this is a delivery of the model annotated by its own register.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Body 4065 mm long × 1750 wide (build print: dims L=4.065 W=1.750). Roof at rear axle 1983.5 mm, n=230 mesh samples. Wheelbase 2400; track 1369 / 1359. Tyre ⌀664.9 — not a 6.40-15; rim flange ⌀439.6, a SIXTEEN-inch rim. Non-manifold edges (body) 0; 229 mesh objects; 366,490 faces. Tail board 38.0° built against 28.0° photographed, chord 0.711 m, tip x −2.4133 z 2.2001 (watched print); TB_WIDTH 0.550 m a POSE CHOICE, parallax bound ≤ 0.590, NO lower bound. Rear hatch 64.0° — the build log's own '[angle NOT MEASURED -- no frame shows it]', watched this session. Off flank: SPEC grade E, never photographed; the build's own line reads 'off flank is graded E, NOT a correctness claim'. Emblem 0.8528 pose-free IoU against a P1b control ceiling of 0.9465. And printed on the page, unchanged: 'The model reads 1.9835 m. The real vehicle's absolute roof height is OPEN and UNMEASURED.'

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — THE EXPORT PATH DOES NOT EXIST. I grepped every .py in the tree for glTF / OBJ / USD / export_scene: ZERO HITS. Only a .blend save exists. So this needs one new call, bpy.ops.export_scene.gltf, plus a material bake and a Draco pass — a day's work, and the whole programme then owns it, because the printers, the animator and any future 3D use all pull from the same .glb. Then a day for the confidence pass (229 objects against a grade table) and a day for the page. Three days, no unit cost, no print, no licence, free hosting. iOS AR needs the USDZ twin, which is a second conversion step and is the one genuinely fiddly part — state it rather than promise it.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — EXPORT THE .glb WITH NO CONFIDENCE PASS AT ALL, drop it into a <model-viewer> page, send him the link. One afternoon. He turns his own bus around on his phone for the first time in seventy-six revisions — after seventy-six revisions of seeing it only as flat stills floating on white paper. That single act will land harder than any poster in this programme, and it tells us within a day whether the ghost version is worth building.

## SOUND

### SILBATO 1:20  ·  screen 7.6/10

**REGISTER** sound / child / three-dimensional  
**ARTEFACT** A one-piece moulded whistle: the measured vehicle at 1:20, 203.3 x 87.5 mm, hollow, played as an ocarina through its own three serving apertures. Food-grade polypropylene, no paint, no decal, three lines moulded into the underside. Given to children at the door.

> The bus lost the one sound everybody knows it by, so give a child a bus that is nothing but sound -- and let its three equal serving holes be the fingering.

MOTOR: NINGUNO. TRANSMISSION: SOLD. This vehicle was stripped of the most recognisable noise ever fitted to a road car. Hand a child back a combi that makes no engine sound at all and only sings.

The body is the measured vehicle at 1:20 -- 203.3 x 87.5 mm, straight off the mesh, not off a die-cast maker's eye. Every VW bus toy ever made is a caricature: nose too round, roof too tall, arches wrong. This would be the first one whose proportions were surveyed rather than remembered.

It is an ocarina, and the fingering is this vehicle's single most solid fact. Three serving apertures, 515.5 x 403.0 mm, corner radius 55, EQUAL, become three holes of 25.775 x 20.15 mm, 512.9 mm2 each. Cover all three and it sings low; lift a finger at a time and you get three pitches. Three holes, three notes, equal because the mesh says equal.

WHETHER IT SPEAKS AT ALL IS UNMEASURED, and that is printed. 513 mm2 a hole is enormous beside any ocarina's fingering, and the first print exists to find that out rather than to confirm it. If the body will not sound at true scale, the apertures get a scale of their own and THAT number goes on the underside next to the other one.

Moulded beneath, three lines: 515.5 x 403.0 / 20 -- MOTOR: NINGUNO -- EMBLEM OMITTED, NOT LICENSED BY VOLKSWAGEN. The nose is deliberately bare.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — TOTAL. There is no other source for this object. A whistle body is a resonating cavity, so its shape IS its sound -- the geometry is not decoration here, it is the instrument, and the only surveyed geometry of this vehicle in existence is the mesh. The three apertures are equal at 515.5 x 403.0 with a 55 mm corner radius because the mesh was ray-tested, not counted off a photograph, which is what makes a three-note fingering honest rather than decorative. Zero non-manifold edges is what lets the shell be split, hollowed and printed at all. AND THE BLOCKER, NAMED: this tree has NO export path -- glTF, OBJ, USD, FBX, STL, none of it, only a .blend save. An STL writer off the evaluated mesh is the whole gate on this concept and it is half a day of code, not a project.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — IT IS THE RISKIEST OF THE THREE AND I AM NOT TRIMMING IT. (1) CHILDREN'S PRODUCT SAFETY IS NOT A FORMALITY. A mouth-blown toy for children is a regulated article -- CPSIA and ASTM F963 in the US, EN 71-1/2/3 in Europe -- and whistles specifically carry a recall history for detached mouthpieces. 203.3 mm of body is far too big to swallow, which is exactly why 1:20 and not 1:43, but the testing is compulsory, it costs real money, and it can fail. (2) TWO HUNDRED CHILDREN WITH WHISTLES IS A DINING ROOM NOBODY WANTS TO SIT IN. Given at the door on the way OUT, and if he says no it is dead, correctly. (3) A SHARED MOUTHPIECE IN A RESTAURANT is a conversation the front of house will have, not the designer. (4) IT MAY NOT SPEAK, and the whole fingering idea goes with it. (5) IT IS NOT F18. It is a companion to the die-cut sticker, not a substitute, and it does not settle F18's papel-picado trigger, which is still his to call. (6) SPEC 10.10, ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ARTWORK: this object carries NO mural, NO script, NO Calidad sunburst and NO roundel. Rule 34 -- the bar's object is the MODEL; this is a derivative object and it OMITS rather than redraws, and it says so in moulded type instead of cropping quietly around the mark he has rejected nine times.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Body 4065 x 1750 mm at 1:20 = 203.3 x 87.5 mm. Serving aperture 515.5 x 403.0 mm, corner radius 55, THREE, EQUAL -> 25.775 x 20.15 mm, r 2.75; open area computed this session with corner radii subtracted and watched print: 512.9 mm2 per hole, 1538.6 mm2 for all three. Speed of sound 331.3 + 0.606*T: 343.4 m/s at 20 C against 352.5 m/s at 35 C = +45.2 cents (watched print) -- the note runs nearly a quarter-tone sharp in a warm mouth, which is a fact to print on the packet, not a defect. Nose roundel 280.0 mm diameter -> 14.0 mm, OMITTED. Rake 17.75 mm/m; rear overhang 773 +/- 22 mm. NOT USED AND FORBIDDEN: any pillar asymmetry -- 20.0 mm against sigma 30.0 is 0.67 sigma and three equal pillars sit inside the measurement. UNMEASURED, and printed on the face: whether the body speaks at that opening ratio, and the full-size cavity's Helmholtz frequency -- the real bus is not a sealed box (roof aperture open, hatch swung at a declared 64.0 deg), so no cavity note for the vehicle itself can be recovered from what we hold.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Order of magnitude only, and these are ESTIMATES, not measurements. STL export: half a day inside the repo. First prototype, resin or FDM, printed in two halves and glued: 40-150 USD for three to five bodies, turnaround in days. A 50-piece printed trial for one restaurant: order of 8-15 USD each. Injection tooling in polypropylene: order of 4,000-9,000 USD for the tool plus roughly 0.60-1.50 USD a part at 2,000+, so the unit economics only work if he wants thousands. Third-party children's-product lab testing: order of 500-1,500 USD per SKU and NOT optional. The cheap path is real and it is printed, not moulded -- fifty whistles in a restaurant for the price of a night's covers, and only tool it if children actually keep them.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — TWO DAYS, THEN IT IS IN HIS HAND. Write the STL export, split the shell, hollow it, print one body at 1:20 on any FDM machine, glue the halves and blow it. Hand him the object across his own counter and let him find out with his own mouth whether it speaks -- he does not need a chart, a crop or a multiple-choice question for this one. If it makes a note, tell him what the note is. If it makes nothing, hand it to him anyway and tell him 513 mm2 a hole was the number and it did not work, which is a result. Either way he is holding the first accurately-proportioned VW combi anybody has ever made.
