### LA CHAROLA (MENOS DIEZ)  ·  screen 6.67/10

**REGISTER** THREE-DIMENSIONAL and FOOD-AND-HAND-OFF — a made object, zero ink, zero trademark. The only item in the programme whose whole purpose is the moment of handing over.  
**ARTEFACT** Serving tray, 495.5 × 383.0 mm, r 45, 12 mm birch ply, 22 mm upstand, oiled and NOT lacquered, with a 4.09 mm brass strip inlaid along the outer edge. Branded underside, no printing anywhere. Retail edition at 1:2: 247.75 × 191.5 mm, r 22.5, 11 mm edge, 2.05 mm brass. One per aperture, three per bus, serialised by city.

> The serving tray is the serving hole — 515.5 × 403.0, r 55 — minus 10 mm of hand clearance on every side, because a tray the size of the hole cannot go through the hole.

The aperture is 515.5 × 403.0, r 55, three of them, equal. Its sill sits at 1.307 above ground, 53 mm above the counter's 1.254 top. Food crosses that hole. So make the tray that hole.

Cut it in 12 mm birch ply — his own reading of the counter top, 'clean varnished plywood', rev 28 — with a 22 mm upstand, oiled, never lacquered. Inlay a brass strip along the outer edge at 0.1860 of its depth: 4.09 mm, the exact fraction the counter's own nosing occupies of its 107 mm slab, a scale-free measured ratio that transfers to any size. Same brass, same tarnish. Tray and counter age into one another over a year of service, which is the only kind of branding that cannot be copied.

Brand the underside: 515.5 × 403.0 · r 55, then MENOS 10, then the city and a serial. The tray is 495.5 × 383.0, r 45. It is smaller than the drawing on its own belly and that gap is the concept: seventy-six revisions on the difference between the figure and the thing, and here the difference is ten millimetres of knuckle. A system, because every bus has three apertures and every city gets its own serial. A thing in time, because unlacquered brass and oiled ply are the only two finishes that get better.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — ALMOST NOTHING, and I would rather say so than fake a dependency. Four numbers and one scale-free ratio. What the model contributes is that the four are TRUE and mutually consistent: the three bays are EQUAL at 515.5 because the mesh says so, and the retired 0.507 / 0.516 / 0.526 taper is the standing counterexample proving a photograph alone would have got it wrong. No render, no line pass, no AO, no camera. A tape measure and this repository.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) The claim is technically false the moment anybody measures it. 'This is the hole' is a lie by 10 mm, and only the underside caption redeems it. If a production run ever drops that caption the object becomes dishonest — the caption is load-bearing, not decoration. (2) Unlacquered brass and oiled ply will pit and delaminate in a commercial dishwasher. Hand-wash-only is a real imposition on a working taqueria at service speed, and he may refuse on those grounds and be right. (3) Zero ink, zero trademark: nothing on it says Tacombi except numbers. A restaurateur may fairly ask what he is paying for, and the answer — 'the proportion of the hole your food comes through' — either lands or it does not. (4) Hot food slides on oiled ply and a wooden tray absorbs grease along the grain. (5) The 10 mm clearance is DECLARED, not measured. It is a pose choice in exactly the sense TB_WIDTH 550 is, and it is labelled that way on the object.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Serving aperture 515.5 × 403.0, r 55 (t1_shell.py: BAY_W = 0.5155, Z_SILL/Z_HEAD 1.3720/1.7750, BAY_R = 0.0550), three, equal — the retired taper explicitly not used; counter slab 107.0 mm thick (CNT_ZT, CNT_ZB = 1.2540, 1.1470), 321.0 mm deep; brass nosing fraction CNT_NOSE_F = 0.1860 of the slab's outer edge, corroborated at 0.191 on a second frame; brass albedo linear (0.6600, 0.4750, 0.1750), roughness 0.255. DERIVED: 495.5 × 383.0, r 45; 4.09 mm brass on a 22 mm edge. DECLARED, not measured, and printed as such: the 10 mm clearance and the 22 mm edge depth.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — ESTIMATES, not measurements. CNC one-off $150–300. Run of 50: $45–70 each. Retail 1:2 edition maybe $30–45 landed, retailing $95–120 — priced as a joinery object, not as merch. No tooling, no die, no minimum. The supplier is a joiner with a CNC bed and a brass stockist, not a printer, which is a new relationship for the house.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Cut 495.5 × 383.0, r 45 out of 3 mm greyboard with a scalpel and a compass. Twenty minutes, zero cost, this afternoon. Put a real order of tacos on it, set it on the counter and photograph it beside the aperture it copies. He will know in three seconds whether the proportion reads, and if it does not, the concept is dead for $0.

### ARMA TU COMBI  ·  screen 6.53/10

**REGISTER** CHILD — collectible paper and vinyl; the die-cut closed path as a collection. Warm, hand-sized, low-literacy, meant to be lost and re-found in a drawer.  
**ARTEFACT** Album sheet: A4 landscape, 297 × 210 mm, 4/4, 200 gsm uncoated, one warm-grey line plus flat colour. Twelve estampas: kiss-cut matt vinyl, digital print, one shape per small sheet, NO steel-rule dies. Twelve issues across a year, one per visit.

> Twelve die-cut estampas, one per visit, that assemble on an A4 keyline into the whole bus at 1:20 — and the album's reverse, the off side, is blank forever because no photograph of it exists.

The front is the show side at 1:20 in one line: 215.85 mm long (4.317 m — the machine's own figure for the bus with its counter and boards out, the pose it is in when you actually buy a taco) and 156.60 mm to the top of the raised mural lid. Twelve numbered outlines wait inside it. A child gets one estampa a visit and sticks it in place. RUEDA first, because he said he liked how the wheels were drawn in the earlier cartoon version: four measured concentric circles, 33.24 / 21.98 / 13.70 / 4.35 mm. Then EL MOSTRADOR with its warmer, four squeeze bottles and caddies; LA VENTANA ×3 at 25.77 × 20.15, r 2.75, equal; EL TECHO; LA TABLA; LA NARIZ. Style is his lock, untouched: vector line and flat colour, shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset.

Two things stop it being a loyalty card. First, the bulb string cannot be cut. At 1:20 the 118 lamps sit 1.426 mm apart, under any kiss-cut blade — so the festoon is printed, never an estampa, and the album says that in six words. Second, turn the album over. There is the off side, keyline only, and it will never carry a sticker, because SPEC grades that elevation E — never photographed. A children's album with one face that cannot be completed, and a sentence saying why. That is this whole project handed to a seven-year-old, and it is the oldest thing we owe him: F18, open since rev 44.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — REGISTRATION, which is the one thing hand-drawing cannot buy. Twelve parts drawn by twelve hands never butt; twelve cut from one orthographic projection of one manifold body (0 non-manifold edges, 229 named objects, side ortho already fits) always do. The Grease Pencil Line Art → SVG pass is the keystone and this is its first consumer; the AO pass is the 'rendered depth' half of his locked style. But state the honest limit: STEP ONE NEEDS NO LINE PASS AT ALL — the wheel is four measured diameters and a compass.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — (1) It is engineered scarcity pointed at children. Twelve visits is a loyalty mechanic wearing a toy's clothes, and a parent who cannot come twelve times has a child with an unfinished album. That it is free is a defence, not an exoneration. (2) The blank reverse will be read as a misprint; staff will be asked 'is mine broken?' every week, forever. (3) LA NARIZ carries the roundel at Ø14.00 mm — the emblem he has rejected NINE times, F191 still shut. It ships wrong, small, with the failure declared on the album's back. A tenth rejection would land in a child's hands, which is worse than on a screen. Declared, not routed around. (4) His locked scene says 'die-cut tight' — ONE closed path. Twelve separate paths is a cut logic he has never ruled on. THAT IS THE UNASKED TRIGGER QUESTION (critique 12): does the sticker ship as one object or as a system? Ask it as multiple choice with this sheet attached. (5) The estampas stylise the mural, the script and the Calidad burst at 1:20 in flat colour. SPEC §10.10's absolute-replication bar has the MODEL as its object; an estampa is a different object (rule 34). That defence is printed on the album's back, not assumed.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — 4.317 m in-service length and 4.065 m body (STATE.md line 28/34) → 215.85 / 203.25 mm at 1:20; bbox top 3.132 m → 156.60 mm; tyre Ø664.9, rim flange Ø439.6, hubcap Ø274.0, badge Ø86.9, roundel Ø280.0 → 33.24 / 21.98 / 13.70 / 4.35 / 14.00 mm; aperture 515.5 × 403.0, r 55, THREE, EQUAL → 25.77 × 20.15, r 2.75; 118 festoon lamps at 28.52 mm → 1.426 mm pitch, below any cut blade (the brief's '115' is an inference about a 3.50 m rail, not this build — corrected on the artefact); counter slab 107.0 thick, 321.0 deep; 229 mesh objects, 0 non-manifold edges. UNMEASURED and printed as such: the off side elevation, grade E.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The production fact the whole thing rests on: digital kiss-cutting needs no steel-rule dies, so twelve different shapes cost what one shape costs. ESTIMATES, not measurements — album sheet ~$0.12–0.25 at 5,000; each estampa sheet ~$0.10–0.20. A child who completes costs the house ~$2–4; most never complete, so true cost is well under that. Build cost is one line-pass build (the keystone item, needed anyway) plus twelve drawings. No tooling, no minimum order, no new supplier — a sticker printer and a laser printer.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — One A4 sheet, both sides, off the office laser this week: front, the 1:20 keyline; back, the empty off side with its E — NEVER PHOTOGRAPHED caption. Plus ONE estampa — the wheel, four circles at 33.24 / 21.98 / 13.70 / 4.35 mm, ten minutes in any vector tool, printed on A4 matt vinyl and cut with a scalpel. Hand it to a child at the restaurant on Saturday and watch her face. No Blender, no render, no line pass.

## FREE DIRECTOR 7 — ruthlessly commercial

### ¿TRAE SU ENVASE?  ·  screen 8.2/10

**REGISTER** packaging as a system — polychrome, five inks, the vehicle's own count — and hand-painted, by a commissioned rotulista rather than imitated. Food, and two named people credited on the object.  
**ARTEFACT** A 150 ml squat flint-glass bottle, five colours screen-printed DIRECT TO THE GLASS — no paper label — with a lug cap, filled with the house salsa. Sold at $14. Refilled at the counter, into the customer's own bottle, at $6. The merchandising unit is a wooden crate of 24 standing on the counter, not a shelf. Back of the bottle, 6 pt, one column: ingredients by weight, the rotulista's name, and the cook's name.

> The returnable-bottle economy already standing in our own reference frame, turned into the only merch item that physically drags a customer back through the door.

Five colours screened onto glass, because paper labels come off in a kitchen and because the vehicle carries five inks and this is the one object in the programme that can carry all of them. Fourteen dollars filled. Then the mechanic: bring the bottle back and the refill is six.

That is the envase retornable, and it is not a sustainability gesture bolted on — it is already in our own evidence. Four stacked crates of returnable Coca-Cola bottles stand beside the bus in IMG_2073. It is a machine for making somebody walk back in, and hot sauce is the single highest-attach paid item at a taqueria counter. Nothing else on the bench has a reorder engine at all.

The art is not ours, and that is the point. We print a true orthographic side elevation of the bus at 90 mm wide, hand it to a rotulista with a fee, and they paint the flank in enamel over our line. We photograph the painted panel and separate it to five screens. Every hand-painted VW bus in the world has the nose too round and the roof too tall; this one will not, because the hand is working over a measurement. That is Francis Alÿs's move — commission the practitioner, do not imitate them — and it converts an appropriation charge into a credit line next to the ingredients.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — One thing, and it is decisive: A TRUE ORTHOGRAPHIC SIDE ELEVATION. The side ortho camera fits the vehicle today (front and rear clip at 2.4406 m against a 3.132 m bbox top and would need fixing first). No photograph can give this — every frame we hold is perspective, ref_side.jpg has the cab door open and a man standing directly in front of the front wheel, and a photogrammetry scan would give a worse outline than the mesh does. The model supplies the ground the painter works over: correct proportions, the two-tone break, three equal apertures, the shut lines as screen registration marks. It supplies NO PICTURE. The picture is a person's, and it should be.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — The one that can kill it, first: REFILLING A CUSTOMER'S OWN CONTAINER WITH A FOOD PRODUCT IS RESTRICTED OR PROHIBITED OUTRIGHT in several US health jurisdictions, New York among the strictest. Check with an inspector before a single bottle is ordered. The fallback — return a dirty bottle, receive a clean filled one — is legally cleaner and operationally much harder, because now you are washing and sanitising glass at a taco counter. Second: a shelf-stable salsa is a scheduled-process, pH-validated, co-packer job, not a kitchen job. That is the real cost and the real lead time, and it is the reason most restaurant hot sauces never ship. Third: commissioning a rotulista means THE ARTWORK IS NOT OURS — write the licence before the brush moves. Fourth, and a designer will hate this one: the flank the painter paints IS NOT THE FLANK ON THE VEHICLE. It is a declared departure from SPEC §10.10's "ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ALL ARTWORK", which he called a hard bar. The bar's object is the MODEL; a bottle is a different object. Stated, not assumed. And the VW roundel is composed out of a 90 mm elevation because at that size it cannot be judged and he has rejected it nine times.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — The elevation itself is the measured artefact: body 4065 mm long, 1750 wide; wheelbase 2400; track 1369 / 1359; tyre ⌀664.9 on a ⌀439.6 rim flange — SIXTEEN INCH, NOT FIFTEEN; three apertures 515.5 × 403.0 at r 55, equal; rear overhang 773 ± 22; rake 17.75 mm/m; ride drop at x=0 47.9 mm; roof at the rear axle 1983.5 (n = 230 mesh samples); counter slab 107.0 thick, 321.0 deep, brass nosing at CNT_NOSE_F 0.1860 of its outer edge; hubcap ⌀274.0 with an ⌀86.9 badge; 65 bobble-fringe balls per aperture at r 9 mm, 26 mm pitch — INFERRED, each ball is ~2 px. Colour references for the painter: RED (196,49,36), CREAM (206,208,200), strip yellow (172,144,17), mural RED (159,45,22) / ORANGE (163,66,21) / GOLD (172,119,24). ⚠ INK (72,46,6) is CONSTRUCTED, not measured — the measured median is (91,59,7) and is blur-limited; do not caption it as measured. UNMEASURED and to be stated if the sheet is ever printed as a plate: the absolute roof height, and the entire off side (SPEC grade E — never photographed).

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — ESTIMATES — get quotes; none of these came off the machine. 150 ml flint bottle plus closure at 10k: $0.42–0.65. Five-colour direct screen print on glass: $0.55–0.95, plus a one-off five-screen set-up of a few hundred dollars amortised over the first run. Co-packed fill of a validated salsa: $0.90–1.40. Landed $1.90–3.00 against $14 retail = 79–86 % gross. The refill is better: roughly $1.10 of product into a bottle you already sold, at $6 = about 82 %, with no packaging cost at all and a visit attached to it. Realistic attach for hot sauce at a taqueria counter is the best in the category — call it 4–8 % of tickets — and the refill converts a one-time buyer into a monthly one. The crate is the cheapest merchandising fixture you will ever buy. This is the item in the portfolio that actually pays.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — This week, with no manufacturing at all. Print the true-ortho side elevation at 90 mm wide, wrap it as a paper band round an off-the-shelf 150 ml bottle, fill it from the kitchen, and stand a dozen on the counter with a hand-lettered card: ¿TRAE SU ENVASE? $14 — RECARGA $6. Run it four days and COUNT: attach rate, and how many people ask about the refill. That is a real number off real customers before anyone spends a cent on glass. In parallel, $150 to one rotulista for one painted panel over the same printed elevation — one week, one object, and it is the asset the entire concept hangs on. Two things in his hand by Friday, both of them lookable-at.

### DIEZ Y MEDIA  ·  screen 8.17/10

**REGISTER** textile and three-dimensional, polychrome, hand-made, beautiful-not-rigorous, food-and-hand-off. The one object in the programme that is pretty before it is correct, and correct anyway.  
**ARTEFACT** A servilleta for tortillas. 450 × 450 mm, natural cotton manta, hand-hemmed, hand-embroidered in six cotton floss colours by a named taller in Puebla or Oaxaca. Six colours drawn from the measured mural palette. Sold with a swing tag that states the departure. Retail $58–68 hand-stitched; a machine-embroidered volume version at $32.

> The cloth the tortillas arrive in, embroidered with the roof lid's own flower ring at its measured count — ten full heads and one half head, cut by the hem exactly as it is cut by the strip.

A cloth for tortillas. In Mexico they come to the table wrapped in one of these; it is washed, folded, kept, inherited and given at weddings. It is the most-kept textile object in the food culture we are working in and this programme has proposed no textile at all.

What is embroidered is the roof lid's own mural — not a version of it, THE COUNT OF IT. Ten full flower heads and one half head cut by the right strip, exactly as the board carries them. Each rosette with EIGHT petals and one pale dot per petal. Eight, not twelve: the angular profile stacked over seven heads has its dominant harmonic at k = 8 with power 42.6 against k = 12 at 6.3, and six of the seven heads give eight individually. A designer redrawing this by eye draws twelve petals and nine heads — which is exactly what the record did, until the owner counted them off the photograph himself and answered ten.

And the half head runs off the hem. It is cut, on the cloth, the way it is cut on the board. Every instinct in a studio says centre the ring and complete the flower. We don't. That is the whole object, and it is the answer to the charge that eleven concepts produced nothing a person would call beautiful rather than rigorous: this is beautiful first, and the rigour is in the place nobody looks.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — NOTHING. This needs no 3D model and I am not going to fake a dependency to make the concept look better. The asset is `lid_gen.py` — measured ARTWORK, not geometry: HEADS_UV with ten entries, HEADS_PARTIAL_UV with the one half head, N_LOBE = 8 and N_DOT = 8 both marked "measured", DOT_SZ 0.118 of the head's outer radius, and a palette read in situ off a rectified board over 2,569,748 pixels. The 3D model contributed the rectification that made that measurement possible and nothing after it. One discipline must be reproduced in the stitch chart: the board's own aspect is uncertain to ±0.09 across three methods (1.520 ± 0.014 / 1.654 [1.591, 1.703] / 1.82), which is why every shape in that file is placed in board-normalised (u,v) with SEPARATE u and v radii. Chart it the same way and the ring survives whichever aspect turns out to be right.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — Four, and I am keeping all of them. (1) EMBROIDERY CANNOT REPLICATE A PAINTING. Floss quantises the palette and the stitch has a minimum feature size, so the scrollwork goes and the rosettes stay. That is a real departure from SPEC §10.10's hard bar and it belongs printed on the swing tag, not buried in a deck. (2) IF WE DRAW FOLK EMBROIDERY OURSELVES AND HAVE IT STITCHED CHEAPLY OFFSHORE, THIS IS EXACTLY THE APPROPRIATION THE FIELD CALLS OUT, and it will be called out, in public, by people who are right. Commissioning a real taller and crediting them by name is the only honest version — and it triples the unit cost, puts lead times at eight to fourteen weeks, and means every piece differs. (3) The cash cycle is bad: you pay the taller up front and sell across a season. You will sell out before Christmas and disappoint people who wanted one. (4) The commercial one, stated plainly: A $58 NAPKIN SELLS TO WELL UNDER ONE PER CENT OF TICKETS. It does not pay rent. It sits on the counter to make the $14 bottle look like nothing and to be photographed. If he wants volume it has to be the machine version at $32, and then it is a nice napkin rather than this.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — HEADS_UV = 10 full flower heads, plus HEADS_PARTIAL_UV = 1 half head cut by the right strip — both read live out of `lid_gen.py`. N_LOBE = 8 and N_DOT = 8, marked "measured" in source: the angular V profile at 0.95 R stacked over 7 heads gives k = 8 at power 42.6 against k = 12 at 6.3, and 6 of the 7 heads give 8 individually. DOT_SZ = 0.118 of the head's outer radius. The six floss colours, all measured in situ on the rectified board (n = 2,569,748 px, median (139,49,21), HSV sat median 0.835, hue median 18.4°): GROUND (55,26,19), GROUND_HI (74,38,26), RED (159,45,22), ORANGE (163,66,21), GOLD (172,119,24), PALE (184,150,76). ⚠ DO NOT USE PEACE (198,172,122) as a measured colour — it is CONSTRUCTED; the measured value is (174,133,73). Board aspect carries an honest bar of ±0.09, so chart in board-normalised (u,v) with separate u and v radii. Three menu strips — left, right, top; NO BOTTOM STRIP, confirmed twice — which is why the ring is cut on one side only.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — ESTIMATES, and hand-embroidery pricing is stitch-count driven, so three quotes with an actual chart in hand. Hand-embroidered, six colours, 450 mm square, 300 units: $16–26 landed → $58–68 retail = 60–73 % gross. Machine-embroidered volume version: $7–10 landed → $32 retail = 69–78 %, far better stock behaviour and a considerably worse object. Run BOTH: the hand piece as the counter's single aspirational thing and the price anchor that makes everything else read as cheap, the machine piece as the line that actually turns. Reorder on the machine version is genuine — they stain, they wear, they get given away at Christmas — and a second colourway costs a sample and nothing else: the mural's vignette palette, PINK (206,118,124) / GREEN (96,128,54) / CREAMV (232,224,198), is already measured and is the food half of the board.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — No manufacturing, no tooling, this week. Print the ring from `lid_gen.py` at 1:1 on a 450 mm sheet in the six measured colours, lay it under a plain cotton napkin on a table with hot tortillas folded into it, photograph it at the counter, and put the photograph in his hand. One image, one sentence, multiple choice. The test word is BEAUTIFUL — because his own record carries "the emotional bar that sits ABOVE clinical accuracy", and a programme this cold has not once been judged against it. If he says yes, commission ONE piece from ONE taller for about $40 and show him the actual cloth in six weeks. (For the record, and because F92 is a live carrier: this does not act on the Playa hero and does not re-propose playa_env.py. It borrows the sentence, not the deliverable.)

### MUY DESPACIO (VERY SLOWLY)  ·  screen 7.07/10

**REGISTER** child — hand-drawn cartoon line in the owner's own locked style, with the people and the hand-off carried on the card's reverse. The first thing in this programme made for the audience he actually named.  
**ARTEFACT** Twelve kiss-cut vinyl stickers, 75 mm on the long side, die-cut tight to the vehicle's silhouette, 4-colour process plus a white underbase on white matte PVC. Plus one route card: DL (105 × 210 mm), 350 gsm uncoated warm stock, 2 inks — measured menu-strip yellow (172,144,17) and a dark brown — printed both faces, with twelve kiss-cut landing panels. One sticker given free with a child's food, one per visit. This is F18, the register's oldest live row, finally shipped.

> The die-cut sticker he asked for in rev 44, made into twelve — the same bus at twelve stops on the journey it actually made — so a family comes back twelve times to finish the card.

Twelve stickers and one card to keep them on. Each sticker is the same vehicle, die-cut tight, drawn in the style he locked himself: vector line and flat colour, shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset. What changes across the twelve is the bus. It starts green in Mexico City, missing three of its windows, three thousand dollars. It goes very slowly through the Yucatán. It gets its roof cut open. It arrives red at Playa del Carmen on 6 February 2006 with the lids up, the tail board propped and 118 lamps lit at 28.52 mm. It ends indoors in New York, in the cold, parked among the tables — a room that used to be a vehicle.

One sticker per child per visit. The card is a route card, not a game board: twelve landing panels, the stops named, the fare at the top. Twelve visits fills it. That is the entire commercial mechanism and it is worth far more than anything we could charge for a sticker.

And the card tells the truth in a child's words. Five of the twelve stops have no photograph. Under those panels it says so: WE DON'T HAVE A PICTURE OF THIS PART. WE DREW IT. One line, read aloud by a parent. That is the house voice made warm instead of austere — which is the criticism this whole round exists to answer — and it is the only children's object I have ever seen that admits a tolerance.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Decisive, and it is the keystone item's first real output. The model is the reason twelve drawings are one bus: one mesh, one silhouette, twelve poses — lids, tail board, hatch, counter — moved rather than redrawn, so the proportions hold across the set where every VW bus cartoon ever printed drifts. And the Grease Pencil Line Art pass emits a CLOSED PATH, which is the die line: the drawing and the cut are the same file. That is the papel-picado insight — a cut sticker and a cut banner are the same operation — and it is the strongest technical argument yet for building the line pass. What the model does NOT give, and the card says so: the green livery, the half-cut roof, the Yucatán states.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — Three, none of them sanded off. (1) IT BREAKS HIS OWN SCENE LOCK. He locked "nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado." The stickers obey it exactly; the CARD does not — it carries places, dates, and on its reverse a person: the woman standing in the freshly cut roof opening in ref_workshop.jpg, redrawn in line. My defence is rule 34, that the lock's object is the sticker and the card is a different object. He can reject that in four words and I would not argue. (2) FIVE OF THE TWELVE STATES ARE INVENTION PRINTED FOR CHILDREN. Rule 11 forbids transferring livery from the green frames, so the green bus, the Yucatán and the cut-roof states are drawings, not records. Putting the admission on the card is my answer; to a restaurateur it may read as an apology printed on a gift. (3) IT SHIPS UNDER AN OPEN HOLD AND COMPOSES THE EMBLEM OUT. F18's trigger is "build it after the model is done", and by the project's own standard the model is never done — the emblem is his ninth rejection and still ~0.09 short of its own control ceiling. Every sticker here either composes the VW roundel out of frame or draws it at a size where it cannot be judged. Declared, not routed around silently.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — 118 festoon lamps at a realised 28.52 mm pitch — I watched this print: BULB_X0 −1.6368 → BULB_X1 1.7000 over BULB_PITCH 0.0286 gives 117 segments and 118 balls. ⚠ THIS CORRECTS THE REV-76 BRIEF'S "115", which is a photograph-side FFT inference about a 3.50 m rail, not the built count. Also: three serving apertures 515.5 × 403.0 mm, corner radius 55, THREE, EQUAL (BAY_W 0.5155, Z_SILL 1.3720, Z_HEAD 1.7750, BAY_R 0.0550, all read live); body 4065 × 1750 mm; wheelbase 2400; tyre ⌀664.9 on a ⌀439.6 flange; RED (196,49,36); CREAM (206,208,200); menu-strip yellow (172,144,17). Historical, SPEC §0.1 grade S: green, $3,000, "missing three of its windows", opened 6 February 2006. ⚠ THE NOLITA ADDRESS IS CONTESTED — SPEC §7 says 251 Elizabeth Street; a photograph read at 9× says 267. DO NOT PRINT EITHER until it is re-read. The tail board is printed as a disagreement (built 38.0°, photographed 28.0°) or not at all. The forbidden pillar asymmetry is not used anywhere; the three holes are drawn equal because the measurement says they are.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — ESTIMATES, not machine figures — three quotes needed before any of this is printed. Kiss-cut vinyl, 4C + white underbase, 75 mm, 10,000 per design across twelve designs: $0.05–0.09 each landed. Route card, DL, 350 gsm uncoated, 2 PMS both faces, 20,000 run: $0.06–0.10. Cost per family: about $0.12 on the first visit, about $0.06 every visit after. Direct revenue: zero. The return is footfall, and it is the only honest way to buy footfall — attach on a free kids' item is effectively 100 % among families, and a twelve-panel card is twelve reasons to come back. Total first-year outlay across all sites is on the order of $2,400, which is cheap enough to be an experiment rather than a bet. Reorder is continuous, and the series reissues annually with no new tooling: series 2 is the 229 named parts, series 3 is the food. If family repeat visits do not move, kill it and you are out less than one week of napkins.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — ONE sticker, in his hand this week. Render the Playa state — red, lids up, tail board propped, bulbs lit — through the line pass, flat-colour it, cut it at 75 mm on a vinyl sheet at any local printer, and hand it to him with the card front on 350 gsm. Then ask the question nobody in 32 revisions has asked, as one sentence with the object attached: DOES THIS SHIP NOW, OR DOES IT WAIT FOR "THE MODEL IS DONE"? He has been asked what done means for the render four times. He has never been asked what done means for the sticker, and that trigger is what has kept the project's original deliverable unbuilt since rev 44.

## FREE DIRECTOR 8 — the record itself

### LO QUE HAY DETRÁS  ·  screen 7.07/10

**REGISTER** child — and polychrome: five measured inks against the programme's two. CHILDREN'S LINE (F331).  
**ARTEFACT** A6 backing card, 105 × 148 mm, warm uncoated 300 gsm, carrying one kiss-cut die-cut vinyl sticker: the bus at 120 mm long (1:33.9 against the measured 4065 mm body). Five spot inks plus a white underbase on matte white vinyl. Three closed cut paths inside the outline.

> The die-cut sticker he has been owed since rev 44 — with the three serving holes cut clean through, so whatever is behind it is what the bus is serving.

Five spot inks, every one read out of source: RED 196,49,36 · CREAM 206,208,200 · mural GOLD 172,119,24 · strip yellow 172,144,17 · mural GROUND 55,26,19.

THE MOVE: the three serving apertures are cut THROUGH. Their waste lifts with the matrix, so the bus has three real holes in it — 515.5 × 403.0 mm at r 55, three of them, EQUAL, because the mesh says equal. Stick it on a window and the street is what the bus is serving. On a lunchbox, your lunch. On a bottle, the drink. A child finds this in about four seconds and then goes hunting for something better to put behind it. That is the joy, and it costs nothing to print.

It obeys his lock exactly. Viewpoint 18° front three-quarter from the serving side, flank chosen — AUDIT_rev43 §5 proves face and flank exclusive. Nothing but the bus, die-cut tight. The sun is NOT a rayed disc: the vehicle already carries eleven radial bursts and is its own light source. The wheels are drawn by hand, because he said he liked how they were drawn in the earlier cartoon version.

Card back, for the parent — the half that families keep: 2400 wheelbase · tyre ⌀664.9, NOT a 6.40-15 · and OFF SIDE — GRADE E — NEVER PHOTOGRAPHED. The three holes you can see through are the only three things on this bus we are certain of.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Real and specific. The cut path is line_pass.py's Grease Pencil Line Art → SVG output, which shipped at rev 77 and delivers the first half of his own locked style — vector line from the 3D asset. The three apertures' rectangle, radius and equality come off the mesh (BAY_W 0.5155, Z_HEAD−Z_SILL 0.4030, BAY_R 0.0550), and r55 is independently corroborated in the photograph at ≈57 mm. BUT THE SECOND HALF OF HIS LOCK CANNOT BE MET TODAY: there is no normal pass and no AO pass (LEDGER_rev77 §3), so 'shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset' is owed, not built. Either the AO pass gets built first or the sticker ships flat and the card says so. And the spec's die-cut rule 'bridge the underbody with the cast shadow' rests on optics-6, an OPEN finding about whether the catcher's alpha survives the alpha-over; one T1_CATCH=0 A/B settles it and it has never been run.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — THE DIE-CUT VW BUS SILHOUETTE IS THE MOST EXHAUSTED GRAPHIC OBJECT IN THE FIELD — flower power, van-life, 'Adventure Awaits'. Ours is measured and none of theirs is, but a child cannot see a tolerance. If the drawing is bad this is a van-life decal with holes in it. Second: the three holes remove the strongest part of the flank and children will tear it at the pillars — and I may not defend those pillars with a width, because that figure is retracted at 0.67σ. I am cutting through the one part of this vehicle the project has forbidden itself to measure. Third: F330 fired the trigger on THE MODEL, not on the emblem. F191 stands. At 1:33.9 the nose roundel is ⌀8.3 mm and I am composing it IN, not out, at a size where nine rejections cannot be resolved. Rev 77 observed the VW badge traces cleanly and is legible in line — but that was the HUBCAP badge, one object, square-on, at one scale. The nose roundel at 18° has never been traced. Declared, not routed around.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The cheapest object in the programme. Kiss-cut vinyl on a printed card, 500 units at five spots plus white, is a low four-figure print job with a standard die. The proof costs a sheet of paper and a scalpel. The only real engineering is the cut path, and the machine that emits it already exists.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Print one at 120 mm on the office printer, cut the three holes with a scalpel, stick it on the restaurant window and photograph what shows through. That is the whole idea, proven, this week, for nothing. Attach that photograph to the two questions his own spec left open and nobody has asked: the papel picado (he deferred it to 'when the sticker is actually being built' and F330 has now made that true), and the cab door at 49°, which opens a 0.306 m void that only a PERSON fills, against a locked scene that says 'nothing but the bus'.

### LA CHAROLA 515.5 × 403.0  ·  screen 6.87/10

**REGISTER** three-dimensional and food-and-hand-off — a service object used in the room, not a print carrier. ADULT LINE (F331), and it is what the children's sticker is handed to you on.  
**ARTEFACT** A food-service tray whose plan IS the serving aperture at 1:1: 515.5 × 403.0 mm, corner radius 55 mm, 18 mm rim. Vitreous enamel on 1.0 mm pressed steel (four colours plus a gold rim band), or digitally printed melamine for the short run. Made in threes, because there are three apertures and they are equal.

> A tray that is the serving hatch at 1:1, so the food arrives on the exact shape of the hole it came through.

The brand's whole transaction passes through one rectangle: 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55, three of them, EQUAL. That rectangle has never been made into anything. Make it the tray.

FACE: the five food vignettes and the arched words off the Playa lid menu strips, redrawn at tray scale from lid_gen.py's measured layout, in their measured order and at their measured v-fractions — two bolillos with green sprigs, the torta with pink meat and green garnish, the tall pale glass and an orange, the pale tostada with magenta filling, the pink prawn. GOURMET · TACOS · & · TORTAS · FRESH · JUICES, every word on a shallow upward arch because _arched() says they arch. Colours PINK 206,118,124 · GREEN 96,128,54 · CREAMV 232,224,198 · STRIP 172,144,17 · INK 72,46,6 — and set in 4 pt on the rim, the tray admits that INK is CONSTRUCTED: the measured median is 91,59,7 and is blur-limited.

RIM: one gold band at 18.6 % of the rim's depth — CNT_NOSE_F = 0.1860, the brass nosing off the real counter, hue ≈32°, sat ≈0.86. The tray carries the brass line, so when it crosses the counter it crosses its own edge.

At the foot, three small lines that are NOT LEGIBLE — '?ANO M?' / '??O?A?' / 'Torta?' — drawn as illegible small type, because that is exactly what the photograph gives and nothing more.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — HONEST AND SMALL, and I am not going to inflate it. The model contributes one rectangle, one radius, and the fact that there are three and they are equal — 515.5 × 403.0 × r55 × 3. That is its entire contribution. Everything on the face came off photographs through lid_gen.py, not off the mesh. It is enough, because that rectangle is the one number the brand's entire transaction physically passes through, and no photograph could have given it: the taper 0.507/0.516/0.526 was measured off frames twice and retired twice, and only the mesh settles that the three bays are equal. SPEC 10.10 DEFENCE, MADE EXPLICITLY: the artwork bar's object is the MODEL. A tray is a derivative graphic and a different object (rule 34 — a requirement inherits its object). This re-draws the strips at a scale where they are legible for the first time; it does not move the model's artwork and does not claim to be the vehicle.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — IT PUTS A DRAWN TACO NEXT TO A REAL ONE. If the drawing is better than the food the tray is a liability; if the food is better the drawing looks cheap. Only he can judge that and he will judge it in public, on a table, every day. Second: enamel scratches. In eight weeks of service the vignettes will be scarred — patina if you like it, vandalism if you don't. This is the first object where his two standing positions physically collide: SPEC §3's WEATHERED lock against his own 'I want this to look like new. Enhanced from the photo.' It collides on a tray in a dining room, not in a probe. Third: 515.5 × 403.0 is larger than the common 457 × 356 mm (18 × 14 in) service tray and may not fit his racks, his stacks or his dishwasher — UNMEASURED. Nobody has measured Tacombi's service equipment and I will not pretend otherwise. Fourth, and I am keeping it: at nominal aperture size the tray cannot actually pass through the aperture. It is the shape of the hole it does not fit through. Cut 6 mm off and the joke dies; keep it and the tray is a small, deliberate lie about its own dimension. I would keep it and print the dimension on the rim.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Melamine, digitally printed, 100 units: a few hundred dollars and no tooling. Vitreous enamel on pressed steel needs a press tool and is a real capital line — do that only after the melamine run has survived a month of service. The artwork is a redraw of a layout that is already measured to two decimal places of board height, so the design cost is drawing time, not research.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Print the face at 1:1 on paper, spray-mount it to 5 mm foam board, cut it to 515.5 × 403.0 with r 55 corners, and put a real plate of tacos on it on his counter. One large-format print, this week. Then hand it through the hatch and watch whether it fits — that single act settles the fourth risk in front of him instead of in a document.

### RUIDO  ·  screen 5.53/10

**REGISTER** people, hand-drawn, beautiful-not-rigorous — the Posada/TGP broadside's economics, not its iconography. ADULT LINE (F331).  
**ARTEFACT** Eight broadsides, 280 × 430 mm, ONE ink each, risograph or letterpress on coloured uncoated stock. Given away or sold for a few dollars in the room. Eight blocks, eight papers, no fifth colour anywhere.

> Eight one-colour sheets of the seven people this project has only ever recorded as measurement error — and the hand-off. The bus appears on none of them.

Seven human beings stand in this project's own reference photographs. In 76 revisions the record has named every one of them only as contamination. Draw them, by hand, and caption them in the record's own words.

1 · EL DE LA CAMISA ROJA — the young man leaning on the open cab door, eating. 'A man stands directly in front of the front wheel — every rev 8 attempt to measure the front arch locked onto his red shirt instead.' Red on cream.
2 · EL COCINERO — white cap, white apron carrying the Señor Tacombi script. '37 % of the VARIANCE is the man and the surfaces he occludes and no dressing can produce it.'
3 · LA NIÑA — the small child in the magenta puffer coat, from behind, bumper height, looking up at the bus. 'Three of its 106 points were on a child's hair — residuals −16.7, −16.1, −13.6 px.' Magenta on grey. THIS IS THE SHEET: his only stated audience is children at the restaurant, there is one in his own photograph, and the record cleaned her out. The headline moved 43 % when it did.
4 · LA MUJER DE LA MESA — chin on her hand, waiting for food. Caption: 'UNMEASURED.' The record never touched her at all.
5 · LA QUE LO ABRIÓ — the woman standing in the freshly cut roof, both hands on the lid, the bus still green. 'I figured out how to cut open the Tacombi.'
6 · EL MESERO — reaching for eight white plates.
7 · EL VENDEDOR — 'a pale band turned out to be the vendor's shirt.'
8 · LA ENTREGA — two hands and a plate crossing one gold rule. Gold on maroon.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — NOTHING, and that is the concept. There is no human geometry anywhere in this tree — no person, figure, mannequin, proxy or silhouette in any .py file. The model cannot make these and never will, which is precisely why the programme deleted people eleven times out of eleven. The model contributes exactly one thing, on sheet 8: a single gold rule at 18.6 % of the counter's outer edge (CNT_NOSE_F = 0.1860), with the slab top at 1.2540 m above ground. One constant. Everything else is a hand and a photograph. In a programme where eleven of eleven concepts deleted the people, this one deletes the vehicle. F92 NAMED, NOT ACTED ON: none of these sheets is the Playa hero, none builds or lights the terrace, none touches playa_env.py — F92 is RULED-rev57b 'DEPRIORITISED, NOT CANCELLED' and its carriers say do not act on it either. What this takes from F92 is one sentence, quoted on the colophon: 'the emotional bar that sits ABOVE clinical accuracy.' It has been in the register since rev 43 and no artefact in this programme has ever been made against it.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — THESE ARE REAL, IDENTIFIABLE PEOPLE PHOTOGRAPHED IN A RESTAURANT WHO DID NOT CONSENT TO BEING DRAWN AND SOLD. The child especially. Drawing rather than photographing does not dissolve that — a recognisable portrait is a recognisable portrait, and a commercial use of a minor's likeness in New York is the thing that gets a letter. The mitigations are real and partial: identification carried by posture and clothing rather than face; no names; sheet 3 from behind exactly as the photograph has her, so no face exists to reproduce. It is still a risk, and it is the reason the set is worth making — the alternative is the habit that made this programme cold. SECOND RISK, and it is the one that would actually kill it: THE CAPTIONS ARE BITTER. '37 % of the variance is the man' said of your own cook can read as contempt. The set works only if the sequence is affectionate and sheet 8 lands; if the tone slips by ten degrees it is a designer sneering at a record it wrote itself. THIRD: Posada is one of the two or three most exhausted Mexican graphic forms. The guardrail is that we take his economics — one block, coloured stock, given away the day it happened — and not one calavera. No skull on any sheet, ever.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The cheapest way into a new register in this whole programme. Risograph: eight sheets, one colour each, 200 of each, is a few hundred dollars and a week of an illustrator's time. Letterpress from photopolymer is a few thousand. No pipeline work, no render, no Blender time at all — it does not compete with the machine for the four cores.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — ONE SHEET. Sheet 3, LA NIÑA, risographed magenta on grey, this week. One drawing, one colour, one afternoon. Put it in his hand and ask him nothing — he has been told for seventy-six revisions that the child in his photograph was noise, and this is the first object that disagrees. If it lands, the other seven are the same afternoon repeated.

## BENCH PROMOTION — LA COMBI CERRADA

### BUENAS NOCHES, COMBI  ·  screen 8.6/10

**REGISTER** child — and time-based: the artefact is in two states and the viewer changes it by turning off a light  
**ARTEFACT** A die-cut vinyl sticker, 200 mm long (1:20.3), kiss-cut tight to the shut silhouette on white gloss vinyl. Screen-printed: THREE colours — RED #C43124, CREAM #CED0C8, and a warm dark for line — PLUS one phosphorescent (strontium aluminate) pass carrying the three apertures, the galley strip and all 118 drip-rail lamps. Sold or given on a printed backing card that carries the figures.

> In daylight it is a bus shut for the night. Turn the light out and all 118 bulbs come on.

Daylight: the bus shut. Red flank, cream lower body, three pale glazed panels where the serving hatches are, a row of dull pearl bulbs along the drip rail, nothing projecting past the sheet metal. Lights out: the three panels and every one of the 118 lamps come on and the bus you thought was closed is open.

That pale daylight state is not a compromise, it is the measured truth. probe_rev71_bulbs.py records that the festoon 'renders unlit pearl white' in every photoreal frame while both in-service photographs show it lit and warm. The sticker gets both states. The render has never managed either.

Scale is decided by the phosphor, not by taste. At 90 mm the 28.52 mm lamp pitch lands at 0.63 mm and a glow deposit — thick, coarse, needing volume to charge — will bridge into a smear. At 200 mm it is 1.40 mm. So 200 mm. Aperture 25.4 × 19.8 mm, r 2.71. Hubcap badge 4.28 mm.

The nose roundel does not appear: a side elevation sees it edge-on. The hubcap badge appears four times, and F333 measured that the mark he has rejected nine times traces cleanly and legibly in line — while STATE.md still prints 'hubcap badge is SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY'. So it will draw, and we do not know it is right. That sentence goes on the backing card.

Children's line (F331), fired by F330.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The die-cut path IS the line pass — a closed cut path is what a cutter wants and what Grease Pencil Line Art emits, which is the technical argument F18 has been waiting for since rev 44. The model also supplies the exact lamp stations and aperture geometry that set the print size, and the four hubcap badges at 4.28 mm, which nothing else in this project can draw at all.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — THREE, and I am not filing any of them off. (1) It contradicts an owner-LOCKED scene. F18 reads 'nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado.' A bus asleep has no sun. This is therefore a COMPANION to F18, not F18, and it must be presented as a scene departure he has to approve — not slipped past as though it were the sticker he ordered. (2) The phosphor is physically chalky and cannot be invisible, so the three apertures ARE pale in daylight whether we like it or not; and standard strontium aluminate glows green-cyan around 520 nm while the galley strip is measured warm at (1.000, 0.918, 0.790) — the night bus will glow GREEN, not warm. That is a real departure from the measured light and no ink fixes it. (3) A five-year-old may simply prefer the open, working, colourful bus to a shut one. The glow turns the shut state into a game rather than a subtraction, but that is a bet, not a measurement.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — 118 lamps at 28.52 mm realised pitch. COMPUTED AND WATCHED: at 200 mm the bus is 1:20.3, lamp pitch 1.40 mm, aperture 25.4 × 19.8 mm r 2.71, hubcap badge 4.28 mm; at 90 mm the pitch is 0.63 mm, which is why 90 mm is refused. Apertures 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55, three, equal. Hubcap ⌀274.0, badge ⌀86.9, tyre ⌀664.9, rim flange ⌀439.6 (sixteen inch, not fifteen). Nose roundel ⌀280.0 at 1017 mm above ground — edge-on in this view, absent. Body 4065 × 1750 mm. Galley strip emission (1.000, 0.918, 0.790) at GAL_LUM 3.04, quoted as the warm target the phosphor CANNOT hit. RED #C43124, CREAM #CED0C8.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Four screen passes plus a kiss-cut die: a few hundred dollars for the die and a short run. Expensive for a sticker, cheap for an object families keep. Glow vinyl is off-the-shelf; the die is the only tooling.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Print the DAYLIGHT state only — 200 mm, side elevation, three colours — on any home printer, cut it out with a scalpel, and stick it on his fridge. Hand him a scrap strip of off-the-shelf glow vinyl beside it so he can carry both states in one hand. Zero cost, this week, and it puts the one question that matters to him in his hand: does the bus asleep make you want to keep it.

### LA ÚLTIMA LUZ  ·  screen 5.83/10

**REGISTER** packaging, and food-and-hand-off — the artefact only exists in the second a person passes hot food across the counter  
**ARTEFACT** A printed kraft takeaway bag. SOS style, natural unbleached kraft, flexo, THREE colours: the measured body RED #C43124 (196,49,36), the menu-strip yellow #AC9011 (172,144,17), and the strip lettering ink #482E06 for type. Bag size is UNMEASURED and a design choice — it should be set by his existing stock, not by me; the artwork is drawn to a 300 mm face, which puts the bus at 1:13.6.

> The bus is shut on the bag, because what was inside it is now inside the bag.

Front face: the bus shut, side elevation, 300 mm long at 1:13.6. Red line, squat, nothing projecting, wheels down. The three serving apertures — 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55, three, equal, at the measured centres 0.6720 / 0.0470 / −0.5980 m — print at 38.0 × 29.7 mm, r 4.06, flooded in the menu-strip yellow. On brown board a warm yellow rectangle reads as a lit window. Three lit windows in a dark shut side. The 118 drip-rail lamps sit at 2.10 mm pitch along the roof edge.

Back face, one line: EL COMBI ESTÁ CERRADO. LO QUE HABÍA ADENTRO LO LLEVAS TÚ. (My Spanish; it needs a native pass before anything is printed.) The shut bus stops being a lament and becomes a receipt.

The gusset carries the food, read rather than invented: GOURMET · TACOS · & · TORTAS · FRESH · JUICES · CEVICHE · TOSTADAS · SHRIMP & FISH, set on the shallow upward arch the real strips use, and the three bottom lines the record could not read printed as NOT LEGIBLE. Colophon, 5 pt: the ink #482E06 is CONSTRUCTED, not measured — the measured median (91,59,7) is blur-limited. He will recognise that sentence and it is the reason to trust the rest.

This is the item that answers 'no food, no people': it cannot exist without both. Handed to everyone — kids and adults (F331).

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Partial, and I will not overstate it. The model gives the true side orthographic line and the exact aperture geometry and positions — nothing else can, since no photograph of this vehicle is orthographic. It does NOT need the glazed-shut geometry built: three rectangles at measured positions is all the print requires. Emptying OPEN_BAYS = (0,1,2) in t1_shell.py would make a renderable shut flank through the same side_glass() path that already builds glass_bay0/1/2_R on the off side, but this bag does not depend on it.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — Printing a SHUT bus on the bag handed over at the moment of purchase is counter-intuitive and a franchisee or GM may read it flatly as 'closed' and refuse it — that risk does not go away with a clever back-face line. Second, and physical: the kraft substrate's reflectance is UNMEASURED and yellow on brown board may simply not separate; if the drawdown fails, the fix is a white flood underbase, which changes the ink count, the price class and the whole plain-kraft argument. Third: this bag will get greasy. Grease turns kraft translucent unevenly, and unlike PAPEL DE GRASA that effect here is uncontrolled — it may read as damaged rather than lit.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Apertures 515.5 × 403.0 mm, corner radius 55, THREE, EQUAL (build ray-tests 'open serving apertures on +Y: 3'); centres BAY_CX 0.6720 / 0.0470 / −0.5980 m. COMPUTED AND WATCHED at a 300 mm face: 1:13.6, aperture 38.0 × 29.7 mm r 4.06, lamp pitch 2.10 mm. 118 lamps at 28.52 mm realised pitch — NOT 115; that figure is an FFT inference about a 3.50 m rail, not this model's count. Body 4065 mm long, 1750 mm wide. RED (196,49,36) #C43124; STRIP yellow (172,144,17) #AC9011; INK (72,46,6) #482E06 CONSTRUCTED, measured median (91,59,7). Footnote figure: 4.317 m open / 4.065 m shut, STATE.md — 252 mm of this bus is shop.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The cheapest printed object a restaurant buys. Three-colour flexo on natural kraft is standard stock work at every bag converter. The real cost is the minimum order — typically five figures of units — which is precisely why the first step must be a single hand-made proof and not a purchase order.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Make ONE bag by hand this week. Screen or even inkjet the front face onto a plain kraft blank, put a real taco in it, and look at it under the restaurant's own light with grease on it. That is the entire test and it costs nothing. If the three yellow rectangles do not glow on brown board, the concept is dead and we will know in an hour instead of after a plate charge.

### NO CIERRA  ·  screen 3.33/10

**REGISTER** motion — the programme's first piece of time-based work, and its first artefact whose subject is a refusal  
**ARTEFACT** A 9-second silent loop. 1080 × 1350 (his channels) and a 1920 × 1080 cut for the restaurant screens. MP4/GIF. Vector line drawn through line_pass.py, black on the measured cream #CED0C8, with one accent — the mural's own measured orange #A34215 — used on the lid and nowhere else. Two colours plus the ground. No sound: the family has no measured sound and inventing one is not available to us. Runtime 9 s, loops indefinitely.

> The bus tries to close, gets 21.1 degrees down, and the machine refuses by 0.9 mm. Nine seconds, on a loop.

The bus stands in true side elevation, drawn entirely in line off the model, and it tries to shut its roof.

The mural lid starts vertical at 90°. It comes down: 85, 80, 76 — the shipped angle, ticked — 72, 70, 69, 68.9. Then it stops. It does not shut. The drawing holds for two seconds and the machine's own sentence types itself across the frame at 6 pt mono, verbatim, because I watched it print: LID_W = 1.2806 m runs the roof aperture 0.9 mm PAST the roof edge. Cut to black: t1_shell.py:1343 — ZeroDivisionError: float division by zero. The lid opens again. Loop.

This is not a conceit. The board's width is derived from the angle it is propped at — LID_W = (LID_X0 − LID_X1) / LID_ASPECT / sin(LID_OPEN_DEG) — because the only observation we have of it is its foreshortened projection while open. Its width is a function of its openness. Shut, it has none. Seventy-seven revisions have measured a vehicle that has no closed state, and nobody noticed until somebody asked it to close.

Adult line (F331). It is allowed to be bleak because it is true and because the other two artefacts in this family answer it. The nose roundel never appears — a side elevation sees it edge-on — and the roof mural is hidden by the pose, not deleted, so SPEC 10.10's replication bar is untouched. This is not the Playa hero: no environment, no terrace, no playa_env.py.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — The model is the entire subject and nothing else could be. Only a parametric build can be ASKED to close and answer no; a photograph of this lid exists at exactly one angle. Seven of the eight elevations in the loop have never existed as an image, and the refusal text is the machine's own, not a caption I wrote.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — It is a film whose climax is a stack trace, and it is a public admission that the model has no representable closed state — a viewer can read that as 'they built it wrong for 77 revisions.' It contains no food, no person, and no open bus; its emotional content is failure, deliberately. It is the item in this family most likely to be rejected outright, and it puts developer error text on a brand channel. I am not softening any of that: if the sadness is going to be anywhere, it is here, alone, so that B and C can refuse it.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — WATCHED THIS SESSION: LID_W at T1_LIDDEG 90/85/80/76/72/70/69/68.9 = 1.1874 / 1.1919 / 1.2057 / 1.2237 / 1.2485 / 1.2636 / 1.2719 / 1.2727 m, all BUILD; T1_LIDDEG=68 REFUSES — 'LID_W = 1.2806 m runs the roof aperture 0.9 mm PAST the roof edge ... so W <= 1.2797 m'; T1_LIDDEG=30 REFUSES at 2.3748 m, 1095.1 mm past; T1_LIDDEG=0 → ZeroDivisionError at t1_shell.py:1343. Closing travel 90 − 68.9 = 21.1°. dims L=4.065 W=1.750 roof@rear-axle=1.9835 m. Colours from lid_gen.py / t1_mats.py: CREAM #CED0C8, mural ORANGE #A34215.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Eight Line Art bakes at ~2.3 s each (F332's measured bake time after the frame-range fix), drawn through the existing sheet.py, assembled in any timeline. No Cycles, no render queue, no farm. Sub-day of work with tooling that already shipped at rev 77. Distribution cost zero.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Run line_pass.py --view side at T1_LIDDEG = 90, 85, 80, 76, 72, 70, 69, 68.9 — all eight build, watched this session — draw them through sheet.py and hand him an 8-frame GIF with NO type on it at all. If the closing sequence does not hold his eye on its own, no words will save it. That is one afternoon.

## BENCH PROMOTION — TWELVE SHADOWS

### SOLO EN PLAYA  ·  screen 7.5/10

**REGISTER** three-dimensional  
**ARTEFACT** A working horizontal sundial. Dial plate 120 × 90 mm, 3 mm brass, hour lines and a twelve-row equation-of-time correction table engraved (diamond-drag or laser anneal — no ink anywhere on the object). Gnomon: the vehicle's TRUE side elevation, cut from 1.5 mm brass at 1:87 — Wiking dealer-model scale — 46.7 mm long and 22.8 mm tall at the roof over the rear axle (both DERIVED by dividing the measured 4065 mm and 1983.3 mm by 87), all edges radiused. It is mounted so that the ROOF LINE, not the body datum, stands at the latitude of Playa del Carmen. Boxed with a second dial plate cut for ≈40.7° N. One material, one colour, no printing.

> A pocket sundial whose gnomon is this exact bus in true orthographic profile, engraved for one latitude only, and therefore wrong in New York by an amount printed on the back.

On a horizontal dial the shadow-casting edge must lie parallel to the Earth's axis, tilted up at the latitude. Make that edge the bus's roof line and the whole object falls into place: the combi stands on the plate at 20.6°, tail high, like a vehicle climbing out of the sea, and the shadow of its roof tells the time.

Then the project's own honesty makes the piece. The roof line is not the body datum — it is raked, 17.75 mm/m applied and −18.1 mm/m measured off the mesh, about 1.02°. So the body sits at ≈19.6° while the style sits at 20.6°, and that 1.0° discrepancy is engraved on the underside next to the time error it produces, which is UNMEASURED until computed and will not be printed until it is. Beside it, twelve rows: the equation of time, month by month — a correction table is an admission that the instrument is wrong in a known way, which is this project's entire method rendered as an object you can hold.

The emblem is composed out by PROJECTION, not by choice: at 1:87 the Ø280 mm roundel is 3.2 mm and it is on the nose, which a side elevation does not show. F191 stands, untouched, declared. (Worth carrying: F333 found the badge traces CLEANLY in the line pass — the mark he has rejected nine times is not wrong in line.)

Back of the dial, engraved: ENGINE: NONE. TRANSMISSION: SOLD.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Total dependence, and it is the cheapest possible consumer of what rev 77 just built. The gnomon is a single closed cut path taken straight off `line_pass.py` through the `side` orthographic camera — the one ortho camera in the tree that FITS (5.90 → 4.0563 m against a 4.065 m body; front and rear still CLIP at 2.4406 m against a 3.132 m bbox top and are a known, narrow fix). No photograph can give a true orthographic profile — every reference frame is perspective — and no die-cast, LEGO set or T-shirt silhouette of a T1 in existence is dimensionally honest; they are all caricatures with the nose too round and the roof too tall. This is the first accurate one, and its accuracy is checkable by a stranger with a wristwatch, which is the rarest property any artefact in this programme could have.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — KEEPING THE DANGER, NAMED: this object is a clock that is wrong nearly everywhere on Earth, it does not work indoors, and it will mostly be bought indoors — in a New York dining room, at ≈40.7° N, where it is both unusable and mis-latitudinal. A customer can feel sold a broken thing. That is the concept and it is also, honestly, a way to lose a sale. Second and harder: it is FALSIFIABLE IN PUBLIC. Someone will take it into the street, set it in the sun and compare it with their phone. If the geometry, the latitude or the equation-of-time table is off by more than a few minutes, the object fails in the buyer's hand, on the first day, and there is nowhere to hide — no other item in this programme can be caught out by a stranger. Third: it is the family's only real trademark surface. A three-dimensional metal object cut to a Volkswagen T1 profile is closer to trade dress than any printed sheet; a lawyer looks at this one before it is tooled. Fourth: thin brass, a pointed nose and children in a restaurant — radius every edge, and if that softens the profile, say so rather than shipping a hazard. Fifth: the rev-76 bench already carries THE TOY THAT WAS NEVER MADE; this is adjacent to it and must not be allowed to quietly become it.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Body length 4065 mm and roof at the rear axle 1983.3 mm, divided by 87 → gnomon 46.7 × 22.8 mm (DERIVED, stated as DERIVED). Rake 17.75 mm/m applied, −18.1 mm/m measured off the mesh ≈ 1.02° — both engraved, because they disagree. Side orthographic camera 5.90 → 4.0563 m against the 4.065 m body (it fits); front/rear cameras clip 3.55 → 2.4406 m against a 3.132 m bbox top (they do not). Tyre Ø664.9, rim flange Ø439.6 (SIXTEEN INCH, not fifteen), hubcap Ø274.0, badge Ø86.9 — all present in the cut profile, all measured. Roundel Ø280.0 mm at 1:87 = 3.2 mm, on the nose, invisible in profile. UNMEASURED AND WILL NOT BE ENGRAVED UNTIL COMPUTED: the time error introduced by the 1.02° style offset. EXTERNAL, secondary, re-check before tooling: Playa del Carmen ≈20.6° N, Nolita ≈40.7° N, the solar disc's ≈0.5° angular diameter, and every value in the equation-of-time table.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Paper prototype: under $10, one afternoon. Short run, 100 units, laser-cut brass plate + gnomon, engraved, folded card box: roughly $18–30 a unit at that quantity, retail $65–85 — the highest unit cost and highest margin in the family, and the only genuinely premium object here that is neither a print nor a garment. Tooling for cast rather than cut: not worth it below ~1,000 units. Compute cost: one line-pass bake at 2.3 s (per F332's pinned frame range) plus an ephemeris and a spreadsheet. Audience line, per F331: ADULT (paid, kept) — and I am flagging deliberately that it is the adult-line object most likely to end up in a child's hands; it is NOT F18 and must not be allowed to stand in for F18's sticker, which is now a live build item under F330. LEAD WITH THIS ONE: it is the cheapest thing in the family to prove and the only one that proves itself.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Build the paper dial this week. Print the hour-line plate 1:1 on 300 gsm card, cut the bus gnomon from 1 mm greyboard with a scalpel off the line-pass outline, fold-tab it at the Playa latitude, take it outside at a known clock time, and photograph the shadow falling on an hour line beside a watch. Under $10. Hand him the card and the photograph together. It is a control as well as a prototype: if it reads the right time, the geometry, the latitude and the table are all right at once; if it does not, that is a finding and it costs $10 to have found it.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — False  ·  **id** `dream:twelve-shadows-2`

### LA JORNADA  ·  screen 6.5/10

**REGISTER** food-and-hand-off  
**ARTEFACT** A silent film. 3 min 30 s, 24 fps, 4K, ONE fixed camera that never moves, positioned inside the galley — the cook's view. Daylight from the roof opening and the three apertures crawls across the back wall and the stainless worktop across the whole of one day, sampled from the ephemeris at 5-minute intervals and interpolated. Everything measured is drawn in one warm grey. The ONLY colour in the film is the food, in the mural's own measured vignette palette: PINK (206,118,124) meat and shrimp, GREEN (96,128,54) garnish, CREAMV (232,224,198) plate and glass, STRIP yellow (172,144,17), INK (72,46,6) — labelled CONSTRUCTED, because it is. Adult-line derivative: six still frames at named minutes, A3, two colours.

> Thirteen hours inside the bus, in which the only things that move are the sun — measured — and a pair of hands passing food out through a hole, drawn by an actual human hand.

TWELVE SHADOWS said: never draw the vehicle, only what it casts. Go one step further. Stand INSIDE it, and the vehicle is all you can never see. Three rectangles of daylight and one big one from the cut roof slide across a white back wall, cross the measured material seam at x = −0.6140 where white becomes warm cream, narrow, tilt, thin, and go out. At the end the only light left is the warm fluorescent strip — emission (1.000, 0.918, 0.790) at strength 3.04 — because, as the record puts it, a serving hatch shows you its strip light. Then the lid comes down at its declared 76.0° and the film ends.

And about twenty times, a hand crosses the light and something is set down and taken away.

The hands and the food are the ONLY things in the film not computed. They are drawn by a person, in a visibly different line, and the head card says so: TODO LO QUE SE MUEVE DESPACIO ESTÁ MEDIDO. TODO LO QUE SE MUEVE RÁPIDO ESTÁ DIBUJADO A MANO. The sun moves at a rate we can measure. Hands do not.

Second head card, and it is the flaw at the centre of the most beautiful thing here: LA PROFUNDIDAD NO ESTÁ MEDIDA. The galley's Y depth is CHOSEN everywhere — the source says so verbatim — so the throw of the light onto that wall is a construction. It is stated on the film, not buried.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Heavy dependence, with one honest hole. Only the model holds the galley interior — 58 built objects, the plancha, the worktop at the measured z 1.5010, the back wall's measured material seam at GAL_SEAM_X = −0.6140, the warm-fluorescent practical — and only the model can compute how the roof opening (2034 × 1223.7 mm) and the three apertures (515.5 × 403.0, r 55) actually deform their light across a curved wall and a stainless slab as the sun moves. The record already measured which hole does the work: "the roof opening does all of it," and "the aperture surround is a matte white bobble fringe, not lamps." THE HOLE: the galley's DEPTH IS NOT MEASURABLE and is authored throughout, so the single most visible quantity in the film — how far the light throws — is a pose. That is printed on the film's own head card. What the model does NOT contribute is the half that matters emotionally: there is zero human geometry in this tree, no proxy, no hand, no scale figure. Do not invent one. Hire a person to draw them.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — This is the item that answers the critic's finding 4, and it carries four live dangers I am not removing. First, ETHICAL and unresolved: it renders thirteen hours of a man's shift as beautiful ambient light, and the man himself appears only as hands — we deleted the cook (we never modelled him) and now we are selling his working day as an artwork. In forty revisions this project has treated the real cook as measurement contamination: "a pale band turned out to be the vendor's shirt," "37 % of the variance is the man." If we do this, he gets a credit by name in the end card, or we do not do it. Second, FORMAL: hand-drawn arms next to machine-exact shadows can read as two films stapled together — and the only safe retreat, making the drawn line more machine-like, destroys the entire idea. There is no fallback; it either marries or it fails. Third, TECHNICAL AND STATED ON THE FACE: the depth is invented, so the film's central visual quantity is a construction. Fourth, DEPENDENCY: this needs an animator, a real cost the project has never carried, on a schedule it has never run. And F92 again: this uses Playa's sun, computed. F92's object is the photoreal Playa hero built with playa_env.py; this is a line-and-flat-colour film that uses no render path, no terrace and no playa_env — declared, not assumed. SPEC §10.10: no vehicle artwork appears at all, and the food is drawn, not replicated; the mural's palette is honoured by being the film's only colour.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Roof opening 2034 mm long × 1223.7 mm DERIVED, lid at 76.0° DECLARED inside a 68.9–78° bracket. Three apertures 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55, EQUAL, ray-tested through the shell. Back-wall material seam GAL_SEAM_X = −0.6140 (white 0.7300,0.7300,0.7355 aft; warm cream 0.7500,0.7040,0.6420 forward). Worktop top at the measured z 1.5010; plancha 0.560 × 0.780, top z 1.455; counter slab 107.0 mm thick, 321.0 mm deep, brass nosing at 18.6 % of its outer edge, gold at h≈32°/s≈0.86. Fluorescent practical emission (1.000, 0.918, 0.790) at GAL_LUM 3.04. Food palette PINK (206,118,124), GREEN (96,128,54), CREAMV (232,224,198), STRIP (172,144,17) — all MEASURED; INK (72,46,6) is CONSTRUCTED and captioned as such, its measured counterpart being (91,59,7) and blur-limited. Festoon 118 lamps at 28.52 mm pitch built — NOT the 115 in circulation, which is an inference about a 3.50 m rail. UNMEASURED, on the film's own head card: the galley's Y depth, everywhere. EXTERNAL: the ephemeris and ≈20.6° N.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Compute is cheap and I have checked why: do NOT render 5,040 photoreal frames on a four-core box at ~5 min each. Sample the sun at 5-minute intervals across one named date's daylight — roughly 150 positions, one silhouette/occlusion bake each at F332's measured 2.3 s per pinned-frame bake, call it ten minutes of machine time — and interpolate the polygons between them. The expensive half is the human one: ~20 hand-off events at ~1 s each ≈ 480 drawn frames, which is a real animator's month, $6–15k depending who. The six-frame A3 still set costs almost nothing on top and is the adult-line object. NAMING FINDING 11 DIRECTLY: a print series ALONE would have been the merely-competent version of this concept, so it is folded in as the derivative rather than promoted as its own idea. Audience line, per F331: the film is the CHILDREN'S line — free, playing in the room, no reading required; the still set is the ADULT line. Neither pretends to be the other, which is exactly what he ruled.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Two sheets of A4, this week, under $5. Compute the sun for a named date at 13:00 and at 17:30, bake the two shadow figures inside the galley, draw them flat in one grey, and print them side by side. On the second sheet only, have an actual person draw one forearm and one plate across the light in pencil, in ten minutes, without tracing anything. Hand him both sheets. He will see the whole argument in five seconds: the light has moved and it was measured; the arm has appeared and it was not.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:twelve-shadows-3`

### MEDIODÍA VERDADERO  ·  screen 5.67/10

**REGISTER** environmental  
**ARTEFACT** A 1:1 shadow, inlaid into the floor of the Nolita dining room. One colour — warm dark grey (70,62,50), playa_env.py's own measured sign ink, explicitly "NOT black" — plus a 40 % tint of the same colour for the disputed edges. Hand-cut stencil, two-pack floor enamel, matt trafficable seal (or epoxy-terrazzo inlay if the floor is ever lifted). The body's plan area alone is 4.065 × 1.750 = 7.11 m² (DERIVED); the cast shadow is larger by a throw that is UNMEASURED until the ephemeris is run. Plus one engraved brass plaque, 120 × 80 mm, two lines.

> The shadow this bus would have cast at solar noon on the day it opened in Playa, laid at 1:1 into the floor of the New York room where it will never see the sun again.

Solar noon, 6 February 2006, Playa del Carmen — the day it opened. Compute the sun for that instant, put it in the scene, take the ground shadow of the whole vehicle with the lids up, and lay it into the floor at 1:1, beside the bus that is standing right there: engineless, indoors, under a ceiling, permanently unable to cast it.

The piece has a fault line in it and the fault line is the point. The shadow of an OPEN bus is dominated by three panels, and all three of their angles are declared poses, not measurements: the tail board at 38.0° (photographed at 28.0°, unresolved since rev 62), the rear hatch at 64.0° (no frame shows it), the roof lid at 76.0° (bracket 68.9–78°). So the tail board is inlaid TWICE — 38.0° at full strength, 28.0° at 40 % tint — and the gap between those two edges is a piece of floor you can stand in. Seven revisions of unresolved disagreement, at full size, walked over by people eating tacos.

SPEC §10.10's artwork bar is met, not dodged: PAINT CASTS NO SHADOW. The mural, the script, the sunburst and the menu strips are absent for a physical reason, not an editorial one. F191 likewise: the roundel is 5 mm of relief on a vertical nose and casts nothing at noon — composed out by optics, declared here rather than cropped away quietly.

Plaque, line two: NO HAY NADIE EN ESTA SOMBRA. NUNCA MODELAMOS AL HOMBRE.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Total dependence, and of a kind nothing else can supply. Only the model holds a closed, watertight 1:1 silhouette of THIS vehicle with the lids up — 229 objects, 0 non-manifold edges on the body — so the occlusion outline is a single clean cut path. No photograph can give it: every reference frame is perspective, and studio.py's own measured verdict on the Playa set is "NO SUN … the SHADOWS ARE WARMER. A sun/skylight pair has the opposite sign by construction, so there is no sun/skylight pair," and "There is no sky in frame." In 77 revisions this project has never once computed a sunlit shadow of this bus. STATED HONESTLY: that capability does NOT exist in the tree today — I grepped it; there is no SUN lamp anywhere, only AREA lights and a shadow-catching cyclorama. It is one `bpy.data.lights.new(...,'SUN')`, one solar-disc angular diameter (≈0.5°, catalogue) and one ephemeris call — hours, not a research programme — but it must be built and watched, not assumed.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — THE RISK IS THE THING AND I AM NOT SANDING IT: a 1:1 dark bus-shaped stain on the floor of a working restaurant is a chalk outline at a crime scene, and it is a memorial to a vehicle that is standing three metres away still trading. Get the tone wrong and you have installed a funeral in a taqueria, permanently, in front of paying customers, where it cannot be quietly withdrawn. Second: it assumes physical access to the Nolita floor and a landlord's consent — the completeness critic's finding 9(c), UNESTABLISHED, and every 1:1 concept in this programme has been assuming it. Third: the sun is COMPUTED, never observed; the whole artefact rests on an ephemeris applied to a vehicle only ever photographed under a canopy, plus three angles the project has declared rather than measured. Fourth, prosaic and real: a dark inlay in a taqueria shows grease and needs a slip rating. Fifth, F92: this invokes Playa and must say so — F92 is RULED-rev57b "DEPRIORITISED, NOT CANCELLED" and its carriers say "do not act on it either." The rule-34 object test: F92's object is the photoreal PLAYA HERO RENDER built with playa_env.py's lateral-source rig. This is a floor inlay, uses no render, no terrace and no playa_env — it uses a sun playa_env explicitly refuses to contain. I believe that distinction holds; I am declaring it rather than relying on it silently, and if he disagrees the piece dies.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Body 4065 × 1750 mm; plan area 7.11 m² (DERIVED from those two). Roof at the rear axle 1983.3 mm (regression baseline 1983.5). Roof line measured −18.1 mm/m off the mesh against the applied rake constant 17.75 mm/m — both printed, because they differ. Tail board 38.0° built vs 28.0° photographed, chord 0.710 / 0.790 / 0.829 m (DO NOT AVERAGE THEM); TB_WIDTH 550 mm is a POSE CHOICE, upper bound ≤590 mm, NO lower bound. Rear hatch 64.0° DECLARED, angle NOT MEASURED, no frame shows it. Roof lid 76.0° DECLARED inside a 68.9–78° bracket; opening 2034 mm long × 1223.7 mm DERIVED. Three serving apertures 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55, EQUAL. 229 mesh objects, 366,490 faces, 0 non-manifold edges on the body. Ink (70,62,50). Opened 6 February 2006. UNMEASURED: solar altitude and azimuth (ephemeris; Playa del Carmen ≈20.6° N is EXTERNAL and secondary — re-check before anything is cut), shadow area, tail-edge separation. NOT USED: the retracted pillar figure.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Paper proof: ~$40, one afternoon (plot + tape + one photograph). Painted stencil version, one overnight closure, one sign-writer: ~$1,200–2,500 for a piece of that footprint — a rotulista is the right trade for it, which converts an appropriation risk into a collaboration credit. Terrazzo-epoxy inlay only if the floor is ever renewed: five figures, and not worth it as a retrofit. Compute cost is negligible — one SUN lamp, one silhouette bake, seconds, not the 5-minute-a-frame photoreal path. Audience line, per F331: ADULT by register, but it is the only piece in the programme a child physically stands inside. UNMEASURED and required before cutting: the sun's altitude and azimuth at that instant, the shadow's actual area, and the separation of the two tail-board edges.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Plot the outline at 1:1 on cheap builder's/kraft paper (or a taped template from the plotted file), lay it on any floor — a garage, a corridor, the yard — stand a person in it, photograph it from above, and hand him that one photograph with the two tail-board edges visibly separated. Under $40, two hours, in his hands this week. If he does not feel anything looking at it, the whole family dies cheap.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — False  ·  **id** `dream:twelve-shadows-1`
