## BENCH PROMOTION — THE FRONTIER ARCHIVE + THE INSIDE-NECK SYSTEM

### EL CARNET DE LA COMBI  ·  screen 6.67/10

**REGISTER** food-and-hand-off · child · packaging (a hand-stamped document)  
**ARTEFACT** Saddle-stitched booklet, passport format 125 × 88 mm (ICAO ID-3 — SECONDARY SOURCE, verify before print), 24pp, 100 gsm uncoated warm cream, cover 300 gsm board in the measured menu-strip yellow (172,144,17). TWO printed inks: black + measured red (196,49,36). A THIRD ink arrives by hand — a rubber stamp inked at the counter, so the object is unfinished until a person completes it. Eight right-hand pages carry a kiss-cut vinyl voucher that tears out on a micro-perforation.

> The vehicle's own customs carnet: the counterfoil stays in the book, the voucher is torn out and handed to you with the food — and the voucher is the die-cut sticker this project has owed since rev 44.

A customs carnet is a booklet of paired pages: a counterfoil that stays, a voucher torn out and surrendered at each border. That is also a taqueria ticket, and it is the shape this vehicle's life already has. Four crossings: Ciudad de México 1963 — verde, $3,000, missing three of its windows; the Yucatán; Playa del Carmen, 6 February 2006; Nueva York. Left page, the counterfoil, stays: a line elevation of the bus in that livery state, drawn by the line pass off one identical camera, with that state's figures. Right page, the voucher, goes: a kiss-cut vinyl sticker of the bus — cartoon with rendered depth, die-cut tight, nothing but the bus. That is F18, the register's oldest live row, finally shipped through the object it always wanted to be. A child tears one out; an adult reads what is left behind. Same booklet, two audiences — the audience conflict answered rather than argued.

Four pages are stamped NO CONSTA — does not appear in the record. The off side: grade E, never photographed. The registration: the plate surround is 330.0 × 168.2 mm and EMPTY, the digits schematic, the number unread. The New York address: SPEC says 251 ELIZABETH STREET, the photograph at 9× reads 267 — both printed, neither resolved. The roof height: OPEN AND UNMEASURED. And the VW roundel is an empty 280.0 mm circle marked NINE TIMES REJECTED — NOT ISSUED, which declares F191 on the artefact instead of quietly cropping it out.

One spread is the cook's view OUT through a 515.5 × 403.0 aperture at the instant of the hand-off, captioned GALLEY DEPTH IS NOT MEASURABLE — THIS INTERIOR IS A CONSTRUCTION.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Decisive, in three ways. (1) FOUR LIVERY STATES FROM ONE IDENTICAL CAMERA. Geometry transfers between livery states, paint does not (rule 11) — so green, Playa red and Nolita chalkboard in exact registration exists in no photograph pair on earth and can only come from re-inking one mesh. (2) THE CUT PATH. The die-cut voucher, the perforation and a papel-picado chisel cut are the same operation — a closed path — and Grease Pencil Line Art → SVG emits precisely that, so the keystone infrastructure item and the oldest owed deliverable are the same build. (3) THE VIEW OUT. A camera behind the counter looking through a 515.5 × 403.0 aperture is trivial in the model and impossible from the reference set, which has no frame taken from inside the galley. CEILING: the side ortho fits the 4.065 m body today; front and rear ortho CLIP, so the front-elevation page prints SHEET 3 OF 4 — NOT ISSUED until they are fixed. That is not a dodge, it is the sheet's own convention.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — THE REAL ONE FIRST, AND IT IS NOT SMALL. A booklet in the grammar of border paperwork, about a Mexican vehicle that ended up in New York, in 2026, is not neutral and cannot be made neutral by good typography. Played lightly it reads as a taco brand borrowing the visual language of crossings that are not stamped, not voluntary and not funny. There are exactly two honest exits and the owner picks: (a) the cover states that the subject is A VEHICLE and only a vehicle, and the book never uses a human word — no 'entry', no 'admitted', no 'alien'; or (b) it takes a position and gives money, the Altro Paradiso/ROAR move, in which case the position is printed on the last counterfoil. Sanding it into a cute passport is the one outcome that deserves to be attacked. SECOND: forgery. It must be structurally incapable of functioning as a real permit — no issuing authority, no seal, no form number, no country name in an official field. THIRD, and it will kill it quietly: a booklet that gets stamped is work the counter has to do at every service. If the staff stop stamping, the object is dead and nobody will report it. DECLARATIONS: this does NOT act on F92 — no terrace is built, playa_env.py is not run, the Playa page is the vehicle alone — but the booklet is measured against F92's own bar, 'the emotional bar that sits ABOVE clinical accuracy'. On SPEC 10.10: the artwork bar's object is the MODEL; this is a derivative graphic and a different object (rule 34), and it reproduces only the words actually READ, printing the unread ones as unread. The papel-picado question is the owner's, deferred by him, and is NOT re-put here — the craft link is to the cut path, not to a claim that the vehicle carries bunting.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Plate surround 330.0 × 168.2 mm, aspect 1.9616, aperture EMPTY, digits schematic (MEASURED / flagged in source). VW roundel Ø280.0 mm at 1017.0 mm above ground (MEASURED). Serving aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55, THREE, EQUAL (MEASURED). Body length 4065 mm, width 1750 mm (MEASURED, mesh). Menu-strip yellow (172,144,17) (MEASURED); red (196,49,36), hue 5.0°, sat 0.816 (MEASURED). Opened 6 February 2006; bought green in Mexico City for $3,000, missing three of its windows (founder, grade S). Read menu words: GOURMET · TACOS · & · TORTAS · FRESH · JUICES · CEVICHE · TOSTADAS · SHRIMP & FISH; Nolita board PUERCO ASADO · BARBACOA · PESCADO · POLLO COSTEÑO · MAIZ Y POBLANO. UNMEASURED AND PRINTED AS SUCH: the registration number; the off side (grade E); the absolute roof height; the New York address (251 per SPEC.md:344 vs 267 per the photograph at 9×); galley depth in Y. FORBIDDEN AND NOT USED: the pillar asymmetry (0.67σ).

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The cheapest bound object there is. 1,000 × 24pp saddle-stitched, two colours, uncoated: routine at any short-run printer. The only non-trivial line is the kiss-cut vinyl — either tipped-in vinyl sheets or a separate sticker sheet bound into the signature; both are standard, neither is exotic. Rubber stamps: ~$20 each from any stamp maker, and one per stamp legend. Realistic timeline: paper prototype in days, production 4–6 weeks. The design load is the twelve line elevations, which is one afternoon once the Line Art → SVG route exists — and building that route is the programme's keystone item anyway, so this concept pays for the infrastructure rather than waiting on it.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — ONE SPREAD, THIS WEEK, ON A DESKTOP PRINTER. Print the Playa counterfoil and its voucher at 1:1 on A4, fold, and cut the sticker out by hand with a scalpel — the die-cut does not have to be machine-made to be judged. Order one rubber stamp in the measured red reading NO CONSTA. Hand him the folded spread and the stamp and ask him to stamp it himself. He will know inside ten seconds whether the border grammar is charming or offensive, and that is the single ruling that gates the whole concept.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:frontier-inside-neck-1`

### CREAM ABOVE, RED BELOW  ·  screen 6.67/10

**REGISTER** garment (cut-and-sew) · three-dimensional · zero ink on the outside  
**ARTEFACT** A cut-and-sew chore jacket and a matching service apron. Two panels, cream above a horizontal seam, red below. INK ON THE OUTSIDE: ZERO — no wordmark, no roundel, no trademark exposure of any kind. Cotton twill dyed to the measured albedos: cream (206,208,200), red (196,49,36). Topstitch gauge 5.5 mm. Everything printed is on the inside: neck tape, cuff facing, pocket bag, care label, hem tape, and the interior of the box lid.

> The vehicle's two-tone belt line becomes a seam instead of a print — and the entire 76-revision archive is moved to the inside, where documents belong and only the wearer reads them.

The title is lifted verbatim from t1_mats.py: 'T1_paint renders cream ABOVE the break line and red BELOW, in ONE material.' That sentence is a cutting instruction. The garment is two panels joined where the bus changes colour, and every construction rule is taken in ABSOLUTE VEHICLE MILLIMETRES — never scaled, because scaling is where this project mixes rulers and gets caught.

The chest pocket opening is the WINDOW SILL. The colour-change seam sits exactly 100 mm below it — SPEC's own line, 'the break sits 100 mm below the sill' — and SPEC's prohibition on deriving one from the other is printed inside the pocket bag where only the wearer will ever find it. Topstitching runs at 5.5 mm, the vehicle's measured panel shut line. The pocket bags are cut at the serving aperture's own proportion, 515.5 × 403.0, corner radius 55: two patch, one interior, THREE, EQUAL. The pillars between them are not used and the label says why — 0.67σ, three equal pillars sit inside the measurement.

And the seam is NOT LEVEL. The belt is a line, not a constant: break_z(x) = Z_BELT0 − RAKE_DZDX·x. The bus sits nose-down and its two-tone break falls 17.75 mm per metre. So the seam is raked front to back across the body. Nobody will consciously see it. Everybody will feel that the jacket sits oddly, correctly, alive.

Inside the neck, instead of a logo: 1963 · CIUDAD DE MÉXICO · $3,000 · VERDE / 06.02.2006 PLAYA DEL CARMEN / NUEVA YORK — 251 or 267 ELIZABETH ST, UNRESOLVED / MOTOR: NINGUNO · TRANSMISIÓN: VENDIDA.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — ONE THING, AND IT IS THE BEST THING IN THE CONCEPT: the raked seam. A photograph cannot give you 17.75 mm/m — it gives you a foreshortened edge on a curved flank at an unknown camera height. The model holds break_z(x) as an explicit function and can report the break's height at every station, so the seam can be graded across the pattern to the real slope instead of being drawn level like every two-tone garment ever made. Second: the box-lid interior plate is a true orthographic 1:1 side elevation of the exact panel the jacket's seam was taken from — true orthographic projection is the model's native capability and is available today on the side camera. Everything ELSE here needs no model: 100 mm, 5.5 mm, 515.5 × 403.0 and the two albedos are all constants already in the record, and I would rather say so than fake a dependency. If the line pass slips, this concept still ships; only the box lid waits.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — IT CONTAINS A PERSON ONLY BY BEING WORN, AND I WILL NOT DRESS THAT UP AS ANSWERING FINDING 4 — the apron does, because the apron is the cook's, and the cook in the photographs wears a white cap, a white shirt and a Tacombi apron with his hands at the plancha. THE REAL RISKS. (1) COST AND MINIMUM. This is not a blank you print on. It is a pattern, a grade, a sample round, a two-colour cut-and-sew and a factory minimum — an order of magnitude above a printed tee, with a floor of hundreds of units before the unit cost is sane. If the volume is not there, this is a beautiful sample and nothing else, and he should be told that in the first sentence, not the last. (2) IT ADVERTISES NOTHING. A garment with no mark on the outside sells to people already in the room and to nobody else. That is the concept, and it is also a commercial defect. Do not sell it as 'quiet luxury' — say plainly that it trades reach for the thing the critic said the programme lacked, which is an object a person would call beautiful rather than rigorous. (3) THE SILHOUETTE. A horizontal red/cream split at chest height is close to a genuinely unflattering line on many bodies, and the raked seam makes it asymmetric. It must be GRADED — seam height moving with size — not held at a constant drop from the hem. On a bad grade this looks like a rugby shirt. (4) UNMEASURED: whether the cream (206,208,200) is dyeable to that value in cotton at all. It is a shader albedo, not a dye recipe, and the round-trip is untested. DECLARED: no artwork is reproduced anywhere on this garment, so SPEC 10.10 is not engaged; the roundel is absent by construction and that is stated on the care label rather than left silent (F191).

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Two-tone order: cream ABOVE, red BELOW — verbatim from t1_mats.py (source). Belt line 1207 mm above ground at x +962; window sill 1307 mm; the break sits 100 mm below the sill (SPEC.md:211, MEASURED — and SPEC forbids deriving one from the other). Rake 17.75 mm/m, break_z(x) = Z_BELT0 − RAKE_DZDX·x (MEASURED, re-based at 4.5σ). Panel shut line GAPW = 5.5 mm (MEASURED). Serving aperture 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55, ×3 EQUAL (MEASURED). Cream (206,208,200), G > R — rev 3 shipped R > G and it was wrong. Red (196,49,36), hue 5.0°, sat 0.816. Body 4065 mm long, 1750 mm wide. UNMEASURED AND SAID SO: the absolute roof height (OPEN); whether either albedo is achievable as a cotton dye; the off side (grade E). FORBIDDEN AND NOT USED: the pillar asymmetry — the care label prints 'THREE EQUAL PILLARS SIT INSIDE THE MEASUREMENT' as the reason.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The expensive one, and it should be priced honestly at the top of the page. Cut-and-sew two-panel twill: sampling in the low hundreds of dollars per prototype, production minimums typically in the hundreds of units per colourway (UNVERIFIED — get three quotes before quoting him anything). The apron is dramatically cheaper than the jacket, simpler to grade, has no sizing problem and is the object the room actually needs — so the apron leads and the jacket follows if the apron sells. Trims — woven neck label, care/condition label, printed hem tape — are cheap, fast and can be sampled in days independently of the garment. That asymmetry is the whole feasibility strategy: buy the cheap half first.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — DO NOT MAKE A GARMENT. Make a CARD OF TRIMS. One board, A5, carrying: the woven inside-neck label with the four-line provenance block; the care label written as a CONDITION REPORT in the project's own grade vocabulary (MEASURED / DERIVED / DECLARED / INFERRED / UNMEASURED, one line per component); 200 mm of hem tape; two dyed fabric swatches at (206,208,200) and (196,49,36); and a 100 mm scrap seamed and topstitched at 5.5 mm so he can put his thumb on it. Trim suppliers run label samples in days for tens of dollars. That is the entire concept in his hand this week at about one per cent of the cost — and the inside-neck block is exactly the part he can rule on.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:frontier-inside-neck-2`

### LA CINTA 8130  ·  screen 4.5/10

**REGISTER** packaging · garment-component · three-dimensional (woven textile)  
**ARTEFACT** One printed cotton/satin tape, 15 mm wide, one colour (black) on natural ground, 3 mm type. ONE tooling, SIX products: inside-neck tape, hem tape, apron ties, box ribbon, bag handle, and a free 200 mm bookmark left in the menus. Pattern repeat: 8130 mm — exactly two body lengths. The first 4065 mm carries the show side's figures in true positional order, nose to tail. The second 4065 mm is BLANK.

> A tape whose repeat is two bus-lengths: one length of type for the side that was photographed, one length of nothing for the side that never was — so half of every metre of merchandise this brand sells is empty, on purpose, with the reason printed at the boundary.

Half the tape is blank because half the vehicle has never been photographed. SPEC grades the off side E — never photographed — an entire flank of a vehicle measured for 76 revisions. So the repeat is 8130 mm: 4065 mm of the near side's figures at their true stations, then 4065 mm of nothing, with one line of 3 mm type at the boundary — LADO OPUESTO · GRADO E · NUNCA FOTOGRAFIADO.

The consequence is the product. Cut a 55 cm neck tape, a 90 cm apron tie, a 120 cm box ribbon or a 40 cm bag handle at random off the roll and every single one is different. Some are dense with survey. Some are entirely empty. Nobody can order the good one and nobody can be sold the good one. That is not a gimmick bolted on; it is the record's own shape, reproduced at 1:1 in a component that costs almost nothing.

And the frontier is already in the record, not as a metaphor. I ran it: grep -ic nolita REF_MEASUREMENTS.md returns 0 against 32 in SPEC.md. One city is measured and the other is not. The second colourway is that — NUEVA YORK in 3 mm type, followed by 4065 mm of nothing — and it needs no invention whatsoever, only the honesty to print a zero.

The cheapest use is the best one: a 200 mm cut, free, in the menus, take one. A thing families keep, at a unit cost of pennies, in the room, this season.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — THE ORDER, WHICH IS THE ONLY THING HERE THAT IS NOT A LIST. The figures must appear at their TRUE STATIONS along the flank — X_NOSE +2108, the arch, the three aperture centres, X_TAIL −1873 — so a length of tape laid against the actual bus registers with it, and a length pinned inside a neck is a scale drawing rather than a slogan. A hand-typed sequence would be a list; the mesh's station data makes it a survey. That is a real and narrow dependency and it is the whole of it. The model contributes NOTHING ELSE here — no line pass, no render, no camera. I would rather say that than manufacture a reason, and it is also why this is the fastest of the three: the station data exists today, unblocked by the front/rear ortho clip and untouched by F191 and F193.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — NO FOOD AND NO PEOPLE — stated flatly rather than claimed away by pointing at the apron ties. This is type on tape and it is the coldest of the three; it earns its place as the connective tissue between the other two, not as an answer to finding 4. THE FAILURE MODES. (1) THE BLANK HALF IS THE IDEA AND IT IS THE FIRST THING EVERY MERCHANDISER WILL ASK YOU TO DELETE. The first stranger who sees it will say the printing failed — and unless the reason is printed ON the tape at the boundary, in type they can read, THEY ARE RIGHT AND THE OBJECT IS BROKEN. That one line of 3 mm type is load-bearing; if it does not resolve, the concept does not work. (2) 3 mm TYPE ON 15 mm TAPE IS UNMEASURED. It must be transfer-printed, not jacquard-woven — woven will not hold it — and even printed it is at the edge of legibility. I have not tested it with any supplier and I am not going to pretend otherwise: get a sample before committing, and if it fails, the type gets bigger and the repeat gets longer, not the other way round. (3) IT IS A COMPONENT, NOT A PRODUCT. On its own it is nothing. Six uses out of six depend on something else existing to carry it — a garment, a box, a menu. That is a genuine structural weakness and the bookmark only partly covers it. (4) NO MARK MEANS NO PROTECTION: anyone can copy this in an afternoon, and it says nothing to a person who has never been in the room.

**MEASURED FIGURES USED** — Repeat 8130 mm = 2 × body length 4065 mm (MEASURED, mesh). Off side graded E, never photographed (SPEC §1) — the reason for the blank half. Station data for the ordering: X_NOSE +2108 mm, X_TAIL −1873 mm, wheelbase 2400 mm, track 1369/1359 mm, tyre Ø664.9 mm, rim flange Ø439.6 mm (SIXTEEN inch, not fifteen), hubcap Ø274.0 with badge Ø86.9, three apertures 515.5 × 403.0 r 55, counter slab 107.0 thick and 321.0 deep, brass nosing 18.6 % of the slab edge, rear overhang 773 ± 22 mm, rake 17.75 mm/m, shut line 5.5 mm, 118 lamps at 28.52 mm realised pitch (I recomputed this from BULB_X0/BULB_X1/BULB_PITCH and watched it print — the circulating 115 is an inference about a 3.50 m rail, not this model). The frontier figure, watched print this session: grep -ic nolita → REF_MEASUREMENTS.md 0, SPEC.md 32. FORBIDDEN AND NOT USED: the pillar asymmetry.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Custom printed tape typically carries a minimum in the thousands of metres (UNVERIFIED — three quotes needed). But an 8130 mm repeat over 1,000 m is only about 123 repeats, so even a modest order gives full coverage of the design, and tape is one of the cheapest custom textile components that exists. The economics are unusually good in one specific way: one tooling amortised across six products means the marginal cost of the fifth and sixth uses is zero. The bookmark is effectively free at the margin and can go into the room the week the roll arrives.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — DO NOT ORDER TAPE. Print the first 1000 mm of the repeat at 1:1 on four sheets of A4, tape them end to end, cut the strip down to 15 mm with a scalpel and a steel rule, and tie it round a takeaway box. One hour, no cost. Then print a second strip that is entirely blank and tie that round a second box, and put both in front of him together. He will rule in about four seconds on the only question that matters — whether the empty one is the best thing in the programme or a printing error — and that ruling is worth more than any quote.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — False  ·  **id** `dream:frontier-inside-neck-3`

## BENCH PROMOTION — F18, THE DIE-CUT STICKER

### LAS CINCO VIÑETAS  ·  screen 7.53/10

**REGISTER** polychrome · food · painted / hand-drawn · beautiful-not-rigorous — ADULT LINE (F331), with the prawn and the torta cross-issued to the children's sheet  
**ARTEFACT** Seven die-cut stickers on one 210 mm strip, each cut to its own painted silhouette with a 2 mm keyline of the strip yellow left round it, so each reads as a chip cut off the actual menu board. Each vignette printed 90 mm tall. Five inks, all measured off the vehicle: STRIP yellow (172,144,17) flooded as the ground; PINK (206,118,124) for meat and shrimp; GREEN (96,128,54) for garnish; CREAMV (232,224,198) for plate and glass; line in the MEASURED (91,59,7). Matte uncoated-look vinyl.

> The five food paintings that are already on this bus, printed legibly for the first time — and captioned with exactly how much of each stroke is ours.

There are five food paintings on the roof-lid menu strips and nobody in seventy-seven revisions has ever drawn them out. In v-fractions of the board: two bolillos with green sprigs at 0.047–0.096; a torta, pink meat and green garnish, at 0.260–0.337; a tall pale glass with an orange at 0.453–0.511; a tostada with magenta filling at 0.677–0.703; a pink prawn at 0.814–0.868. They are the only food this brand has ever painted on itself, in a palette already measured, and they are the answer to "a taqueria programme with no food" that does not require inventing a taco.

Sixth chip: the index — the whole strip reduced, with each vignette's v-band marked. Seventh, and the one people will want: the three small lines at 0.935–0.966 that the record could not read. They resolve to about "?ANO M?" / "??O?A?" / "Torta?" and are carried as UNREAD. Printed as an illegible sticker captioned NOT LEGIBLE. A chip that admits it could not read something, small enough to collect.

One decision, from the record's own reasoning: the line prints in the MEASURED (91,59,7), not the model's (72,46,6). `lid_gen.py` says the measured median was darkened "because the reading is blur-limited" — and a printed sticker has no blur limit, so the compensation should come off. Both swatches go on the backing.

The caption is on the FACE, not hidden on the back: on the vehicle these are between 32 and 94 mm tall; in the only frame that shows them they are a few dozen pixels; at 90 mm, most of every stroke is ours.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — NOTHING — and this is the concept where saying so is the point. The five vignettes come from a photograph and from `lid_gen.py`'s measured positions and palette; no mesh, no line pass, no render is involved and faking a dependency would be the defect. The model contributes exactly one thing, and it is narrow: the mural board's true size — 2034 mm long by a width of 1223.7 mm that is DERIVED from a measured board aspect of 1.7130 rather than typed, take it as ±30 mm — so the index chip's v-bands are real and the five chips can be laid back onto the 1:25 board of concept 1 in their correct places. ⚠ ONE ARITHMETIC AMBIGUITY, UNRESOLVED AND FLAGGED RATHER THAN GUESSED: whether the source's v-fractions are of 1223.7 mm or of 2034 mm. At the first the torta is 94 mm tall on the vehicle; at the second, 157 mm. It is one line to settle against the file. Settle it before printing.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — FOUR, and the first is the one that should be argued out loud rather than managed. (1) SOMEBODY PAINTED THESE AND WE DO NOT KNOW WHO. A rotulista painted these five, probably in Playa, probably around 2006, and we would enlarge and sell their work. The only honest answer is Alÿs's: commission a working rotulista to repaint the five at size in enamel, credit them by name on the sticker, and print PAINTER OF THE ORIGINAL: UNKNOWN if we cannot find the first hand. If we will not pay for that, do not make this set. (2) MOST OF IT IS INVENTED AND THE CAPTION IS THE ONLY DEFENCE. At 90 mm we are interpolating perhaps 95 % of every stroke from a specimen of a few dozen pixels. (3) F94 CUTS BOTH WAYS. "ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ALL ARTWORK" is his hard bar; its object is the MODEL, and a derivative graphic is a different object under rule 34 — but this is also the closest to replication the project has ever come, and if he reads it as replication and it is wrong, that is his tenth report. (4) WE MAY BE SELLING FOOD HE NO LONGER SERVES. CEVICHE and TOSTADAS are read off the Playa lid strips in an unknown year; the Nolita board and the priced green-bus board are both different. Check the current menu — only he can.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Five spot inks on one strip is a single pass and the most economical polychrome item in the programme; no through-cutting, no registration to a die-line, and the 2 mm yellow keyline is a tolerance, not a decoration — it absorbs the cut wander that would otherwise show as a white edge. Run cost UNQUOTED. The genuine cost is the rotulista commission, and it should be paid. Feasible immediately: no camera, no build, no render, no line pass. It could be drawn while the model is doing something else.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — ONE CHIP: THE PRAWN. Enlarge it from the frame at 22×, redraw it at 90 mm in the five measured inks, print it A4, and pin it beside a crop of the photograph reproduced at the SAME 90 mm. He sees the source and the drawing side by side at one size and can judge in three seconds how much of it we made up. If he flinches, the whole set is wrong and it cost one sheet of paper to find out.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:f18-sticker-3`

### LA COMBI CALADA  ·  screen 7.37/10

**REGISTER** child · packaging (sheet + printed liner) · polychrome — CHILDREN'S LINE (F331 requires every item to say which line; this is the children's)  
**ARTEFACT** A5 sheet, 148 × 210 mm. White matte monomeric vinyl on a printed liner. THREE stickers: one hero at 1:25 (body 162.6 mm long) and two smaller states at 1:50 (81.3 mm). On the hero, the three serving apertures are cut CLEAN THROUGH vinyl AND liner — 20.62 × 16.12 mm each, corner radius 2.20 mm — so the sheet has three real holes in it. Below 1:30 the holes are printed, not cut; that threshold is a manufacturing limit, not a taste decision. Face: five spot inks (RED 196,49,36 · CREAM 206,208,200 · GOLD 239,194,69 · mural GROUND 55,26,19 · STRIP yellow 172,144,17) plus a line ink at the MEASURED (91,59,7). Liner printed 1/1.

> The bus die-cut tight with its three serving hatches cut clean through the vinyl, because three missing windows are why the business has three windows.

He bought it green, for $3,000, and it was missing three of its windows. That is why the show side became three serving hatches. So the sticker's three holes are not a graphic device — they are the origin of the company, and a child can put a finger through them.

The sheet tells that in three cuts: VERDE (roof intact, before), EL TALLER (lid up, primer white, no artwork, mid-cut), PLAYA (full livery, counter, bulbs). The die-line changes exactly once — at the moment he cut the roof open. The die-line is the biography.

AUDIT_rev43 §5 already art-directed this and nobody has read it since: viewpoint 18° front three-quarter from the serving side, eye height 1.55 m, "the face and the flank are provably exclusive; choose the flank"; three line weights on a 0.15 mm floor; four contact lines; do not outline the paisley; delete the 48 of 66 gold components that each land under 0.40 mm; bridge the underbody with the cast shadow; and — the best line in the file — the sun "must NOT be a rayed disc. The vehicle already carries ELEVEN radial bursts."

The papel picado is the CUT, not drawn bunting: the sheet's outer edge is chiselled in the Huixcolotla vocabulary. That does not pre-empt his deferred ruling; the drawn-bunting version stays available.

DECLARED: the nose roundel is absent because the rev-43 viewpoint excludes the face, not because we dodged it. F191 stands. The VW appears only on the hubcap, at 86.9 mm — where F333 found it traces cleanly in line. Redrawing his artwork at 1:25 is a DESIGN, a different object from the MODEL under rule 34; SPEC §10.10's replication bar is on the model.

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Everything structural. `line_pass.py` (F332, built rev 77) gives editable vector strokes — which is exactly what a die-cut plotter wants — and 0 non-manifold edges on the body means the silhouette closes, so the cut path is a real boundary rather than a traced guess. `sheet.py` writes the SVG master. Every figure on the liner is machine-read from STATE.md at draw time, as `sheet3_notissued.py` already does, so when the model moves the sticker moves. ⚠ HALF THE OWNER'S STYLE RULING IS NOT BUILDABLE TODAY: there is no normal pass and no AO pass, so "shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset" is unbuilt (F332). The flat shading is authored by hand until it exists. Say that, do not round it up.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — THREE, none of them cosmetic. (1) THE PILLARS MAY TEAR. Three cut apertures leave the pillars as the narrowest bridge on the cut path, and rev-43's own rule bars any feature under 0.159 m (vehicle scale) from touching the cut line. At 1:25 they are a few millimetres of vinyl with a 2.20 mm inside corner beside them; weeding may rip them and the sticker becomes a bus with a ragged wound. This can kill the concept and the cut test is the first thing to run. (2) VERDE'S GREEN IS A SENTENCE, NOT A MEASUREMENT. No photograph in the set is graded as proving THIS bus green — `ref_workshop.jpg` is carried as "the GREEN vehicle, geometry only". We would print an unmeasured claim about his own vehicle on a child's sticker. It must be captioned UNMEASURED. (3) WE ARE EXECUTING AN ART DIRECTION WE CAN ONLY READ THREE-QUARTERS OF: AUDIT_rev43 §5 survives with its rows hard-cut at 120 characters, three findings ending mid-sentence, and no fuller version in 793 commits. And the "earlier cartoon version" whose wheels he praised is not in the repository at all — if he holds it, we need it.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — Cheap, and the constraint is his own chalkboard: the sheet must land under the cheapest thing on the menu — $11 pesos, about US$0.92 at the board's own "1 USD = $12". ⚠ Both figures are read off the GREEN bus's board, a different vehicle in an unknown year; treat as an order of magnitude, not a price. Run cost UNQUOTED — get three quotes; the variables are the through-die (holes) versus kiss-cut only, and five spots versus process. Cut test needs a desktop vinyl plotter and an afternoon. The 18° perspective camera does not exist in the build (front/rear ortho also CLIP, 2.4406 m against a 3.132 m bbox top) — one cheap build item, not a blocker.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — A CUT TEST, IN HIS HANDS THIS WEEK, AND IT IS NOT THE STICKER. Draw the flank through the EXISTING `side` orthographic camera (it fits: 5.90 → 4.0563 m against a 4.065 m body) via the existing `line_pass.py`, one ink, at 1:25 = 162.6 mm long. Cut it on a desktop plotter with the three apertures cut clean through. Hand him a die-cut bus with three real holes and ask him to try to tear the pillars. That single object settles the one thing that can kill the concept, proves the whole chain end to end, and he can put it on a window.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:f18-sticker-1`

### AQUÍ TIENE  ·  screen 7.07/10

**REGISTER** food-and-hand-off · people · environmental · hand-drawn — CHILDREN'S LINE (F331)  
**ARTEFACT** TWO SIZES OF ONE IMAGE. (a) Take-home: clear polyester sticker, the serving aperture at 1:5 — 103.1 × 80.6 mm, corner radius 11.0 mm — printed white-plus-four, with the aperture VOID LEFT CLEAR so the room lights it. (b) In-room: the same artwork at 1:1 — 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55 — as a window decal on the restaurant's glass, sill set at the true aperture sill height, 1307 mm above the floor. One drawing, two scales, five times apart.

> A hand comes through a hole that is the exact size of the real hole, holding a plate — the one transaction this brand actually sells, and the one thing 77 revisions of measurement never built.

For seventy-seven revisions this project has built a serving hatch, a griddle, six tool hooks, a worktop and a warm fluorescent tube, and has never once built anybody to stand behind them. I checked: across 229 mesh objects there is no person, no hand, no proxy, no scale figure. The plancha is a bare 0.560 × 0.780 slab and nothing has ever been on it.

That absence is the concept. The bus is drawn from the model. The hand is not, because it cannot be — and the honest way to fill it is to commission the drawing from a Mexican illustrator or a rotulista and credit them, which is Francis Alÿs's answer to the appropriation question and is also simply better work.

The optics are measured, not invented. The record says the roof opening does all the lighting, and that "a serving hatch shows you its strip light." So the sticker is printed on clear film with the aperture void left clear: stuck on glass, daylight comes through the hatch and lights the galley from behind, exactly as the real one works. The sticker is lit by the room it is in.

The 1:1 decal at 1307 mm is where it stops being merchandise. A small child stands at it and is at the height they are at the real bus. An adult meets it at chest height, which is comic, and that is the point: the vehicle was never built for us.

F92 is named, not acted on: this is a drawing, not the Playa hero render, and its bar is his — "the emotional bar that sits ABOVE clinical accuracy."

**WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES** — Narrow and real. The model gives the aperture's exact rounded rectangle (515.5 × 403.0, r 55, MEASURED, and the photograph corroborates the radius independently at ≈57 mm), the sill at 1307 mm above ground, and the galley behind it drawn through `line_pass.py`: the plancha top at 1.455 m, the worktop at the measured 1.5010 m, six hooks at their measured x stations, the strip light 20 mm under the head rail, the counter's brass nosing at 18.6 % of the slab's outer edge, and the white bobble fringe round the cut edge. WHAT IT DOES NOT GIVE, STATED: the hand, the plate, the food, and the galley's DEPTH — `galley_dressing()`'s own docstring says "DEPTH (Y) IS NOT MEASURABLE", so Y is chosen everywhere. Any view INTO the bay is a construction and the caption says so.

**THE RISK IT CARRIES** — FOUR, and the first two are the kind that stop a job. (1) WE WOULD BE DRAWING A REAL EMPLOYEE'S HANDS. What makes the image specific rather than generic is the Tacombi-script apron cuff at the wrist — and every cook in every reference frame is an actual person who works for him. Putting their labour on merchandise without asking, naming and paying them is not a grey area. If we will not do that, draw a generic hand and accept that it is weaker, or do not make it. (2) THE OPTICAL TRICK DIES ON AN OPAQUE SURFACE. Clear vinyl on a fridge is a grey smear. Children stick things on fridges. That halves the object's usable life and there is no fix — it is a window sticker or it is nothing. (3) THE 1:1 DECAL INVITES CHILDREN TO PRESS THEIR FACES ON THE GLASS, which is the most-cleaned, most-breakable surface in the room; operations may veto it outright. (4) THE FOOD ON THE PLATE IS INVENTED. Our only food evidence is five painted vignettes a few dozen pixels across and a readable menu. The taco is drawn, not measured, and must be captioned INVENTED.

**COST / FEASIBILITY** — The cheapest concept of the three. The take-home is a standard clear-vinyl kiss-cut sticker, five colours including white — no die-line complexity, no through-cut, no tearing risk. The 1:1 decal is one panel of printed window film, an off-the-shelf item. Both run costs UNQUOTED. The real cost is the illustration commission, which should be a named person paid properly and is the item worth spending on. One figure only he can supply: whether 1307 mm is the right sill height for the children actually in his room. That is UNMEASURED and we cannot measure it from here.

**FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP** — Print the 1:1 aperture — 515.5 × 403.0 mm, r 55 — on plain paper, tape it to the glass with its sill at 1307 mm, and photograph a child standing at it. That is a sheet of paper and ten minutes, it produces the single most persuasive image this programme has ever had, and it answers the one figure we cannot get: whether that height works for his customers.

**CONTAINS FOOD OR PEOPLE** — True  ·  **id** `dream:f18-sticker-2`
